The Ten Wheels of Kṣitigarbha, a Great Vehicle Sūtra from the Great Collection (Daśacakrakṣitigarbha-nāma-mahāyānasūtra)

The Ten Wheels of Kṣitigarbha, known in Sanskrit as the Daśacakrakṣitigarbha Sūtra and in Tibetan as Sai Nyingpö Khorlo Chupa, is a Great Vehicle sūtra presented as part of the Great Collection, the vast Mahāyāna compilation of discourses. Set on Mount Khaladeya, where the Buddha dwells with great assemblies of monks and bodhisattvas, it tells how the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha arrives amid wondrous signs, and the Buddha then teaches the ten wheels, the powers and qualities by which buddhas and bodhisattvas protect beings and uphold the Dharma. Across its eight chapters it treats moral conduct, the duty to respect and protect monastics even when they are flawed, the dangers of slandering the teaching, the true marks of a field of merit, and practices such as recollection of the Buddha and meditation on emptiness, closing with the Buddha entrusting the sūtra to the bodhisattva Ākāśagarbha. It is Toh 239 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the General Sūtra section, and its colophon records that it was translated into Tibetan from Chinese by a Chinese preceptor together with the Tibetan translator Nampar Mitok.

In the language of India: daśacakrakṣitigarbhanāmamahāyānasūtra. In the language of Tibet: From the Great Collection, the Great Vehicle Sūtra called The Ten Wheels of Kṣitigarbha.

The first fascicle.

Homage to all buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One was dwelling on the mountain called Khaladeya, the abode frequented by many sages and munis, together with an immeasurable, countless great Saṅgha of monks, all of them a great Saṅgha of śrāvakas alone. Together with him also were immeasurable, countless bodhisattva great beings, all of them a great Saṅgha of bodhisattvas alone.

Just after the teaching called Candragarbha had been given, great clouds of fragrance gathered from the southern direction, and from them a great rain of fragrance poured down. Great clouds of flowers gathered, and from them a great rain of flowers poured down. Great clouds of divine jewel ornaments gathered, and from them a great rain of divine jewel ornaments poured down. Great clouds of divine garments gathered, and from them a great rain of divine garments poured down. Such great clouds and rains covered that mountain, the abode frequented by many sages and munis.

From those fragrances, flowers, jewel ornaments and garments there resounded hundreds of thousands of the various voices of the great Dharma, namely: the sound of going for refuge to the Jewels; the sound of upholding the bases of training; the sound of being gentle and patient; the sound of undertaking diligence; the sound of the four māras subdued; the sound of being intent upon and entering wisdom; the sound of great renown spreading widely through the three worlds; the sound of applying oneself to the special qualities of mindfulness, meditative absorption and dhāraṇī; the sound of emptiness, signlessness and wishlessness; the sound of utter weariness with desire; the sound, "Form is like a mass of foam"; the sound, "Feeling is like a water bubble"; the sound, "Perception is like a mirage"; the sound, "Formations are like a plantain tree"; the sound, "Consciousness is like a magical illusion"; the sound of impermanence; the sound of suffering; the sound of selflessness; the sound of emptiness; the sound of possessing conscience and a sense of shame; the sound of complete renunciation; the sound of the applications of mindfulness; the sound of loving-kindness, compassion, joy and equanimity; the sound of the full comprehension of all phenomena; the sound of birth in the higher realms and of passing beyond sorrow; the sound of entering the three vehicles; the sound of turning the great wheel of the Dharma; the sound of letting fall the great rain of the Dharma; the sound of bringing sentient beings to full maturity; the sound of complete liberation from the three lower realms; the sound of applying oneself to perfecting the six perfections; the sound of skill in means; the sound of entering the ten grounds; the sound of playing with the higher knowledges; the sound of delighting in the pure, unsurpassed Great Vehicle; the sound of not turning back from the grounds; the sound of acceptance that phenomena are unborn; the sound of consecration upon the crown of the head; and the sound of entering the great ocean of all the buddhas. All these resounded.

Then all those many circles of the assembly saw such clouds and rains of every kind, and heard the resounding voices of the Dharma. Each of them, according to the inclination of his own mind, saw his own body fully adorned with various fragrances, flowers, jewel ornaments and fine divine garments. They also saw that each of them held a precious wish-fulfilling jewel in the palms of both hands, and that from each of those precious wish-fulfilling jewels various precious things fell down like rain.

Moreover, rays of light of many kinds streamed forth from those precious jewels, and by the power of those rays each of those beings also saw buddha fields as numerous as the grains of sand in the river Ganges, throughout the ten directions. By the power of those rays they also saw, in those buddha fields, each of those tathāgatas surrounded with reverence by immeasurable great assemblies.

Furthermore, by the power of that light, all sentient beings in those buddha fields who were tormented by illness were freed from the suffering of illness the moment those rays touched them. Those facing execution, and those held in prison and bound, were completely freed from these the moment those rays touched them. Those whose body, speech and mind behaved without ease, weighed down by grave negative tendencies, became utterly light and completely purified by the power of that light. Those who were hungry and thirsty became sated and satisfied. Those afflicted by execution and by punishments of every kind were freed from their suffering the moment those rays touched them. Those who were lacking and destitute of clothing, jewel ornaments, wealth and possessions came to have them in abundance, just as they wished. Sentient beings who delighted in and were inclined toward killing, and so on up to those who delighted in and were inclined toward wrong view, came to delight in and be inclined toward completely abandoning killing, and so on up to completely abandoning wrong view, the moment that light touched them. Sentient beings who suffered from failing to find what they desired in its many forms found it just as they had hoped, the moment that light touched them.

Moreover, by the power of that light, all the sentient beings in all the buddha fields who were tormented by suffering were seen to have it all pacified and to be filled with great happiness. Furthermore, because all those buddha fields were illuminated by the light, clouds, dust, mist, wind, hail, inauspicious sounds, foul smells, bad tastes such as bitterness and pungency, unpleasant textures, terrors and the many kinds of harm all ceased to be.

Moreover, those fields became entirely free of evil deeds, wrong speech, wrong intentions and wrong inclinations. They were neither too cold nor too hot, utterly peaceful and pleasant, the ground as level as the palm of the hand, and they were filled with every excellent requisite of happiness.

At that time the earth element in the bodies of each of the many members of the assembly became exceedingly heavy, so heavy that they could hardly bear it. Seeing such omens appear, they were all alarmed, and the thought arose in them, "What is the cause, what is the condition, for such omens as these?"

Then, within that circle of the assembly, Śakra, lord of the gods, called Born Stainless, who was seated not far from the Blessed One, rose from his seat, bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet, joined his palms toward the Blessed One, and addressed him in these verses:

You who speak the truth and see the truth,
O Sage, firmly established in the truth,
To all this great assembly gathered here
Please teach the ultimate Dharma of truth.

Many ordinary beings
Are rid of suffering and the cause of suffering.
For what reason have clouds and rains
Appeared here in this assembly?

The assembly, overjoyed,
Has conceived a mind of deep faith;
All are intent upon the Great Vehicle;
Right view has arisen and they have crossed beyond doubt.

The bodies of the many gods and humans in this assembly
Have grown so heavy in their earth element
That they cannot bear their own weight.
Through what condition has this omen arisen?

From the jewels in the palms of both hands
Rains of precious things fall, and light streams forth,
Illuminating the ten directions and clearing away evil;
Suffering is stilled and happiness is gained.
Guide, please tell us: from what condition?

Upon every circle of the assembly
Various incense and garlands
Appear as adornments on each one's body.
All the gods and humans have grown doubtful:
What is the cause, and what the condition?
Who has come here, and from where,
To display this power of miraculous ability?
Is it a buddha or a bodhisattva?
Or is it Brahmā, or a māra?
Is it Śakra? Who is it who has come?
Great Guide, please explain it.

Then the Blessed One said to Śakra Born Stainless, "You should understand it in this way. There is a bodhisattva great being called Kṣitigarbha who, throughout immeasurable, countless great eons in the past, during the times of the five degenerations, has brought sentient beings to maturity in world realms where no buddha dwells. Now, together with a retinue of eighty thousand koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, he is coming here to this world realm with his retinue in the guise of śramaṇas, in order to pay homage to me, to serve and venerate me, and moreover to look upon the circles of the assembly and give rise to rejoicing. Through the power of his higher knowledge he has displayed these miracles.

"That bodhisattva great being called Kṣitigarbha is adorned with immeasurable, countless, inconceivable special qualities, so that no śrāvaka, no pratyekabuddha, nor anyone in the world is able to fathom him. That bodhisattva is a treasury of all supreme qualities. He is a mine of the jewels of liberation. He purifies the eyes of bodhisattvas. He is a caravan leader for those who are journeying to nirvāṇa. Like a precious wish-fulfilling jewel, he rains down every kind of precious thing, and so satisfies all hopes and desires. He is like an island of jewels for many merchants. Because he makes the roots of virtue grow and increase, he is like a fertile field. Because he places beings in the happiness of liberation, he is like a vessel. Because he is a source of excellent jewels, he is like an auspicious vase of good qualities. Because he gives light to those devoted to virtue, he is like the bright sun. Because he gives light to those who have lost their way, he is like a lamp. Because he dispels the torment of the afflictions, he is cooling like the moon. For the lame he is like a conveyance. For those setting out on a long road he is like a store of provisions. For those lost in the wilderness he is like a guide. For the mad and the deranged he is like an excellent medicine. For those tormented by illness he is like meeting a skilled physician. For the old and the frail he is like a support and a staff. For the weary and exhausted he is like a bed and a seat. For crossing the four floods he is like a bridge. For going to the far shore he is like a boat and a ship.

"He brings to ripeness the special fruits of the three roots of virtue. He brings forth the bases of the three kinds of virtue and lets them pour down continuously. His unbroken stream of giving turns like a wheel. In the firmness with which he guards moral discipline he is like Sumeru, king of mountains. His diligence, hard to destroy, is like a precious vajra. His unshakable patience is like the great earth. His profound meditative concentration is like a hidden treasure. His beauty of meditative equipoise is like an excellent garland of flowers. His wisdom, deep and vast, is like the great ocean. His freedom from attachment to anything at all is like space. His nearness to the cause of the supreme fruit is like a flower in full bloom. In taming the non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) he is like a king of lions. In crushing the divine māra he is like a great nāga or elephant. In cutting down all the enemies that are the afflictions he is like a divine sword. In his weariness with much busy activity he is like a follower of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. In washing away the stains of the afflictions he is like clear water. In clearing away the filth of impurity he is like a strong wind. In cutting off the opportunities of the thorough afflictions he is like a sharp weapon. In protecting from every fear and anxiety he is like a kinsman or a friend. In defending against hostile enemies he is like a moat or a fortress. Because he frees beings from all dangers he is like a father and mother. In sheltering the weak and feeble he is like a dense forest. For those travelling a long road in summer he is like a great tree of cool shade. For those tormented by heat and thirst he is like cool water. For the hungry he is like sweet fruit. For the naked he provides clothing to wear. For those oppressed by heat he becomes a dense cloud. For the poor and destitute he becomes a precious wish-fulfilling jewel. For the fearful and frightened he becomes a refuge and a shelter. For the crops he becomes the rain.

"For turbid waters he becomes a water-clearing jewel. Because he keeps the roots of virtue of many sentient beings from being destroyed, he shows them the excellent field of practice and brings delight and satisfaction to the assembly. He urges sentient beings toward a higher conscience and sense of shame and leads them into it. He adorns those who have gathered the accumulations of merit and wisdom with the ornaments of these. In thoroughly dispelling the afflictions he is like an emetic and a purgative medicine. In gathering in the agitated mind he is like the field of practice of meditative equipoise. His unobstructed eloquence is like a turning water-wheel. His gathering of the mind upon its object is like gazing upon a beautiful form. His steadfast patience is like Sumeru, king of mountains. His form, deep and vast, is like the great ocean. His unobstructed bases of miraculous power are like space. His thorough removal of all the habitual tendencies of the obscurations is like the warmth of the sun melting ice. Always delighting in the meditative concentrations, the formless absorptions and the true path, he is like a supreme island of the jewels of omniscient wisdom, and so, even without deliberately setting out to do so, he turns the great wheel of the Dharma.

"Noble son, such is that bodhisattva great being Kṣitigarbha, endowed with such immeasurable, countless, inconceivable special qualities. Because he and his retinue are drawing near to this place, these miracles and omens were displayed beforehand through that light."

As soon as the Blessed One had spoken of the many qualities of the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha, the bodhisattva great being Kṣitigarbha, together with a retinue of eighty hundred thousand koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, appearing through the power of his miraculous ability in the guise of śramaṇas, arrived from the southern direction into the presence of the Blessed One. With the great assembly he touched his head to the Blessed One's feet in homage, circumambulated him three times, and, standing before the Tathāgata with palms joined, praised him in these verses:

Guide, supreme among two-footed beings,
With a mind of loving-kindness you protect all.
Like the great earth you are supremely patient,
And you have cleared away all thoughts of hatred and wrath.

Endowed with the supreme major and minor marks,
You adorn the fields of all the buddhas.
With true loving-kindness and compassion
You fill all the fields of the Victors.

Having cut through every net of craving,
You dwell in happiness, true and just as it is.
Forsaking the utterly pure fields,
You free sentient beings in times of degeneration.

Having made your vow in this degenerate field,
You bring to maturity beings of the lower realms.
With true and steadfast effort
You practised austerities for a very long time.

Hearing of the austerities you practised so long,
Even a mind of awe arises.
You cultivated generosity, moral discipline, patience,
Diligence, meditative concentration and wisdom.

To buddhas and to bodhisattvas
And to measureless śrāvakas you paid supreme honor.
Moreover, many beings
You rescued from death by hunger and thirst.

For the sake of other, ordinary beings
You gave up body and life many times without attachment.
To seek the true and sacred Dharma
You gave up your flesh and bones many times as well.

Giving up all your own happiness,
You hold beings lovingly in your heart.
For the sake of many ordinary beings
You strive to cut through the net of doubt.

Having fully restrained the six faculties,
You long ago abandoned every desire.
You realized that the conditioned is impermanent and suffering,
Selfless, and empty.

The cause from which
All suffering is born is desire and craving.
Therefore, first, through the six faculties,
Desire should be cut off completely.

In every realm where sentient beings dwell
You abide always with great compassion.
Even having awakened to supreme awakening,
You do not abandon any of your former aspirations.

Whenever you see sentient beings
Tormented by suffering,
With overwhelming diligence
You strive to free them from it.

Having cultivated generosity, moral discipline, patience, diligence,
Meditative concentration and wisdom, all of them,
Just as a mother nurtures
Her only child with a loving mind,

So in the past too, toward every class of sentient beings,
You always gave rise to a loving mind.
Therefore, having swiftly awakened,
You free measureless assemblies.

Of all the conduct of awakening you have cultivated,
There is none you did not dedicate to the welfare of beings.
Therefore, toward all sentient beings
You do not abandon the six perfections.

Because you always accomplished unsurpassed awakening
In the evil times of the final age,
Now too, in the evil times of the final age,
You have fully awakened to unsurpassed awakening.

Gods, nāgas, humans and yakṣas
Holding evil views you have tamed, every one;
You cut through their doubts and set them in happiness
Upon the vajra-like path.

To measureless beings
You also give prophecy of supreme awakening.
Worthy of offerings, foremost among guides,
You have become the supreme field of merit.

Blessed One without equal,
With loving-kindness you protect all beings.
By your measureless, great renown
The worlds of the ten directions are pervaded.

Therefore bodhisattvas,
In order to accomplish their own aim,
Have gathered here together from every side
And come into the presence of the Great Sage.

Having heard the sacred teachings,
They give rise to minds of great joy,
And, rousing true diligence all the more,
They apply themselves to the conduct of awakening.

Through the power of the Guide's Dharma
All will attain complete awakening.
Therefore the assembly that you, Guide,
Have gathered here today is without precedent.

Some one hundred and thirty thousand yakṣas,
Who always ate flesh and drank blood,
Have all given up their evil deeds
And now incline and turn toward great awakening.

Some have gained special, supreme forms,
And patience and meditative absorption as well.
Some, all their defilements exhausted,
Have become arhats, supreme in the world.

Some cultivate the four immeasurables,
And some abide in the four means of attraction.
Some have gained the four correct knowledges,
And some abide in the patience that accords with truth.

Some have gained the heroic march absorption (śūraṃgama),
And some possess the eye of wisdom.
Some abide in acceptance of the unborn.
All of them are blessed by the Guide.

Through the power of the Blessed One's great splendor
The hosts of Māra are vanquished,
And all the ranks of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas)
Of ninety-five kinds are tamed.

The streams of beings of the hells, the animals,
The pretas and the asuras are all cut off.
Made happy, sentient beings, all of them,
Have come into the presence of the Victor.

The terrors of disease and weapons
And the terror of famine too are quelled here today.
Sentient beings who were blinded and bewildered,
Who had lost the true path, are delivered.

Those drunk and deranged with the afflictions
Are all set upon the path of peace.
I too have left aside all my tasks
And come to bow at the Blessed One's feet.

The blessed ones now dwelling
In measureless fields
All have praised you in every way;
Hearing this, all have come here.

Ocean of omniscience,
Your true qualities are beyond measure.
You who act for the welfare of many beings,
With a mind of devotion I pay you homage.

Having perfected measureless merit,
I have bowed at the Blessed One's feet.
Throughout measureless eons as well
May I worship you with many offerings.

Just as the Blessed One did,
I too have made such a vow:
In this degenerate world
May I attain unsurpassed awakening.

When the bodhisattva great being Kṣitigarbha had praised the Blessed One with these excellent verses, he and his retinue further strewed over the Blessed One divine fragrances, flowers of many kinds and measureless jewel ornaments, which became a jewel parasol in the sky above. Then, in order to listen to the sacred Dharma, he sat silently before the Blessed One.

Then all the assembly gathered in that circle of the assembly, seeing the bodhisattva great being Kṣitigarbha, were filled with wonder. Each of them offered divine incense of many kinds, flowers, jewel ornaments, robes, victory banners, pennants, parasols and so on, and having made offerings to the bodhisattva great being Kṣitigarbha, they spoke these words: "We have gained a great gain. Through the blessing of the Blessed One we have seen you, great being, face to face, and have been able to make offerings to you with reverence."

Then, within that circle of the assembly, there was a bodhisattva great being called Inquiring Rightly into Doubts. He rose from his seat, arranged his robe, bared his right shoulder, bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet, set his right knee upon the ground, joined his palms toward the Blessed One, and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, where has this noble son come from? How far from here is the buddha field where he dwells? With what qualities and roots of virtue is he endowed, that the Blessed One praises him with praises of many kinds, and that he in turn praises the Blessed One's inconceivable ocean of qualities? Since we have never seen him before and are filled with wonder, may the Blessed One please explain it to us."

The Blessed One said, "Enough, noble son. The extent of this great being's qualities and roots of virtue cannot be understood by the whole assembly of gods and humans together with the world. If the Tathāgata were to explain that great being's qualities and roots of virtue in full, all gods and humans together with the world would be bewildered and would not believe it."

Then the bodhisattva Inquiring Rightly into Doubts again said, "Blessed One, out of love for us, please explain it."

The Blessed One said, "Listen well and closely, and keep it in mind, and I shall explain to you just a portion of how that great being is endowed with measureless, inconceivable special qualities. Namely, he dwells at ease in the special heroic march absorption. He has thoroughly entered into understanding the domain of the tathāgatas. He has attained the special acceptance that phenomena are unborn. He has gained mastery over all the qualities of a buddha. He has attained acceptance with regard to omniscient wisdom. He has crossed to the far shore of the ocean of omniscient wisdom. He abides firmly in the absorption called Banner of the Lion's Stretch. He has climbed the mountain of omniscient wisdom. He has utterly vanquished all non-Buddhists and opponents. In order to bring all sentient beings to maturity, he dwells in every buddha field there is.

"In accordance with how that great being abides, so too do the buddha fields abide: through the absorptions he brings forth measureless special qualities, and so brings to maturity measureless sentient beings who are to be trained.

"In accordance with how that great being abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Bringing Forth Wisdom, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in that buddha field see, each in a way suited to them, the whole domain of the absorptions.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Endowed with Immeasurable Wisdom, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields, each as best they can, worship the buddha, the blessed one there, with reverence and with measureless excellent requisites of offering.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Perfection of Pure Wisdom, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields, each in a way suited to them, see the measureless faults and defects of the objects of desire, and their minds become pure.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Wisdom Endowed with Conscience and Shame, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields come to possess a higher conscience and sense of shame, are entirely parted from evil and nonvirtuous qualities, and their good intentions do not decline.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Clear Illumination Endowed with All the Vehicles, then all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields come to realize, with skill in means, the higher knowledges of miraculous power and of the divine eye, the higher knowledge recollecting former lives, the higher knowledge of death and birth, and the causes and results of this world and the world beyond.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Knowing through the Sorrowless Higher Knowledge, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields become free of all sorrow, dejection and torpor.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Endowed with Special Higher Knowledges, then all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields come to possess the higher knowledges together with skill in means.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Illuminating All the World, then through the power of that absorption all the world realms of the ten directions become free of darkness, and all who dwell in that buddha field see directly all the buddha fields of the ten directions.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Lamp of the Buddha, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields completely abandon wrong views and go for refuge to the Three Jewels.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too did the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Vajra Light, then through the power of that absorption, in those buddha fields the ring of encircling mountains, the great encircling ring, Sumeru, king of mountains, and all other mountains, the valleys, the small valleys, the ravines, gravel, potsherds, thorns, grasses and all noxious trees disappear. In that buddha field all that is reckoned as evil, poison, sorcery, savage beasts of prey, calamities, sudden accidents, epidemics, bad lands, murky skies, dust, dirt and all filth and impurity are stilled and cease to be. The ground of that buddha field becomes as level as the palm of the hand, with auspicious things of many kinds arising of themselves; it becomes pure, it becomes excellent, it becomes sublime, and it is adorned with many good signs.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Invincible Power of Wisdom, then through the power of that absorption the king of the māras dwelling in that buddha field, together with his retinue, all become terrified and go for refuge to the Three Jewels.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Lightning Rays, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields become free of fear of the world beyond and delight in the Dharma.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Endowed with the Supreme Taste, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in that buddha field are satisfied with food and drink just as they wish.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Endowed with Supreme Radiance, then through the power of that absorption there is no sentient being at all dwelling in those buddha fields who does not possess exceptional might and strength, and they become free of all the suffering of illness.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Supreme Sublime Requisites, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields, according to their inclinations, are never in want or lacking in seats and cushions, bedding, clothing, jewel ornaments, or anything whatsoever that belongs among the requisites of life, and they come to possess things utterly beautiful and exceedingly delightful.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Wisdom Free of Conflict, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields become at ease and light in body and mind, free of all hostility and all bondage, harmonious and joyful, and enthusiastic; and they come to delight in and aspire to generosity, moral discipline, patience, the undertaking of diligence, an unagitated mind, and the perfection of wisdom.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Exceptional Enthusiasm, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields come to possess a special, immeasurable supreme joy.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Light of the Worldly Path, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in that buddha field attain unobstructed wisdom and train in many kinds of pure action.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Firmly Abiding in the Vajra of the Victors, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in that buddha field possess all their faculties, their faculties do not decline, they constantly aspire to the path of true deliverance, and their minds become utterly peaceful.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Examining the Superior Banner, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields become thoroughly weary of their own sinful, nonvirtuous deeds and want no part of them; all of them guard virtue, and through the ten virtuous courses of action they are reborn as gods in the higher realms.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Endowed with Loving-kindness and Compassion, then through the power of that absorption there arise in all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields a mind of loving-kindness, a mind of compassion, a mind free of harm or violence, a mind impartial toward all, and a mind that brings benefit and happiness to one another.

"In accordance with how he abides, so too do the buddha fields become. If he enters into equipoise in the absorption called Thoroughly Gathering All Merit, then through the power of that absorption all the sentient beings dwelling in those buddha fields are completely free of strife, disputes, disease, and famine, and all unpleasant tactile objects, such as untimely wind, rain, snow, roughness, heat, and painful contact, are entirely pacified.

"Thus, if that great being enters into equipoise in the absorption called Ocean Lightning Light, then through the power of that absorption the whole great ground of those buddha fields becomes made of many kinds of jewels, completely free of every fault and defect, and it becomes adorned with immeasurable sublime requisites of life, such as trees of many kinds of jewels, clothing trees, vessel trees, ornament trees, flower trees, fruit trees, and trees of many kinds of music.

"In the same way, in short, in order to bring many sentient beings to maturity, this son of noble family every day, in the morning, enters into equipoise in absorptions as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River. Arising from those absorptions, he brings to maturity all the sentient beings to be trained in all the buddha fields of the ten directions, through whatever benefit and happiness will train each of them.

"For immeasurable, incalculable great eons in the past, this son of noble family has brought sentient beings to maturity in world systems where no tathāgata was present, during the time of the five degenerations.

"In times to come, if in some world system the danger of weapons arises and sentient beings are tormented, this son of noble family, seeing such beings, in the morning, through the power of his absorptions, quells those dangers of weapons and causes those sentient beings to cherish one another with minds of loving-kindness. If in some world system the danger of disease arises and sentient beings are afflicted, this son of noble family, seeing such beings, in the morning, through the power of his absorptions, quells the danger of disease and causes those sentient beings to attain perfect well-being. If in some world system the danger of famine arises and sentient beings are afflicted, this son of noble family, seeing such beings, in the morning, through the power of absorption, quells the danger of famine and fully satisfies those sentient beings with food.

"Thus through the force of many absorptions this son of noble family brings relief to sentient beings with immeasurable, incalculable, inconceivable benefit and happiness of these and other kinds. Possessing in this way immeasurable, countless, inconceivable kinds of special earnest endeavor, this son of noble family strives intensely for the aim of bringing benefit and happiness to all sentient beings.

"In times past, in order to benefit, bring happiness to, and fully mature many beings, he made great inexhaustible vows in the presence of immeasurable, countless buddhas, blessed ones, as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River, and he fully generated great compassion and great diligence that cannot be taken away. Having thus generated great compassion and diligence that cannot be taken away, and having made inexhaustible vows, through the force of advancing ever higher and higher, even in a single day and night, or in a mere instant or moment, he tames immeasurable koṭis of niyutas of ordinary beings, frees them from many kinds of suffering, and fulfills the hopes of each and every one of them with whatever each of them desires.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who suffer from many kinds of desire and are tormented by longing, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, they will obtain whatever they desire and be free of suffering, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings in want of food and drink, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, they will obtain whatever they desire and have food and drink, and they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are in need and lacking in clothing, jewel ornaments, medicine for illness, bedding, seats and cushions, or the requisites of life, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, they will find whatever they desire, and they will have in abundance clothing, jewel ornaments, medicine for illness, bedding, seats, and all the requisites of life; and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are separated from those they love and have met with those who are hostile to them, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, they will meet with those they love and be parted from those who are hostile, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings tormented in body and mind by illness and much suffering, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be at ease in body and mind and free of all illness, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who fight and quarrel, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, they will be free of resentful minds; with joy toward one another, with patience, and with minds of loving-kindness toward one another, they will feel remorse and grow fond of each other, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who suffer because they have been taken captive and thrown into prison, or bound with iron fetters, shackles, and wooden stocks, or bound limb by limb, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be freed from prison, iron fetters, shackles, and chains, and will be at ease and joyful, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who have been taken captive and are beaten or flogged or subjected to harsh punishment, and who face execution, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be completely freed from captivity, beating and flogging, and harsh punishment, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are exhausted in body and mind, without might or strength, and feeble, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will become light and at ease in body and mind and will gain might and strength, and in accordance with whatever will tame them they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings whose faculties are impaired or incomplete, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, none of them will have incomplete faculties; they will possess their faculties, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are insane and mentally disturbed, or possessed by bhūta spirits and grahas, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be free of insanity and mental disturbance and will be perfectly at ease, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings whose bodies and minds are tormented by attachment, aversion, delusion, anger, jealousy, pride, wrong views, sleep, heedlessness, doubt, and the like, and who cannot rid themselves of them, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be free of attachment and the rest, will be perfectly at ease in body and mind, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are burned by fire, swept away by water, carried off by wind, or have fallen down a precipice, or fallen from a tree or a rooftop, and are terrified and cowering, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be freed from all those harmful obstructions and perils, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are seized by venomous snakes or vicious beasts of prey, or struck by many kinds of poison, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be free of all violence and harm, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are struck by bhūta spirits and grahas, and suffer from constant fever, or fever that recurs every day, every second day, every third day, or every fourth day, or who are deranged so that their bodies and minds tremble and are disturbed, their mindfulness fails, and they lose consciousness, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be completely released from fear and will be perfectly at ease in body and mind, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are threatened by yakṣas, rākṣasas, bhūtas, piśācas, pūtanas, kaṭapūtanas, kumbhāṇḍas, and vitality-eaters, and besides these, by lions, tigers, wolves, and other vicious beasts of prey; who are beset and laid open to harm by mantras, sorcery, going into battle, and many other kinds of danger, so that they are utterly terrified in body and mind, afraid of death and wishing to live, seeking happiness and not wanting suffering, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all of them will be free of every danger, will suffer no harm to body or life, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who are afflicted and tormented by suffering because they desire great learning, or desire pure faith, or desire pure moral discipline, or desire to accomplish meditative concentration, or desire the higher knowledges, or desire wisdom, or desire liberation, or desire excellent forms, or desire pleasant sounds, or desire fragrant scents, or desire delicious tastes, or desire the finest tactile objects, or desire profit and gain, or desire fame, or desire merit, or desire to make a living by a craft, or desire flowers and fruits, or desire pleasure groves, or desire seats and cushions, or desire bedding, or desire to travel to other lands, or desire wealth and grain, or desire medicine for illness, or desire houses, or desire servants, or desire pigments, or desire rain, or desire water, or desire crops, or desire fans, or desire cool breezes, or desire fire, or desire a vehicle, or desire sons and daughters, or desire to be skilled in means, or desire to make merit, or desire warmth, or desire coolness, or desire a keen mind, or desire many other kinds of things of worldly and world-transcending aims, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, then through the force of the qualities of that son of noble family's sublime absorptions, and of his might and splendor, all of them will be free of the torment of suffering, all their hopes will be fulfilled, and in accordance with their fortune they will attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who have sown seed in fertile fields or infertile fields, whether or not they have tended them diligently, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, then through the force of the qualities of that son of noble family's sublime absorptions, and of his might and splendor, all their crops will flourish, and their fruits will ripen and be good.

"Why is that? Because for immeasurable, countless great eons in the past this son of noble family, in the presence of immeasurable, countless buddhas, blessed ones, applied himself with great diligence, and through the power of his aspirations, made with steadfast vows, in order to bring sentient beings to maturity he constantly upholds the whole great earth, constantly upholds all the multitude of seeds, and constantly enables all sentient beings to enjoy them as they wish. Through the power of that son of noble family's miraculous abilities, all the grasses that grow from this great earth, and the roots, branches, sprouts, leaves, trunks, petals, flowers, fruits, and so on of trees, all grow abundantly; and he makes the flowers and fruits of medicinal plants, trees, crops, and so on ripen, and become full, sweet, rich, and fragrant.

"In whatever places there are sentient beings who, because their attachment, aversion, delusion, and the like are fierce and sharp, actively engage in the ten nonvirtuous courses of action: killing, taking what is not given, sexual misconduct, lying, harsh speech, divisive speech, idle chatter, covetousness, malice, and wrong view, if they single-mindedly recite the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention make offerings to him with reverence, all their afflictions will be pacified, they will be completely free of the ten nonvirtues and will possess the ten virtuous courses of action, and there will arise in them minds of loving-kindness, compassion, benefit, and happiness toward all sentient beings.

"Thus, through the force of such sublime qualities, absorption, might, and splendor, and through his absorption of intense diligence, this son of noble family goes in an instant, in a moment, to immeasurable, countless buddha fields, and in each of those buddha fields, in an instant, in a moment, he frees from suffering immeasurable, countless sentient beings who are fit vessels, as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River, and brings them to happiness, so that in accordance with their fortune they attain everything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to passing into nirvāṇa.

"Thus that son of noble family, possessing such qualities, steadfast aspirations, and inconceivable diligence of intense effort, in order to bring sentient beings to maturity, in the world systems of the ten directions, appears to some in the body of Great Brahmā, the king, and teaches sentient beings the Dharma by which they will be trained. To some he appears in the body of the god Maheśvara (Śiva). To some he appears in the body of a god of the Paranirmitavaśavartin heaven of the desire realm. To some he appears in the body of a god of the Nirmāṇarati heaven. To some he appears in the body of a god of the Tuṣita heaven. To some he appears in the body of a god of the Yāma heaven. To some he appears in the body of a god of the Heaven of the Thirty-Three. To some he appears in the body of a god of the Four Great Kings. To some he appears in the form of a buddha. To some he appears in the body of a bodhisattva. To some he appears in the body of a pratyekabuddha. To some he appears in the body of a śrāvaka. To some he appears in the body of a universal monarch. To some he appears in the body of a kṣatriya. To some he appears in the body of a brahmin. To some he appears in the body of a śūdra. To some he appears in the body of a vaiśya. To some he appears in the body of a man. To some he appears in the body of a woman. To some he appears in the body of a youth. To some he appears in the body of a girl. To some he appears in the body of a gandharva. To some he appears in the body of an asura. To some he appears in the body of a kinnara. To some he appears in the body of a mahoraga. To some he appears in the body of a nāga. To some he appears in the body of a yakṣa. To some he appears in the body of a rākṣasa. To some he appears in the body of a kumbhāṇḍa. To some he appears in the body of a piśāca. To some he appears in the body of a preta. To some he appears in the body of a pūtana. To some he appears in the body of a kaṭapūtana. To some he appears in the body of an oppressor spirit. To some he appears in the body of a lion. To some he appears in the body of a fragrant elephant. To some he appears in the body of a horse. To some he appears in the body of a bull. To some he appears in the bodies of various beasts of prey. To some he appears in the body of Yama. To some he appears in the body of a guardian of the hells. To some he appears in the body of a hell being.

"Showing these and immeasurable, countless other kinds of bodies, and teaching the Dharma to many beings in accordance with their fortune, he establishes them on the irreversible stage of the three vehicles.

"Son of noble family, that great being thus possesses such inconceivable, distinctive qualities. He is a treasury of supreme and exceptional qualities. He is the source from which all the jewels of liberation arise. He is the perfectly pure eye of many bodhisattvas. He is the caravan leader who goes to the great city of nirvāṇa. Thus, without deliberate effort, through the great qualities explained at length above, he fully turns the great wheel of Dharma.

"It is like this, son of noble family: suppose someone, for a hundred eons, were to single-mindedly go for refuge to bodhisattva great beings as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River, such as Maitreya, Mañjuśrī, Avalokiteśvara, or Samantabhadra, recite their names, with focused attention make offerings to them with reverence and prostrate to them, and make aspirations for the sake of the hopes they desire. Compared with that, if someone, for an instant, a moment, a mere second, single-mindedly recites the name of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, and with focused attention makes offerings and prostrations to him with reverence, all the aspirations they hope for will quickly be accomplished and fulfilled.

"Why is that? Because this bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, for the sake of the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings, fulfills the aspirations of sentient beings; he is like a treasury of wish-fulfilling jewels. Thus, because for a long time, in order to bring sentient beings to maturity, this great being has cultivated great and utterly steadfast aspirations, great compassion, and diligence of intense effort, he surpasses all bodhisattvas. Therefore you should make offerings to him with reverence."

Then the bodhisattva great beings who had gathered from the ten directions, and the śrāvakas, gods, humans, yakṣas, gandharvas, and all the rest of the assembly rose from their seats, and each, as best they could, scattered flowers of gold, silver, and many kinds of jewels, and incense, as an offering to the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha. Besides these, they also presented to the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, as offerings divine garments, gems, precious jewels, garlands of pearls, pearl necklaces, garlands of gold, silver, and jewels, victory banners, pennants, parasols, and so on. Furthermore, with immeasurable sweet divine music and many kinds of praises in verse, they made offerings with reverence to the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, offered those many kinds of divine offering articles in turn to the Blessed One and spoke these verses:

"The offerings of gods, nāgas, yakṣas, and humans,
And of the bodhisattvas of the ten directions,
I present to the Lord of the World, of great qualities.
Please accept these supreme offerings."

Then, having spoken that verse, the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, bowed his head at the feet of the Blessed One, and the Blessed One spoke to him in verse as follows:

"Having developed a steadfast intellect and a pure resolve,
You quell the suffering of immeasurable sentient beings.
Like jewels, you are seen to be a source of happiness;
Like a vajra, you cut through the net of doubt.
Possessing a mind of great compassion, with diligence
You present sublime offerings to the Blessed One.
With oceanic wisdom you free beings from suffering;
Fearless, you have gone beyond existence."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, rose from his seat and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, there are these words of dhāraṇī, the great vidyā-mantra called Endowed with Fire and Water, Shining with Auspicious Light, Retaining What Is Heard, which bring about, for the close disciples of the Blessed One, who frees beings from the four floods, the monks, nuns, laymen, laywomen, and so on, the increase of mindfulness, the increase of guarding mindfulness, the increase of livelihood, the increase of bodily ease, the increase of good health, the increase of beauty and strength, the increase of renown, the increase of the requisites of life, the increase of relatives and friends, the increase of disciples, the increase of moral discipline, the increase of learning, the increase of generosity, the increase of absorption, the increase of patience, the increase of skill in means, the increase of the factors of awakening and of the noble truths, the increase of devotion to the true path of the Great Vehicle, the increase of the lamp of Dharma, the increase of bringing sentient beings to maturity, the increase of great loving-kindness and great compassion, the increase of pure, wholesome qualities, the increase of renown throughout the three realms, the increase of the rain of Dharma in the three realms, the increase of the savor of the whole great earth, the increase of the radiance and virtuous deeds of all sentient beings, the increase of joy in the Dharma and of virtuous conduct, the increase of the light of wisdom, the increase of the six perfections, the increase of the five eyes, the attainment of consecration, rebirth among the gods, and the increase of passing into nirvāṇa. I heard these words of dhāraṇī when I served buddhas, blessed ones of the past, as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River.

"Through this dhāraṇī all pure, wholesome qualities increase and arise; and the multitude of seeds, roots, sprouts, branches, trunks, leaves, petals, flowers, fruits, medicinal plants, and the flavor and fragrance of crops increase and arise; rainwater comes, and beneficial earth, water, fire, and wind increase and arise; joy and happiness increase and arise; jewels and wealth increase and arise; might and strength increase and arise; and all the requisites of life increase and arise. Moreover, through this dhāraṇī wisdom arises, and so the enemy of the afflictions is utterly vanquished. The mantra is this:

chimbho chimbho cim chimbho ākāṣa chimbho vakara chimbho amavar chimbho var chimbho vacir chimbho aroga chimbho dharma chimbho sateva chimbho satenihala chimbho vivarokaśava chimbho upaśama chimbho nāyan chimbho prajñāsamamone ratna chimbho kṣaṇa chimbho viṣemavariyā chimbho śāsītalamava chimbho vi adrasota mahele dam veyamave cakrāse cakrāvasile kṣile phile karabha vāravarīte hasere prarāve pare carābhandāne aradāne phānra ca ca ca ca hile mile akhatathāgekhe thagkhīlo thhāre thhāre mile madhe nante kule mile aṅkucitabhe ārai gyire vara gyire kutaśamamale ton gye ton gye ton gūle hurū hurū hurū kulostomile merito merite bhandhata harakhamarema huru huru

Purifying the stains of existence is declared virtuous;
Pacifying the eon of strife is also declared virtuous.
Purifying nonvirtuous intentions is declared virtuous;
Purifying the great elements is also declared virtuous.
Purifying the lower realms is declared virtuous;
Purifying the habitual tendencies of evil is also declared virtuous.
Fulfilling hopes is declared virtuous;
Ripening the crops is also declared virtuous.
This, declared virtuous by all the buddhas,
Blessed by all the tathāgatas,
Through this declared virtue the bodhisattvas
Bestow their blessings and rejoice.

"Blessed One, I heard these words of dhāraṇī, the great vidyā-mantra Endowed with Fire and Water, Shining with Auspicious Light, Retaining What Is Heard, through serving buddhas, blessed ones of the past, as numerous as the grains of sand in the Ganges River. This dhāraṇī makes all pure, wholesome qualities increase and arise, and likewise, in full, makes everything up to all the requisites of enjoyment increase and arise.

"Venerable Blessed One, this dhāraṇī frees beings from the four floods. For the close disciples of the Blessed One, the monks, nuns, laymen, laywomen, and so on, it makes their focus increase, and likewise, in full, makes everything up to all the requisites of life increase and arise. Blessed One, this dhāraṇī will cause the nectar-like noble scriptures to flourish and long endure, and it will be for the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings of the three realms."

Then, when the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, spoke these words of dhāraṇī, the great vidyā-mantra Retaining What Is Heard, Mount Khalateya shook violently, and koṭis of niyutas of divine instruments sounded of themselves without being played. Divine incense, flowers, and immeasurable jewels of many kinds fell like rain, and at that moment all those many in the assembly were struck with wonder and amazement.

At that time, in that assembly, the great goddess Great Auspiciousness, the great goddess Śrī, the great goddess Song of Worship, the great goddess Dṛḍhā, the great goddess Watery One, the great goddess Great Light, and others, eighteen thousand goddesses who hold sway over the four great elements, rose from where they were seated, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One (the Buddha), joined their palms, and with reverence said this to the Blessed One:

"It is wondrous! Blessed One, it is wondrous! Sugata, although we hold sway over the four great elements, we do not know the beginning, middle, and end of the four great elements, nor their arising, their ceasing, and what is in accord with them and what is not. Yet this great being, having attained the subtle and most profound perfection of wisdom, is supremely skilled in the characteristics of the beginning, middle, and end of the four great elements, and in their arising, their ceasing, and what is in accord with them and what is not."

The Blessed One declared: "So it is, so it is. Because this son of noble family has attained the subtle and most profound perfection of wisdom, he is supremely skilled in the characteristics of the beginning, middle, and end of the four great elements, in their arising, their ceasing, and what is in accord with them and what is not. Goddesses, you should understand it in this way.

"Just as the precious wish-fulfilling jewel, endowed with many qualities, rains down precious things of every kind and gives them to many beings, so too this son of noble family rains down the precious factors of awakening of every kind and gives them to sentient beings.

"Just as an island of jewels is completely filled with precious things of every kind, so too this son of noble family is endowed with the precious factors of awakening of every kind.

"Just as the divine pāricitra tree is beautiful with fragrance and flowers, so too this son of noble family is made exceedingly beautiful by the precious qualities of the Buddha of every kind.

"Just as the king of lions is not overpowered by any wild animal and is not afraid, so too this son of noble family is not intimidated or overpowered by any being.

"Just as the bright sun dispels all the darkness of the world, so too this son of noble family dispels all the darkness of ignorance and wrong views of all sentient beings.

"Just as the full moon, in the time of night, shows a pleasant path to all beings who have lost their way, so that they reach wherever they wish to go, so too this son of noble family, for all beings who in the night of ignorance have strayed from the three vehicles and wander in the wilderness of saṃsāra, shows the true path by means of the three vehicles, and through skillful means, in accordance with their fortune, sets them on the path of true deliverance.

"Just as the great earth is the support of all the multitudes of seeds, of plants and trees, of crops, and of all beings belonging to the element of earth, so too this son of noble family is the source from which all the distinguished qualities of the factors of awakening are taught.

"Just as Mount Meru, the great jewel king of mountains, is utterly firm, unshaken and undiminished, so too this son of noble family abides firmly in all the unshared qualities of a buddha; he does not diminish, because he never abandons any sentient being, and he is unshaken, because he dedicates all his roots of virtue.

"Just as all beings move about in space, so too all sentient beings make use of this son of noble family. Thus this son of noble family is endowed with immeasurable, incalculable qualities."

At that time the many in the assembly, hearing the praise of the immeasurable qualities of the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, were struck with wonder and amazement. They showed him honor and service, and with great joy and one-pointed mind they gazed at the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha without blinking.

Then, to restate those points clearly, the Blessed One spoke these verses:

"The true being, Kṣitigarbha,
Perfectly endowed with many qualities,
Bearing and showing forth the guise of a renunciant,
Has come to pay homage to the great Teacher.

He bestows happiness on all sentient beings,
Frees them from all the suffering of the three realms of existence,
And, raining down measureless things of every kind,
Makes offerings to the great Teacher.

Śakra (Indra), born free of stain,
Having looked about and examined all four directions,
Joined his palms with deep reverence
And extolled and praised the great Teacher:

'I see the assembly of the Blessed One:
Like the rays of light of a precious jewel,
It illuminates all the many buddha fields,
And there is nothing that is not made clear.

With the six higher knowledges he illuminates the world:
The one who has come and gathered here today
Is the most heroic Kṣitigarbha,
Who manifestly displays the conduct of one gone forth.

He is a treasure from which the seven noble riches come,
The roaring voice of the fearless Buddha,
The victory banner of many bodhisattvas,
The chief who guides many sentient beings.

He is a mine of the jewel of liberation,
An ocean of merit with steadfast diligence;
With a mind of loving-kindness he delights in compassion and tender care,
And frees many suffering beings.

He is a moat and a fortress guarding against fear,
And like the moon in a clear sky he shows the path.
Like the earth, he gives rise to all roots of virtue;
Like a vajra, he cuts through doubt completely.

He scatters the jewels of liberation to all;
Like water, he washes away all obscurations.
He becomes a parasol for those tormented by the heat of afflictions;
Like a physician, he cures every illness.

The merit of great renown obtained
By reciting the name of Kṣitigarbha for a single day
Surpasses that of reciting the names of other wise ones
Even for koṭis of niyutas of eons.

He liberates ordinary beings
And fetters all the afflictions.

He has perfected the samādhi of Heroic Progress (śūraṃgama),
And gone to the far shore of other samādhis as well.
Because the thirteen many conditions are also purified,
His wisdom is like space for all the many.

Of the measureless buddha fields
He destroys the masses of darkness of many beings.
He enters that samādhi according to each field,
And constantly enters the four meditative concentrations as well.

Having entered samādhi, he thoroughly dispels
The torment of the afflictions of sentient beings.
Ordinary beings, through their former evil deeds,
In fear of weapons, disease, and famine,
Are harmed and killed in many ways:
He sets them all completely free.

When beings whose five appropriated aggregates
Are harmed and tormented by many sufferings
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
All the sufferings of existence are pacified.

When beings, spinning on the wheel of suffering,
Who harm and injure one another,
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
They come to abide in patience and a loving mind.

When those terrified and frightened by the thirteen conditions,
And those pained by the condition of not finding what they seek,
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
They come to abide in happiness, without fear.

When those who delight in doing meritorious deeds, and who long for mindfulness,
Moral discipline, much learning, and wisdom,
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
All is fulfilled just as they hope.

When those who long for the forbearance and skill of each quality described,
And for medicines and stores of seeds,
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
They are endowed with all they hope for, and it is fulfilled.

When, wishing for fine crops, plants, and trees,
Or for sons and daughters, clothing, and servants,
People go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
All is fulfilled just as they hope.

Because he is endowed with all the many qualities,
He does not forsake this great earth but firmly upholds it.
Because of that, medicines and crops
Become rich, soft, and full of flavor.

When those obscured by many afflictions,
Who delight in the deeds of the ten nonvirtues,
Go for refuge to Kṣitigarbha,
All nonvirtue and affliction are pacified.

He displays bodies of every kind
And teaches the Dharma to many beings as well.
Because all the qualities of giving are perfected,
All sentient beings are cared for with compassion and made happy.

Even if for many hundreds of eons
One were to extol and praise his qualities,
It would be extremely hard to reach the end and complete them.
Therefore let all make offerings to him with reverence."

This concludes the first chapter, the Introduction, from the Great Collection, the sūtra called The Ten Wheels of the Bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha.

The second fascicle.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, touched his head to the feet of the Blessed One, placed his right knee on the ground, and bowing with joined palms, said this to the Blessed One:

"Ocean of measureless qualities,
I make a request of the Blessed One:
Whatever doubts and hesitations I have,
Please explain them to me at length."

The Blessed One declared: "True being, you are unobstructed in seeing all phenomena with wisdom. Nevertheless, because you wish for the benefit and happiness of sentient beings, ask the Tathāgata whatever you wish, and by the Blessed One's answer to each question I shall bring satisfaction to your mind."

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, spoke in verse:

"In the past, for thirteen eons,
I practiced the most difficult conduct,
And for the sake of all ordinary beings
Dispelled the three fears and the five degenerations.

Having made measureless offerings
To koṭis of niyutas of buddhas,
I saw in the past this assembly of pure intention
And the gathering of this great host.

The wise, who strive with diligence,
Have all assembled and come to this gathering.
To see these utterly faultless assemblies
Is a wonder and a marvel.

Why is it that in all these buddha fields,
Where wrongdoing has caused virtue to decline,
All the evil conduct shunned by the wise
Dwells side by side?

Beings commit the evils of immediate retribution,
Denigrate the true and sacred Dharma,
Revile the noble ones, and with wrong views
Expound the treatises of eternalism and annihilation.

They commit the deeds of the ten evils,
Have no fear of the world to come,
Have long abandoned the three vehicles;
These evildoers are bent upon the lower realms.

Their eyes obscured by ignorance,
They live by attachment, envy, and deceit.
How does the Buddha turn the wheel
And liberate these sentient beings?

How are the afflictions that link to rebirth
Cut by what is like a vajra?
How are dhāraṇīs attained,
And likewise how is forbearance made certain?

How great a wonder it is
That I have seen the Guide and this great assembly!
Such a gathering of all qualities
I have not seen in other directions.

Endowed with all the many qualities
And striving intently on the path of awakening,
How is it that for assemblies of the childish
The Buddha turns the wheel?"

The Blessed One declared: "Excellent, excellent! Son of noble family, in past times too, in the fields of buddhas as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges, in times of the five degenerations, you asked questions about such matters as these of blessed buddhas as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges. Because in the past you applied yourself with great earnestness to these matters you ask about, you have fully realized and understood them. Through the perfection of many excellent practices you possess a wisdom supremely skilled in means.

"It is excellent that you ask about such matters as these: in order to bring all sentient beings to full maturity and to bring them to the attainment of benefit and happiness; for the sake of the distinctive conduct by which all bodhisattvas, great beings, through skill in means, bring all sentient beings to full maturity by the six perfections beginning with giving, the treasury of noble conduct; in order to bring to completion the ocean of qualities of omniscient wisdom; in order to turn back all crowned kṣatriya kings who abide in nonvirtuous conduct from the three lower realms, so that they do not fall into them; and so that the lineage of the Three Jewels may flourish greatly and long endure in this field. Listen very well and keep it in mind, and I shall teach you."

"Blessed One, I shall do so," he replied, and listened to the Blessed One.

Then the Blessed One said this to the bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha: "Son of noble family, through the force of his former aspirations the Tathāgata is endowed with the ten kinds of buddha wheels. He dwells in a buddha field of the five degenerations, a place where all the sentient beings have turned away from all purely white qualities; are bereft of the seven precious riches of the noble ones; have grown distant from the wise; are entangled in the net of annihilationism and eternalism; take pleasure and delight in the chariot that goes to the lower realms; do not regard the sufferings of the next life with fear; place their trust in dwelling in the thick black darkness of ignorance; carry to completion the courses of action of the ten nonvirtues; commit the five acts of immediate retribution; denigrate the sacred Dharma and despoil the noble ones; and, having cast all virtuous qualities far away, carry to completion the nonvirtuous ones.

"Although he dwells in a field filled with such manifold sufferings, he dwells there in great ease. Free of timidity and free of fear and terror, he goes to the place of the great sages (ṛṣis), turns the buddha wheel, and subdues all non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) of false doctrine, together with the gods and māras. In the lineages of all ordinary beings he clears away the afflictions that are as hard as a vajra, and then establishes each of them according to their inclination. Those beings who have the power, he also makes irreversible in the three vehicles.

"Son of noble family, it is like this. Suppose that in some country there comes to be no government. All the people who live there are harassed by their own armies and by foreign armies, and they are troubled and in misery. Many of the people living there, being unhappy, fall into quarrels and disputes and countless mutual recriminations. There is lying, deceit, harsh speech, slander and strife. People become blind and dim-sighted; they are tormented by diseases of every kind, by virulent illnesses of cold and of heat, by many kinds of fevers, by madness, by wasting sickness, by indigestion, by agitation and stupor of mind; their faculties are impaired and their limbs and lesser limbs are damaged. They are destitute and bereft of clothing, of food, and of every kind of necessity for living. All their possessions are unpleasing. All the people go for refuge to non-Buddhists and to worldly gods; wrong views, wrong intentions and wrong inclinations spread everywhere; and having lost the true path, they are about to fall into the lower realms.

"At that time the elders living in that country, who are intelligent and steady of mind, learned, and of great integrity, gather and take counsel together. Assembling all the people of the countryside and the towns, they all agree in selecting one prince. He is one who in the past has given generously, kept moral discipline perfectly, applied himself with diligence, and practiced great austerities. He bears the marks of supreme merit. His faculties are complete and his limbs unimpaired; his body is well proportioned, neither too large nor too small; he is adorned with excellent major and minor marks; he has a supreme and excellent complexion. He is honored and served by all, pleasing to all the people living in that country, who long to see him. By nature he is upright and steadfast, endowed with loving-kindness and compassion, learned, accomplished in every skill, and his mind is well adorned with gentle forbearance. He is the eldest son of the chief queen.

"They bathe his body in pure water mixed with many kinds of fine perfume, and he puts on fine garments of many kinds, adorned with jewels and scented with incense. A precious jewel is fastened in the topknot on his crown, and on his head he wears a golden diadem adorned with garlands of jewel flowers, bound with exceedingly light and soft silk. He also wears earrings, necklaces, armlets, bracelets and other ornaments made of jewels, pearls, gold, silver, and many other kinds of precious things. His feet are shod in sandals made of many kinds of precious things. Above him a canopy is spread, a parasol made of many kinds of precious things. A throne is set out that in the past all the gods and sages guarded and upheld, guarded and protected by Śakra (Indra) and all the others. In a great palace built by the most skilled craftsmen they seat him on the throne on which the former kings sat, and they make him king.

"When he has ascended the throne, the bells and the great drums guarded and upheld by the lords of the gods, the lords of the nāgas, the lords of the yakṣas, the lords of the asuras and the lords of the kumbhāṇḍas are struck, and their sound is heard throughout the whole realm. Countless people belonging to the four great castes, beginning with the kṣatriyas, bathe their bodies thoroughly, put on clean clothes, and, holding in their hands countless parasols of many kinds of jewels, victory banners, pennants, precious jewels, pearls, gold, silver, conch, crystal, coral, beryl and so on, present them to that king. In addition, all the brahmins of high and noble birth, who live a pure life, are learned and of great knowledge, praise that king with verses and with countless fine songs and melodies. They also make the aspiration, 'May he obtain every kind of good fortune!' They sprinkle many kinds of auspicious substances on the king's head.

"Learned and knowledgeable men belonging to the families of the former king's ministers, who are honest, without guile and wise, are appointed as ministers, each according to his suitability and in proper rank. By carrying out the king's work, they put an end to all the attacks by their own and foreign armies that had formerly afflicted that country, and to all rebels and bad associates. All hostile enemies and all who do harm are pacified, the whole dark side is cleared away, and the whole bright side arises and increases.

"Son of noble family, when a great crowned kṣatriya king in this way possesses that single wheel, by the power of that wheel his kingdom becomes very great, all hostile enemies are thoroughly subdued, and his life too becomes long.
