The Blessed One said, "Son of noble family, so it is, so it is; it is just as you have said. Yet if any of those, from the virtuous kings up to the virtuous brahmins, do not themselves engage in the ten kinds of wheels of evil and also teach others who do engage in them to give up the ten wheels of evil, their heap of merit is greater than that former heap of merit: immeasurable, beyond reckoning, and impossible to express, reckon, count, or even illustrate by example.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, consider this: suppose a single person of great power appeared in the world and, for the sake of the Saṅgha of the four directions, built monasteries and dwellings, excellent and spacious, as vast as the four continents. He furnished all those places with everything needed: seats, robes, alms food, medicine for the sick, and all the requisites of life. Then, while the Tathāgata and the whole great assembly of his close disciples, the śrāvakas and bodhisattvas, dwelled there, he made offerings and paid homage to them without interruption, diligently applying himself to all kinds of virtuous deeds, day and night without laziness, for hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of years. Would that person's heap of merit be exceedingly great?"

The bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, replied, "Exceedingly great, Blessed One, exceedingly great! Sugata, that person's heap of merit is beyond measure and beyond reckoning; it cannot be expressed, reckoned, counted, or even illustrated by example. The Buddha alone knows it; no one else does."

The Blessed One said, "So it is, so it is; it is just as you have said.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, consider this: suppose a single person of great power appeared in the world and, for the sake of the Saṅgha of the four directions, built monasteries and dwellings, excellent and spacious, as vast as ten four-continent worlds. He furnished all those places with everything needed: seats, robes, alms food, medicines, and all the requisites of life. Then, while the Tathāgata and all the close disciples of the great Saṅgha of śrāvakas and bodhisattvas dwelled there, he made offerings and paid homage to them without interruption, diligently applying himself to all kinds of virtuous deeds, day and night without laziness, for hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of years. Would that person's heap of merit be exceedingly great?"

The bodhisattva, the great being Kṣitigarbha, replied, "Exceedingly great, Blessed One, exceedingly great! Sugata, that person's heap of merit is beyond measure and beyond reckoning; it cannot be expressed, reckoned, counted, or even illustrated by example. The Buddha alone knows it; no one else does."

The Blessed One said, "Son of noble family, so it is, so it is; it is just as you have said.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, consider this: suppose a single person of great power appeared in the world and, for the Buddha's relics, built a stūpa exceedingly beautiful, lofty, and vast, as large as a great trichiliocosm, a world system of a billion worlds. The heap of merit from building a stūpa for the Buddha's relics is greater than the heap of merit from building monasteries and dwellings for the Saṅgha of the four directions, as described before. That earlier merit does not come near even a hundredth part of it, nor even a thousandth part, nor even a hundred-thousandth part, nor even a koṭi-th part, nor even a niyuta-th part. It does not come near even a fraction expressible by number, by calculation, by measure, by example, or by cause.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, consider this: suppose this great trichiliocosm were completely filled with great arhats, every one of whom had attained the perfections, possessed the eight liberations, and was absorbed in meditative concentration, packed together like rice seedlings, sesame plants, bamboo, reeds, or a grove of sugarcane. Suppose all of them had been bound with the five bonds for a hundred thousand koṭis of years, and then a single person of great power appeared in the world. Wishing for merit, he untied all their bonds, washed all those arhats with scented water, and offered them Dharma robes and alms bowls for a hundred thousand years, and also gave them, just as they wished, dwellings, seats, clothing, alms food, medicine for the sick, and all the various requisites of life. And when those arhats had passed into nirvāṇa, he made offerings to them, cremated them, gathered their relics, and placed them inside stūpas made of the seven kinds of precious things.

Furthermore, the heap of merit from building stūpas for the relics of the buddhas is greater than the heap of merit from making offerings with jeweled victory banners, flags, parasols, incense, flowers, and music of every kind. Yet compared with the merit of untying the bonds of the arhats and making offerings to them, it does not come near even a hundredth part; it does not come near even a thousandth part; it does not come near even a hundred-thousandth part; it does not come near even a koṭi-th part; it does not come near even a niyuta-th part; and so on, until it does not come near any part that can be reckoned, counted, measured, compared, or likened.

Son of good family, if anyone, from a virtuous kṣatriya up to a virtuous brahmin, does not himself commit the ten wheels of evil and also counsels others, saying, "Do not engage in the ten wheels of evil," then his heap of merit is greater, immeasurably, incalculably, inexpressibly, than all of those earlier heaps of merit; and as much merit as arises, so much evil is also exhausted.

Son of good family, suppose there is a virtuous kṣatriya, or a virtuous minister, a householder, a merchant chief, a śramaṇa, or a virtuous brahmin who, in the future, in the last five hundred years, when the true Dharma is close to perishing, carefully guards the Dharma eye that I have taught, keeps himself well restrained, and also protects others. Mindful of the world to come, he treats the disciples who have gone forth in my teaching with great conscientiousness, whether they have become vessels of the Dharma or have not become vessels of the Dharma, and even if, in the end, they are without moral discipline: since they have shaved their hair and beards and wear saffron robes, he honors them with respect and does them no harm.

Or again, he carefully guards the true Dharma of the three vehicles, listening to it and retaining it. When he reveres the Dharma of the śrāvakas, he neither disparages nor denigrates the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and of the Great Vehicle, and he does not grow jealous of the followers of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle or of the Great Vehicle. When he serves the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, listening to it and retaining it, he neither disparages nor denigrates the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and of the Great Vehicle, and he does not grow jealous of the Śrāvaka Vehicle or of the Great Vehicle. When he serves the Dharma of the Great Vehicle, listening to it and retaining it, he neither despises nor denigrates the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he does not grow jealous of the followers of the Śrāvaka Vehicle or of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle; yet he does not make the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle his own realization. He realizes the genuine Dharma of the Great Vehicle itself.

Abiding in the Great Vehicle, he constantly attends upon those persons who keep moral discipline, who possess good qualities, and who apply themselves earnestly, from those who have begun to exert diligence up to those who abide in the fruit. He serves them, makes offerings to them, and honors them with a mind of faith, questioning them, listening to them, and retaining what they teach. He keeps far away from monks of corrupted discipline who engage in evil. He does not allow the property of the Saṅgha of the four directions, offered out of faith, to be wasted in ways contrary to the Dharma. He does not himself make free use of the property of the Saṅgha of the four directions, of the stūpas, or of the Saṅgha's own share, nor does he let others make free use of it. He venerates with great respect the persons who teach the Dharma of the three vehicles and lends them his strength, and he does not allow others to disparage, denigrate, or revile them. With deep faith he encourages those who have gone forth. He has faith in the noble word spoken by the Tathāgata and upholds it. He does not destroy stūpas. He maintains the monasteries that are the dwelling places of the Saṅgha of the ten directions. He does not oppress the disciples who have gone forth in my teaching, nor force them back into the household life. He does not himself engage in the ten kinds of wheels of evil, and he counsels others, saying, "Abandon the ten kinds of wheels of evil completely." He lives by the Dharma customs and governance of the former kings, gathering together and instructing many people of the world in the ten courses of virtuous action. He always relies on spiritual friends. He carries on the lineage of the Three Jewels and makes it flourish greatly. And he guards the Dharma eye so that it never sets.

Such people, from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, since they possess such qualities as these, will not see their livelihood and their enjoyments come to ruin. Gods, humans, yakṣas, pretas, kaṭapūtanas, and all the rest will rejoice in them and will protect and guard them with minds of loving-kindness. All the genuine fields of merit who have become vessels of the Dharma will also rejoice in them, and will protect and care for them with minds of compassion.

Through that cause and that condition, the land in which they dwell and all its people will become ever more prosperous and exceedingly happy. Foreign enemies will be unable to harm them; all will heed their commands and live together in harmony, undefeated. Because of this, they will strive ever more in the side of virtue, and the stream of nonvirtue will dry up completely. Because gods, humans, and others will flourish and protect them on every side, their lives will become very long. They will vanquish their own afflictions and crush the afflictions of others as well. By fully taking up the six perfections, the path of the bodhisattvas, they will destroy every evil path of wrong view, and so turn back from their long wandering in the ocean of saṃsāra. They will abandon evil friends and always rely on spiritual friends. In every place where they are born, they will meet buddhas and bodhisattvas, attend upon them, and make offerings to them without interruption. And before long, just as they intend, in whatever buddha-field they please, they will become fully and manifestly awakened in unsurpassed, perfect, complete awakening.

Then at that time, from within that assembly, all the lords of the gods with their retinues, up to all the lords of the piśācas with their retinues, rose from their seats, touched their heads to the feet of the Blessed One, joined their palms, and respectfully addressed the Blessed One with these words:

"Venerable Blessed One, in the time to come, in the last five hundred years, when the true Dharma in this buddha-field is close to utter destruction, suppose there are any, from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, who themselves completely abandon the ten wheels of evil and also counsel others, saying, 'Abandon them completely'; who are skilled in protecting themselves and others, skilled in being mindful of the world to come; who guard the true Dharma, and carry on the lineage of the Three Jewels and make it flourish without interruption; and so on, in short, just as the Blessed One has taught, those persons and the rest, who listen with respect to the Dharma of the three vehicles and retain it, and never conceal it; who carefully protect and make offerings to persons abiding in the three vehicles and do them no harm; who carefully protect the wealth and goods of the Three Jewels and do not let them be lost. To those, from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, we and our retinues will give protection and shelter, and we will cause these ten things also to arise and increase for them.

"What are the ten? Long life; and the arising and increase of freedom from obstacles, the arising and increase of freedom from illness, the arising and increase of a retinue, the arising and increase of wealth and jewels, the arising and increase of the requisites of life, the arising and increase of rank, the arising and increase of renown, the arising and increase of virtuous companions, and the arising and increase of wisdom. Venerable Blessed One, if those from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins completely abandon the ten kinds of wheels of evil, counsel others too to abandon them, and possess the qualities mentioned before, then we will protect and shelter them, and these ten things too will arise and increase.

"Moreover, Blessed One, whoever, from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, possesses those qualities mentioned before, we and our retinues will protect and shelter, and we will make them entirely free of these ten things. What are the ten? Namely: freedom from all hostile enemies; freedom from unpleasant objects of form, sound, smell, taste, and touch; freedom from all fevers and diseases; freedom from all wrong grasping and evil views; freedom from going for refuge to what is false; freedom from all false and evil omens; freedom from all false and evil deeds; freedom from all false and evil friends; freedom from all the mire of the household life; and freedom from all sudden, untimely death. Venerable Blessed One, if any, from exceedingly virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, possess the qualities spoken of before, we and our retinues will protect and shelter them, and we will cause them to obtain these ten things of freedom.

"Moreover, Blessed One, whoever, from virtuous kṣatriyas up to virtuous brahmins, applies himself earnestly to perfecting the qualities spoken of before, we and our retinues will strive to protect and shelter. And we will make the king with his retinue, and all the people dwelling in that land, entirely free of these ten things. What are the ten? Freedom from all enemies of foreign lands; freedom from all enemies within one's own land; freedom from all inauspicious pretas; freedom from drought; freedom from harmful rains; freedom from all untimely heat, cold, wind, violent rain, frost, and harmful hail; freedom from all planets and ill omens that have turned malign; freedom from all the harms of famine; freedom from untimely death through disease; and freedom from all wrong grasping and evil views. Venerable Blessed One, if a virtuous kṣatriya king applies himself earnestly to all those qualities spoken of before and brings them to perfection, we and our retinues will strive to protect and shelter him, and we will cause the king with his retinue, and all the people dwelling in that land, to obtain these ten things of freedom."

Then the Blessed One praised the lords of the gods with their retinues, up to the lords of the piśācas with their retinues, saying, "Excellent, excellent! These matters to which you have so pledged yourselves are right for you to do; and by that cause and that condition, they will also bring you benefit and happiness for a long time."

Then the great Brahmā Devagarbha further addressed the Blessed One with these words: "For the sake of all the virtuous kṣatriyas in this buddha-field in the time to come, I offer these dhāraṇīs, whose resolve to protect the land never turns back. Through the splendor and power of the words of this great dhāraṇī-mantra called the Irreversible Wheel, for the full protection of the land, in the time to come in this buddha-field all virtuous kṣatriya kings will be unassailable by any hostile enemy, and all hostile enemies will be pacified and turn back of their own accord. Their actions of body, speech, and mind will be well restrained, and they will be extolled and praised by the wise. They will completely abandon evil things and always apply themselves earnestly to virtuous things. They will always abandon evil views and evil partisanship, and with steadfast diligence and great exertion they will apply themselves earnestly to the Great Vehicle. They will bring to full maturity immeasurable, countless sentient beings who have become vessels. Not depending on others for their understanding, they will become wise by nature; and by applying themselves earnestly to perfecting the precious treasury of the six perfections, they will completely abandon anger, avarice, jealousy, and all the other stains of affliction. All humans, nonhumans, and others will protect them with respect. They will not forget their duties. Delighting in the four means of gathering, they will not abandon sentient beings. And they will never be parted from the vessels of the Dharma who have become fields of merit."

Again the Blessed One spoke to Devagarbha: "For the protection of the lands of virtuous kṣatriya kings in this buddha-field in the time to come, speak the words of the great dhāraṇī called the Heart of the Irreversible Wheel. Declare how, through the power and splendor of the words of this great dhāraṇī called the Heart of the Irreversible Wheel for the full protection of the land, in the time to come in this buddha-field all virtuous kṣatriya kings will be unassailable by any hostile enemy, and so on in full, up to how they will be protected so as never to be parted from all of them, up to the buddhas and the retinues of the buddhas."

Then the great Brahmā Devagarbha spoke these words of the great dhāraṇī called the Heart of the Irreversible Wheel for the full protection of the land:

tadyathā muni mori muna garbhe muni khili dhaye muni roga bicale muni halite muni game śukla pakṣe pālala pakṣe mrila pakṣe souri sakriti toraṇa kricale pataga śakrite kukula mrile apakṣa sara odaha kiliba muni pāthaba svāhā

When the great Brahmā Devagarbha had spoken these dhāraṇīs, he further addressed the Blessed One with these words: "Blessed One, together with this great assembly, please rejoice in this great dhāraṇī that I have spoken."

The Blessed One said, "Excellent, excellent!" And all the great assembly also said, "Excellent, excellent!"

Then at that time the Blessed One spoke these words to the venerable Mahāmaudgalyāyana and to the bodhisattva, the great being Maitreya: "Son of good family, retain as a dhāraṇī these words of the great dhāraṇī called the Heart of the Irreversible Wheel for the full protection of the land, which the great Brahmā Devagarbha has offered. In the time to come, in this buddha-field, teach it in full to all the virtuous kṣatriya kings as well. Through that cause and that condition, it will bring benefit and happiness to all the virtuous kṣatriya kings with their retinues, and to all the people dwelling in their lands. They will always turn the wheel of Dharma; they will become greatly renowned and of great splendor; they will overcome evil views and establish right view; they will guard the Dharma eye and carry on the lineage of the Three Jewels without interruption; they will bring to full maturity immeasurable, numberless sentient beings; their faith in the Great Vehicle will be utterly firm; and abiding for a long time in the cultivation of the six perfections, they will cut through all obscurations and swiftly reach the final state."

Then, to recite these meanings clearly, the Blessed One spoke these verses:

At that time the great Brahmā Devagarbha
Addressed the Supreme among Two-Legged Beings thus:
"People of keen faculties
Aspire to cultivate samādhi and to make merit;
Wise kings establish the ways of the Dharma
To lift up and deliver those who wander far astray.
Among these three matters of conduct,
Which ones dispel doubt and do not turn back?"

The Blessed One spoke thus:
"If one does not abide in them, does not do them, has not done them,
Then though great of intellect and sharp in wisdom,
One will fall into the Avīci hell.
The unwise, and even the pure,
If they delight in practicing the wheel of the ten evils,
Since their roots of virtue are cut off entirely,
Will swiftly fall into the hells.

"Samādhi cuts through all afflictions;
The merit of hearing does not.
Therefore those who wish to pass into nirvāṇa
Should always rest evenly in meditative concentration.
The wise who undertake great diligence,
Who guard and uphold my true Dharma,
And have faith and respect for those who wear the saffron robe,
Will be freed from the ocean of afflictions.

"Dwelling in utterly solitary wilderness,
Keeping far away from deeds of immediate retribution,
Upholding moral discipline and cultivating samādhi,
Through faith they will cross the ocean of existence.
Those who have faith in all three vehicles
Make my true Dharma flourish;
If they make offerings to those who wear the saffron robe,
They will surely become an ocean of every kind of quality.

"Having thoroughly tamed the mind so hard to tame,
They do not speak of the faults of monks;
Content, living by the lineage of the noble ones,
They will surely become the holiest, the supreme among two-legged beings.
Abandon sinful monks,
And rely on the places of noble conduct.
Do not use or consume the property of the Saṅgha,
And you will swiftly attain great awakening.

"Whatever happiness and joy there are in all three realms,
They all arise from the Three Jewels.
Therefore, you who wish for happiness and joy,
Always make offerings to the Three Jewels.
Caṇḍāla kings and others like them,
Companions of monks who practice evil,
By committing offenses against the Three Jewels,
Will fall into the Avīci hell.

"The evil of ten oil pressers
Equals the evil of one who sells her body;
The evil of ten who sell their bodies
Equals the evil of one seller of liquor;
The evil of ten taverns
Equals the evil of one butcher;
The evil of ten slaughterhouses
Equals the evil of one king.

"But kings who are truly virtuous
Make all my true Dharma flourish;
By making offerings to all three vehicles
They will thereby become an ocean of every kind of quality.
The heap of merit gained
By filling Jambudvīpa with the seven precious things
And offering them to the Buddha and the Saṅgha
Does not match even the merit of guarding the Buddha's Dharma.

"The heap of merit gained
By building dwellings for the Buddha and the Saṅgha
Equal in extent to ten four-continent worlds
Does not match even the merit of guarding the Buddha's Dharma.
The heap of merit gained
By building stūpas for the buddhas
Equal in extent to a great trichiliocosm
Does not match even the merit of guarding the Buddha's Dharma.

"Beyond untying the bonds of the arhats
And making offerings of every kind,
The merit of placing no obstacle before the Dharma
Is more excellent than that.
Greater than whatever special insight arises
When the wise cultivate samādhi
For a thousand koṭis of eons
Is the merit of guarding the Dharma.

"Those exceedingly virtuous kings
Who abandon the wheel of the ten evils
And shelter this true Dharma of mine,
Or else protect those who wear the saffron robe,
Do not disparage or denigrate
The three vehicles I have taught, or the persons who follow them.

And listen to all of them and make offerings to them,
And act as refuge and protector to those who teach the Dharma,
And do not let the wealth of the Three Jewels go to waste,
And place no obstacle before those who wear the Dharma robes.
If they show respect to all, both the vessels and those who are not vessels,
Their merit is beyond compare.

"Just as, when the five suns have risen,
All the great oceans are dried up,
So the one who guards my Dharma
Will dry up the ocean of the afflictions.
Just as, when destruction by wind comes about,
All the mountains are destroyed,
So the one who guards my Dharma
Will vanquish every defilement.

"Just as, when destruction by water comes about,
The great earth is swept away by the water and destroyed,
So the one who guards my Dharma
Will vanquish unwanted results.
Just as the precious wish-fulfilling jewel
Fulfills all desires completely,
So the Dharma of the three vehicles
Fulfills the aspirations of all sentient beings.

"Whoever finds an excellent vase
Is parted from poverty and gains happiness and comfort;
So whoever meets the Dharma of the Buddha
Will cut through doubt and hear of awakening.
Just as the full moon on the fifteenth day
Illuminates all of space with its light,
So the person who guards the Dharma
Pervades the whole dharmadhātu with wisdom.

"Just as space is everywhere the same,
Without substance and without characteristics,
So the person who guards the Dharma
Knows all phenomena as of a single taste.
Just as the spreading light of the sun
Dispels all the darkness of the world,
So the person who guards the Dharma
Always brings happiness to the whole world."

This concludes the third chapter, on what should not be dwelt in or practiced, from the very extensive great sūtra called The Ten Wheels of Kṣitigarbha.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being called Vajragarbha, who was seated within the circle of that assembly, rose, touched his head to the feet of the Blessed One, draped his upper robe over one shoulder, set his right knee on the ground, joined his palms, bowed, and addressed him in verse:

"Those who through broken moral discipline have lost their pure qualities,
Since they are not vessels of the noble, are not your children, so you have said.
You said: 'Those who have abandoned the nature of a śramaṇa and are like ashes
Do not grace this pure assembly of mine.
Steeped in the three stains, having lost cessation and the path,
They are not fit to receive any special offering.
Of the wealth dedicated to the Saṅgha of the four directions
I have not permitted them to use even the smallest share.
Those who have broken even one of the four roots of moral discipline
Are not included in the Saṅgha, like the ocean that keeps no corpse.'
Why then, here and now, toward sinful monks,
Do you accept them with compassion and not cut them off with punishment,
And urge, 'Still, make offerings to them as to me'?

"Loving and compassionate one, you said: 'Abandoning all ill will,
If one listens with respect to the Dharma they teach...'
Yet you, who have gained the accumulations of merit and wisdom, of great compassion,
Protector of the world endowed with superknowledges, said in other sūtras:
'Have faith in the Great Vehicle,
The subtle and profound path of perfect awakening,
And abandon that path of liberation through the two vehicles.'
Why then, here and now, do you teach three vehicles,
And urge all to listen to them and to honor them?

"The faculties, the powers, the factors of awakening, and the fruits of the śramaṇa
Arise from this sūtra, and it is not said that they arise elsewhere.
Here, in the incomparable noble eightfold path,
All three vehicles alike enter that path;
If those who long for liberation rouse great diligence,
They will attain awakening, each according to their hopes.
When the Lord of all beings distinguishes these matters,
And shows that these earlier and later teachings do not conflict,
All the assemblies of gods, humans, and bodhisattvas
Will realize the genuine truth and give rise to the joy of liberation.

"Whom will the teaching of the Great Vehicle benefit,
And whom will the teaching of the Great Vehicle harm?
When the teaching of the ten liberations through the Śrāvaka Vehicle
Is heard, whom will it harm, and whom will it benefit?
Who, on hearing this Dharma, rises higher and higher?
Who, on hearing this Dharma, falls lower and lower?
How, through weariness at the faults of all conditioned things,
Will the ocean of aging and death be swiftly dried up?
Those who strive greatly in virtue day and night,
Abiding in which supreme aim, and traveling by which vehicle,
Are delivered from the four rivers so vast and deep?
Protector of the world, distinguish these, and protect all!"

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to the bodhisattva, the great being Vajragarbha: "Excellent, excellent! Son of good family, it is excellent that, for the benefit and happiness of countless people, and for the great welfare of gods, humans, asuras, and others, you have asked the Tathāgata about these extremely profound matters. Listen very well and keep it well in mind, and I shall explain it to you in detail." When he had spoken thus, the bodhisattva Vajragarbha listened to the Blessed One.

The Blessed One said: "Son of good family, these ten kinds of persons wander in saṃsāra and find a human body extremely hard to obtain. What are the ten kinds of persons? They are these: those who do not cultivate roots of virtue; those who do not perform meritorious deeds; those who take rebirth through the defilements; those who follow evil friends; those who do not look with fear upon the suffering that ripens as a result in the world to come; those whose desire grows rampant; those whose anger grows rampant; those whose delusion grows rampant; those whose minds are intoxicated and confused; and those who cling to the evil of wrong views. Because of these ten kinds of things that should not be dwelt in or practiced, sentient beings destroy the root of moral discipline and break their discipline, and so they fall into the lower realms.

"What are the ten kinds of things that should not be dwelt in or practiced? They are these. Among those who have gone forth in my teaching, there are some who have lapsed in higher conduct but have not lapsed in conviction. There are some who have lapsed in conviction but have not lapsed in higher conduct. There are some who have lapsed in both higher conduct and conviction. There are some who have lapsed in moral discipline but have not lapsed in view. There are some who have lapsed in view but have not lapsed in moral discipline. And there are some who have lapsed in both moral discipline and view.

"There are some who, though they have not lapsed in higher conduct, conviction, moral discipline, or view, fall under the sway of evil friends and do what should not be dwelt in or practiced. There are some who, though they have come to rely on spiritual friends, are dull by nature, mute like sheep, and cannot even distinguish one kind of deed from another; though they hear the spiritual friend teach what is virtuous and what is unvirtuous, they do not understand it, do not retain it, and do not know it, and through that cause and that condition they do what should not be dwelt in or practiced. There are some whose minds are disturbed because they search everywhere, never wearied, for wealth, jewels, and all kinds of requisites of life, and so they do what should not be dwelt in or practiced. And there are some who, afflicted and tormented by the many illnesses of conditioned existence, resort to worldly gods and practice mantras and the like, and through that cause and that condition they do what should not be dwelt in or practiced.

"In this way, because of these ten kinds of things that should not be dwelt in or practiced, sentient beings lapse from the root of moral discipline and do not become vessels of the noble ones in this very life; and because their discipline is broken, they fall into the lower realms.

"Son of good family, a person may have lapsed in higher conduct but not in conviction, and because of some one of the things that should not be dwelt in or practiced he may have broken one of the root precepts. If he is then greatly afraid and terrified at this, holds himself in check and feels shame, does nothing of the kind from then on, and abandons the evil, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, calls him a śramaṇa who corrupts the path. Why is that? Because although he has committed so great an evil deed, he does not conceal it but confesses at once, and with scruple and shame he feels remorse. Since through his scruple and shame he confesses it individually and the evil is purified, he will cut off the stream of doing it from then on. He is not included in any of the formal acts and rites of śramaṇas; he is to be expelled, and he has no right to use any of the requisites of the śramaṇa's life. Yet that person becomes a vessel for the Dharma of the three vehicles, and therefore the Tathāgata, out of loving concern, at times teaches him the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, at times teaches him the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and at times teaches him the Dharma of the unsurpassed vehicle. And so, in two or three lifetimes, through the power of aspiration and the power of the spiritual friend, all the evil deeds he has done, all that has become evil, are purified. Then some attain the fruit of the Śrāvaka Vehicle; some pass into nirvāṇa through the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle; and some may even come to realize the meaning of the vast, unsurpassed vehicle. In the same way one should understand the case of one who has lapsed in moral discipline but not in view.

"If a person has lapsed in conviction but not in higher conduct, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, teaches the Dharma of accomplishing the four abodes of Brahmā. Such a one is a vessel of the Śrāvaka Vehicle; such a one possesses the vessel of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

"If a person has lapsed in both higher conduct and conviction, then although he does not become a vessel for the Dharma of any vehicle, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, speaks in praise of generosity.

"If a person has lapsed in view but not in moral discipline, then although he does not become a vessel for the Dharma of any vehicle, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, teaches him the Dharma of dependent origination, so that he abandons the evil of wrong views and, in this very body, realizes the Dharma of the śrāvakas or of the pratyekabuddhas; or else he causes him to enter into it and realize it in another lifetime.

"If a person has lapsed in both moral discipline and view, he is not a vessel of the Dharma of the noble ones; yet the Tathāgata, in order to help him, speaks in praise of generosity.

"If a person, though he has not lapsed in any of higher conduct, conviction, moral discipline, or view, has fallen under the sway of evil friends and relies on them and lives with them, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, praises and teaches the ten courses of virtuous action.

"For a person who, though he has come to rely on a spiritual friend, is bad and dull by nature, mute like a sheep, and grasps nothing at all of virtue or nonvirtue, the Tathāgata, in order to help him, speaks in praise of it so that he will become habituated to recitation.

"For those persons who are tormented by the many kinds of sickness of desire and intoxicated by views about the many kinds of conditioned things, the Tathāgata, in order to help them, teaches as follows. To those who seek liberation he teaches the four noble truths of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, which bring one out of saṃsāra. To those who hold the view of annihilation he speaks in praise of the Dharma of dependent origination. To those who hold the view of permanence he teaches that, from these streams of existence in the three realms, one dies, transmigrates, and is born elsewhere, circling without interruption like a potter's wheel; and he teaches impermanence and the other teachings.

"Son of good family, nothing that the Tathāgata has taught through names, signs, letters, words, and the sounds of speech is fruitless or meaningless; there is nothing at all that he did not teach for the sake of bringing sentient beings to full maturity. Therefore, if anyone disparages or denigrates the Dharma spoken by the Tathāgata, since that is the evil of destroying the eye of the true Dharma for sentient beings, he will incur immeasurable evil misdeeds, surpassing the acts of immediate retribution or equal to the acts of immediate retribution.

"Whoever disparages or denigrates, or obstructs and hinders, the true Dharma that I have spoken for the benefit and happiness of sentient beings, whether it was spoken on the basis of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, or on the basis of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, or on the basis of the Great Vehicle, or who, at the very least, acts to conceal even a single verse of it, should be known to be denigrating the true Dharma. Because he does away with the eight limbs of the noble path and ruins the eye of the true Dharma for all sentient beings, that person in this way both engages in great ruin himself and leads others to engage in great ruin. Further, that person is said to rely on a Saṅgha without conscience or shame and to disparage and denigrate the true Dharma of the Tathāgata.

Son of good family, there are, further, four kinds of monk. What are the four? They are the monk in the ultimate sense, the conventional monk, the monk who is like a dumb sheep, and the monk without conscience or shame.

Among these, who is the monk in the ultimate sense? It is this: the buddhas, the Blessed Ones; the monks who are bodhisattva great beings, endowed with great qualities and having mastery over all phenomena; those of the awakening of a pratyekabuddha; arhats; non-returners; once-returners; and stream-enterers. These seven kinds of person are counted as monks in the ultimate sense. And as for one who lives in a household, who has not shaved off his hair and beard, who does not wear the Dharma robes, who has not received the whole moral discipline of the prātimokṣa that belongs to going forth, and who is not included in any of the formal acts, namely the motion, the act of request, the poṣadha, and all the procedural acts: because such a one possesses the noble Dharma and has attained a noble fruit, he too is counted as a monk in the ultimate sense. These are called monks in the ultimate sense.

Among these, what is the conventional monk? One who has shaved off his hair and beard, wears saffron robes, has gone forth, and possesses the moral discipline of the prātimokṣa is called a conventional monk.

Among these, what is the monk who is like a dumb sheep? He does not know whether he has broken the root offenses and the rest or has not broken them. He does not know whether they are grave or light. Though he commits subtle faults of many kinds, he does not know to regret them and confess them. He is careless about the faults he has committed, pays no heed to minor wrongdoings, and does not fear them. He does not rely on wise persons. He does not, from time to time, attend upon and serve those who are learned and endowed with wisdom. Nor does he know how to ask and inquire respectfully. Those persons who do not ask or inquire what is virtue, what is non-virtue, what is wrongdoing, what is free of wrongdoing, what is good to do and what is not good to do are counted as monks who are like dumb sheep, and are called monks who are like dumb sheep.

Among these, who is the monk without conscience or shame? It is the being who turns to my teaching and goes forth merely for the sake of a livelihood. Having gone forth, he breaks all the observances of the moral discipline of the prātimokṣa. Without conscience or shame, he pays no heed to the result of suffering in the next world and does not fear it. He is like a shellfish rotten within and full of filth. His conduct is like that of a dog at the sound of a conch. He constantly speaks falsehood and has no words of truth. He is gripped by avarice, envy, delusion and pride. Far removed from the three supreme and sacred kinds of action, attached to gain, honor and fame, he delights in the six sense-objects, is distracted by desire, and is attached to the objects of form, sound, smell, taste and touch. Such persons are counted among those without conscience or shame who disparage and denigrate the true Dharma; such a one is called a monk without conscience or shame.

Son of good family, among those called monks in the ultimate sense, some are counted among the śramaṇas who are victors by the path. Victor by the path means one who abides in the eight limbs of the noble path, and who both frees himself from the flood of the afflictions and frees others as well. How is that? The buddhas, the Blessed Ones, the pratyekabuddhas, and the arhats: these three kinds of person are called victors by the noble path, because they are far removed from the whole cycle of the limbs of existence. Further, the Saṅgha of bodhisattvas, who do not depend on others, who see all phenomena with unobstructed wisdom, and who gather in all sentient beings for the sake of their benefit and happiness, is also counted among the śramaṇas who are victors by the path.

Some of the monks in the ultimate sense and conventional monks are counted among the monks who teach the path. The person who possesses the moral discipline of the prātimokṣa and, relying on knowledge and the power to instruct, from the genuinely virtuous ordinary person up to one who possesses worldly right view, through the power of miraculous display distinguishes, teaches and explains to others the dharmas of the noble path, is the first and lowest kind of śramaṇa who teaches the path; he is called a śramaṇa who teaches the path. The person who has attained the fruit of stream-entry is called the second. The person who has attained the fruit of once-returning is called the third. The person who has attained the fruit of non-returning is called the fourth. Further, the Saṅgha of bodhisattva great beings is called the fifth. Those who abide from the first ground up to the tenth ground, and those who abide in their final body of existence, are also counted among the śramaṇas who teach the path.

Those who possess the moral discipline of the prātimokṣa and possess pure deportment are counted among the śramaṇas who live by the path; because they sustain their life by the path, they are called śramaṇas who live by the path. Further, the bodhisattva great beings who gather in all sentient beings for the sake of their benefit and happiness, and who apply themselves diligently to the six perfections, are also called those who live by the path.

Thus these three kinds of śramaṇa, the victors by the path, the teachers of the path, and those who live by the path, are called the genuine field of merit for the world. The śramaṇas other than these are called corrupters of the path; although they are not genuine, they too are reckoned as a field of merit. The person who dwells in and relies on a Saṅgha without restraint or shame is like a corpse within the Vinaya and Dharma that I have rightly taught. So it was said. Because he is not a vessel for the Dharma and is to be expelled from the ocean of the pure Saṅgha, I do not say of that person, "I am his teacher," and that person is not my śrāvaka.

Shameless monks who have not become vessels for the Dharma may nonetheless hold me as their teacher, have great faith in my relics and in my images, and have great faith also in my Dharma, in the Saṅgha, and in the moral discipline of the noble ones. Because of that, they do not themselves hold wrong views, nor do they cause others to cling to wrong views. They teach extensively the Dharma that I have taught, praising and extolling it highly, and they do not disparage or denigrate it. They constantly make right aspirations, and as soon as they have committed a wrongdoing they confess it again and again, regret it and give it up. If they do so, many karmic obscurations will be pacified. In this way, by the force of his faith in the Three Jewels and in the moral discipline of the noble ones, such a person surpasses the ninety-five kinds of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) by hundreds of thousands of times. Although he has not quickly arrived at the city of nirvāṇa, even a universal monarch should not denigrate him; what need, then, to speak of other people who are not such a monarch?

Therefore, because the Tathāgata knows and sees the distinct characteristics of the karma of all sentient beings and of the phenomena they experience, he has said: "Kṣatriyas and others have no right to revile, abuse or punish those who, having faith in the Dharma of my teaching, have shaved off their hair and beard and wear saffron robes." If anyone shows contempt for, reviles and abuses, or punishes any of those who have gone forth, the ripening of the fruit of that wrongdoing is just as has been explained at length.

Further, when someone goes forth from home into homelessness in my teaching, shaves off his hair and beard, and puts on saffron robes, all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, of the past, the future and the present hold him in their compassion. His deportment, his appearance, and his wearing of the Dharma robes are likewise held in compassion by all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, of the past, the future and the present. Therefore, to show contempt for one who has gone forth, who has shaved off his hair and beard and wears saffron robes, is to show contempt for all the buddhas, the Blessed Ones, of the past, the future and the present. For this reason and on this account, the wise and intelligent, those who are weary of suffering and afraid of it, and who long for the happiness of gods and humans and for the bliss of nirvāṇa, should not show contempt for or disparage those who have gone forth from home into homelessness, shaved off their hair and beard, and put on saffron robes.

Among the shameless śramaṇas of broken moral discipline, who have not become vessels for the noble Dharma of the three vehicles, who themselves cling fervently to the evil of wrong views and cause others too to become attached to the evil of wrong views, there are those who say, "There are no virtuous kṣatriyas, no śramaṇas, brahmins, ministers, householders, merchants, vaiśyas, śūdras, men or women." There are those who say, "In the world there is no father and no mother," and so on up to, "there is no virtue and no non-virtue, no fruit of karmic maturation, and no attainment of the fruit of the noble path." There are those who say, "No phenomena arise from causes." There are those who hold that the form realm is permanent and not subject to destruction, and those who hold that the formless realm is permanent and not subject to destruction. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained through the ascetic observances of the non-Buddhists.

Some hold that ultimate purity is attained through the Śrāvaka Vehicle alone, and not through the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle or the Great Vehicle. Having faith and confidence in the Śrāvaka Vehicle, they praise and extol it, explain it and teach it, but they disparage and denigrate the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and the Great Vehicle, revile them, obstruct and hinder them, and do not allow them to be taught.

Some hold that ultimate purity is attained through the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle alone, and not through the Śrāvaka Vehicle or the Great Vehicle. Having faith and confidence in the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, they extol and praise it, explain it and teach it, but they disparage and denigrate the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Great Vehicle, revile them, obstruct and hinder them, and do not allow them to be taught.

Some hold that ultimate purity is attained through the Great Vehicle alone, and not through the Śrāvaka Vehicle or the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Because they themselves have faith and confidence in the Great Vehicle, they lead others too to have faith and confidence in it. Because they themselves serve and honor it, they bring others too to serve and honor it. Because they themselves extol and praise it, they have others too extol and praise it. Because they themselves copy it into volumes, they have others too copy it into volumes. Because they themselves read and recite it, they have others too read and recite it. Because they themselves listen to it and retain it, they have others too listen to it and retain it. Because they themselves reflect on it and bring it to mind, they have others too reflect on it and bring it to mind. To all people, whether they have become vessels for the Dharma or not, they explain and teach the Great Vehicle, the ultimate, extremely profound and subtle. But they disparage and denigrate the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, revile them, obstruct and hinder them, and do not allow them to be taught. Because they themselves lack faith in them, they obstruct the arising of faith in others. Because they themselves do not serve them, they obstruct others from serving them. Because they themselves do not praise them, they obstruct others from praising them. Because they themselves do not copy them into volumes, they obstruct others from copying them into volumes. Because they themselves do not read, recite, listen to, retain, understand or bring them to mind, they obstruct others from reading, reciting, listening to, retaining, reflecting on and bringing them to mind. Thus there are also those who do not teach the Dharma of the two vehicles.

Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by practicing generosity alone, and not through moral discipline, nor through patience, and so on up to wisdom.

Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by moral discipline alone, and not through generosity, nor through patience, and so on up to wisdom. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by patience alone, and not through generosity, nor through moral discipline, and so on up to wisdom. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by diligence alone, and not through generosity, nor through moral discipline, and so on up to wisdom. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by meditative concentration alone, and not through generosity, nor through moral discipline, and so on up to wisdom. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by wisdom alone, and not through generosity, nor through moral discipline, and so on up to meditative concentration. Some hold that ultimate purity is attained by training in and knowing the many different worldly arts and crafts. Some hold, and teach, that ultimate purity is attained through many kinds of ascetic observances, such as leaping from cliffs, leaping into smoldering embers, and fasting.

Son of noble family, in this way that monk of corrupt moral discipline, who engages in evil and is not a vessel for the Dharma, deceives with all manner of lies all those who are vessels for the true Dharma, and leads many people to embrace wrong views. Thus, through the power of their perverse and wrong views, they corrupt those of perfectly pure faith, from virtuous kṣatriyas to vaiśyas, śūdras, men, women and others, and ruin their moral discipline, their learning, their generosity and their wisdom as well. They turn them all into outcastes (caṇḍālas), from outcaste kṣatriyas to outcaste vaiśyas. These are therefore not vessels for the Dharma. The monks of corrupt moral discipline and the outcaste kṣatriyas and the rest, teacher together with disciples, sever their roots of virtue, and so they fall even as far as the hell of Unceasing Torment.

Son of noble family, a human corpse, bloated, putrid and decaying, is repugnant to all beings; its stench, the moment it reaches the nose, comes close to destroying one, and whoever is smeared with it reeks of it. In just the same way, if virtuous kṣatriyas, and so on up to virtuous śūdras, attend upon, keep company with, dwell together with, or carry out activities together with one of corrupt moral discipline, who engages in evil and is not a vessel for the Dharma, they are at once fouled with the filth and stench of wrong views. Son of noble family, just so, the virtuous kṣatriyas, and so on up to the virtuous śūdras, lose all their faith, moral discipline, learning, generosity and wisdom, and become outcastes. And so, teacher together with disciples, having severed their roots of virtue, they fall even as far as the hell of Unceasing Torment.

The sixth fascicle.

Son of noble family, see how in this way countless people, kṣatriyas and others, by relying on a monk of corrupt moral discipline, who engages in evil and is not a vessel for the Dharma, lose all virtuous qualities and fall even as far as the hell of Unceasing Torment. Therefore, those who wish for anything from rebirth as gods in the higher realms up to the bliss of nirvāṇa should attend upon, make offerings to, and show reverence to the śramaṇa who is a victor of the path, and request to hear the sūtras of the Dharma of the three vehicles. Furthermore, they should seek a śramaṇa who teaches the path and a śramaṇa who lives by the path. If there is no śramaṇa abiding in those three paths, they should seek even a śramaṇa who corrupts the path. Although such a one's moral discipline is corrupt, if he possesses right view, faith and superior conduct, attend upon him and make offerings to him, and ask him the meaning of the three vehicles. But to one whose superior conduct, faith and view are all corrupt, one should not offer service and offerings.

As for those of corrupt moral discipline: although their moral discipline is corrupt, they do not hold wrong views, and since they possess faith, superior conduct and view, one should go wherever they are and request and listen to the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and the Dharma of the Great Vehicle, and one should not despise or disparage them. Even though one has faith in any one of the three vehicles, makes aspirations, undertakes diligence and trains accordingly, one should not despise or disparage the other vehicles either.

Whoever despises or disparages even so much as a single verse of any one of the three vehicles, do not serve him, do not attend upon him, do not keep company with him, do not dwell together with him, and do not join with him in communal acts. Whoever serves him, attends upon him, keeps company with him, dwells together with him, or carries out activities together with him will without doubt fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment.

Therefore, son of noble family, those who abide in any one of the three vehicles, who aspire to pass beyond saṃsāra and to supreme bliss, and who are weary of suffering, should have faith in and deeply revere all the true Dharma spoken by the Tathāgata: what was taught on the basis of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, what was taught on the basis of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and what was taught on the basis of the Great Vehicle. Do not scorn or denigrate it, and do not create hindrances or obstacles to it, not even, at the very least, an obstacle to a single verse. Always, with reverence, make a firm commitment to reading, reciting and listening, and come to understand it. If someone scorns and denigrates the Dharma of any one of the three vehicles, do not dwell together with him, not even for a single day, and do not serve him or listen to the Dharma from him. Any sentient being who scorns and denigrates even one of the three vehicles, or who relies on a person who scorns and denigrates the three vehicles, questioning him and listening to him, will by that cause and that condition fall into the great hell of Unceasing Torment and experience great suffering, and it will be extremely hard to be freed.

Why is that? Son of noble family, in times past, when I was practicing the conduct of a bodhisattva and was earnestly striving to seek unsurpassed wisdom, at times, out of longing for the true Dharma belonging to the Śrāvaka Vehicle, even for the sake of a single verse at the very least, I gave away the parts of my body: my hands, my feet, my flesh, my blood, my skin, my bones, my head, my eyes, my marrow, and even my brain. At times, out of longing for the true Dharma taught on the basis of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, even for the sake of a single verse at the very least, I gave away the parts of my body: my hands, my feet, my flesh, my blood, my skin, my bones, my head, my eyes, my marrow, and even my brain. At times, out of longing for the true Dharma taught on the basis of the Great Vehicle, even for the sake of a single verse at the very least, I gave away the parts of my body: my hands, my feet, my flesh, my blood, my skin, my bones, my head, my eyes, my marrow, and even my brain.

Practicing such austerities, whenever I found even a single verse of the three vehicles, I rejoiced greatly, and with reverence I kept it, held it in memory, and practiced it just as it had been taught, without abandoning it in the least. In this way, for countless eons, I practiced every kind of extremely difficult deed, and only then, having performed the final deed, did I attain the fruit of unsurpassed wisdom. Moreover, for the sake of the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings, I have taught the true Dharma of the three vehicles, distinguishing them one from another. Therefore, one should not scorn it, denigrate it, hinder it or obstruct it, nor conceal even so much as a single verse of it. Toward it, always with reverence, make a very firm commitment to reading, reciting and listening.

Son of noble family, this true Dharma of the three vehicles has been taught in harmony by all the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, the future and the present, as numerous as the sands of the river Ganges, and all of them bless it with their blessing. It is upheld in order to rescue and deliver all sentient beings from the great suffering of saṃsāra, and to keep the lineage of the Three Jewels unbroken. Therefore everyone should have faith in the true Dharma of the three vehicles and show it reverence. One should not scorn it, denigrate it, hinder it or obstruct it. Whoever causes even a single verse of the true Dharma of the three vehicles to disappear will without doubt fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment.

Furthermore, son of noble family, in the future, in this buddha field, there will be outcaste kṣatriyas, outcaste brahmins, outcaste ministers, outcaste householders, outcaste śramaṇas, outcaste merchants, and outcaste men or women of the vaiśya and śūdra castes. They will live by deceit and guile, foolish and deluded, puffed up with a little knowledge, by nature fierce and wrathful, impatient and harsh, not looking with fear upon the fruit of suffering in the world beyond, delighting in everything from taking life up to wrong views, avarice, envy, meanness and attachment, turning their backs on virtuous spiritual friends and relying on evil friends, not vessels for the noble Dharma of the three vehicles. Having heard a little of the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, they scorn, denigrate, revile, hinder and obstruct the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and the Dharma of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle, which are upheld by all the buddhas, and they do not let them flourish. Some, having heard a little of the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, scorn, denigrate, revile, hinder and obstruct the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Dharma of the unsurpassed vehicle, which are upheld by all the buddhas, and they do not let them flourish. Some, having heard a little of the Dharma of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle, scorn, denigrate, revile, hinder and obstruct the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, which are upheld by all the buddhas, and they do not let them flourish. And for the sake of gain and fame they say, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle. As a companion of the Great Vehicle, I delight in listening to, keeping and holding in memory the Great Vehicle alone. I take no delight in the Dharma of the śrāvakas and of the followers of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. I have no wish to rely on those two vehicles."

Those who thus falsely claim to be followers of the Great Vehicle are seized by delusion about the self, and, having fallen under the sway of pride, they scorn, denigrate, hinder and obstruct the true Dharma of the three vehicles in this way, and do not let it flourish. They also scorn, denigrate and revile persons who train in the three vehicles, and render them powerless.

Son of noble family, all the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, the future and the present, and all the bodhisattva great beings, for the sake of the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings, through the force of great compassion, uphold these two things. What are they? The first is what keeps the lineage of the Three Jewels unbroken: going forth from home into homelessness, shaving off hair and beard, and wearing saffron robes. The second is possessing the true Dharma that has come forth from the three vehicles, such as the Four Noble Truths. These two things are upheld only by the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattva great beings, and not by śrāvakas, pratyekabuddhas and the like. Nor are they upheld by the great Brahmās, numbering hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas, by Śakra, lord of the gods, by universal monarchs who rule the four continents, or by any such beings.

In the future, in this buddha field, outcaste kṣatriyas will contrive to find faults and pretexts against those who have gone forth in my teaching, shaved off their hair and beards, and put on saffron robes. On all manner of grounds they will abuse and revile them, denigrate and humiliate them, beat them, strike them, throw them into prison, seize their means of livelihood, strip them of their Dharma robes, force them back into lay life and make them do all kinds of household work, press them into servitude and punish them, banish them, cut off their food and drink, and go so far as to deprive them of their life faculty. That king, the outcaste kṣatriya, having thus become foolish and deluded by nature and seized by the power of pride, reviles, humiliates, harshly punishes and otherwise mistreats the disciples who are upheld by the power of the compassion of the buddhas and bodhisattvas. He also scorns and denigrates the extremely profound Dharma that all the buddhas and bodhisattvas uphold through the force of their great compassion, and he corrupts it. He hinders and obstructs the true Dharma of the three vehicles upheld by all the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the three times and by all the bodhisattvas, and does not let it flourish.

Thus those, from outcaste kṣatriyas to outcaste vaiśyas and śūdras, who are foolish and deluded by nature and seized by pride, and who claim, "We are followers of the Great Vehicle," have not become vessels even for the Śrāvaka Vehicle or the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, let alone vessels for the Dharma of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle. Out of desire for gain and fame, honor and offerings, they deceive all foolish and deluded worldly people, saying, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," and they scorn and denigrate the true Dharma of the Tathāgata's two vehicles. Such foolish and deluded persons, living by deceit and guile, seized by pride, and driven by avarice, envy and attachment, by that cause and that condition corrupt my Dharma eye and swiftly bring it to extinction. In doing so they commit a great wrong that violates the sacred commitment of all the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the three times. They commit a great wrong that violates the sacred commitment of all the bodhisattvas of the three times. They commit the evil of a great wrong that violates the sacred commitment of all the śrāvakas of the three times, and before long all their limbs will waste away and they will be struck by many kinds of grave and virulent disease.

All these, from outcaste kṣatriyas to vaiśyas and śūdras, outcaste men or women, who commit evil deeds, have, under the sway of perverse views, severed all their roots of virtue. So even if at other times they have amassed much merit through generosity, in the future they will be born in the realm of hungry ghosts or of animals, and will not experience the happiness of wealth. With that very body they cannot generate even a small root of virtue for birth in the form and formless realms, let alone produce the seed of the roots of virtue of the śrāvakas, of the pratyekabuddhas, or of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle, the spontaneously accomplished wisdom of omniscience. Moreover, their tongues will constantly be afflicted by disease; for many days and nights their tongues will be mute and unable to speak; they will undergo the great suffering of utterly unbearable sickness and fever; and when they die and pass on, they will fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment.

Therefore, for the long-lasting benefit and happiness of those men and women, from virtuous kṣatriyas to vaiśyas, śūdras and the rest, the Tathāgata has said again and again: "Do not harm, abuse or revile, or otherwise mistreat, those who have shaved their hair and beards, put on the patched saffron robe, and gone forth in my teaching. Do not scorn, denigrate, revile, hinder or obstruct the true Dharma of the three vehicles that I have taught." If anyone deliberately contravenes what I have taught, the ripening of that evil is just as has been explained at length.

Why is that? Whoever has gone for refuge in my teaching, shaved hair and beard, put on saffron robes, and bears the appearance of one gone forth, is upheld by the blessing of the great compassion of all the buddhas, the blessed ones, of the past, the future and the present, and of all the bodhisattvas. One who has shaved hair and beard, put on saffron robes, and bears the deportment of one gone forth, is the victory banner of the liberation of the noble ones. He is the victory banner of the taste of the Dharma that all śrāvakas enjoy through liberation. He is the victory banner of the taste of the Dharma that all followers of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle enjoy through liberation. He is the victory banner of the taste of the Dharma that all followers of the Great Vehicle enjoy through liberation. He is the true Dharma of the three vehicles taught by the Tathāgata. He is upheld by the blessing of the great compassion of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas of the three times. He is the abode of the liberation of the noble ones. He is the abode of the taste of the Dharma that all followers of the Śrāvaka Vehicle enjoy through liberation. He is the abode of the taste of the Dharma that all followers of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle enjoy through liberation. He is the abode of the taste of the Dharma that all followers of the Great Vehicle enjoy through liberation.

Therefore, son of noble family, those who seek liberation should serve, make offerings to and honor any person who has gone for refuge in my teaching, shaved hair and beard, put on saffron robes, and gone forth.

In the past I had faith in the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. When I myself listened to it and held it in memory, I had others listen to it and hold it as well. When I myself read and recited it, I had others read and recite it as well.

When I myself copied it into volumes, I had others copy it into volumes as well. When I myself practiced generosity, I had others practice generosity as well. When I myself taught and explained it, I had others teach and explain it as well, bring it to mind, apply themselves to it earnestly, and spread it widely. Just so, having faith in and confidence in the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, when I myself listened to it and held it in memory, I had others listen to it and hold it as well. When I myself read and recited it, I had others read and recite it as well. When I myself copied it into volumes, I had others copy it into volumes as well. When I myself practiced generosity, I had others practice generosity as well. When I myself taught and explained it, I had others teach and explain it as well, bring it to mind, apply themselves to it earnestly, and spread it widely. In the same way, having faith in and confidence in the Great Vehicle, when I myself listened to it and held it in memory, I had others listen to it and hold it as well. When I myself read and recited it, I had others read and recite it as well. When I myself copied it into volumes, I had others copy it into volumes as well. When I myself practiced generosity, I had others practice generosity as well. When I myself taught and explained it, I had others teach and explain it as well, bring it to mind, apply themselves to it earnestly, and spread it widely. If someone has not become a vessel, then he does not listen himself and does not have others listen, and so on; the text is to be expanded just as it was explained before.

One should keep far away from all evil things, give up evil companions, rely on virtuous spiritual friends, strive earnestly in the six perfections, again and again regret and confess all non-virtuous deeds, and make genuine aspirations in keeping with one's inclination. Whoever does this may, in this very body, become a vessel for the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and may also reach the point of not turning back from the lineage of the pratyekabuddhas, and of not turning back from the lineage of the Great Vehicle. Therefore one should train in and apply oneself earnestly to all three vehicles, and should not, out of pride, claim to be a follower of the Great Vehicle while scorning and denigrating the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. What I said before, only to those who had become vessels for the Dharma of the Great Vehicle and who practiced steadfastly, namely, "By cultivating the Great Vehicle alone one will reach the final state," does not contradict what is said here.

Then, in order to restate these points clearly, the Blessed One spoke these verses:

"Before the great maṇḍala of the assembly,
Vajragarbha asked me:
'Why do you urge that offerings be made
To monks whose moral discipline is corrupt?

Though they have lost every quality of training,
Are foolish and hold wrong views,
Are not vessels for the Dharma and corrupt the path,
You do not permit them to be reviled or punished.

You said that whoever hears from them
The true Dharma of the three vehicles explained,
Which genuinely liberates, like excellent medicine,
Will attain the peace of nirvāṇa.

Why is it that in other sūtras
One is freed by the Great Vehicle alone,
Giving up training in the Dharma of two vehicles,
Yet here the three vehicles are taught?

O you who love all sentient beings,
Who have given up every deed of wrong view,
For the sake of benefit and happiness,
Please free us and cut through our doubt.'

For the benefit of kings of the kṣatriya caste,
For the happiness of all down to vaiśyas and śūdras,
I do not permit harm to be done to monks:
That would be the evil of a great wrong.

To shave the beard and wear saffron robes
Is the Dharma banner of the buddhas,
The eye of the path of liberation,
Blessed by all the buddhas.

Though they have wholly fallen from much of their conduct,
They have not wholly abandoned liberation.
By giving up the evil of wrong views,
They will swiftly attain nirvāṇa.

Just as the finest medicine, even when spoiled,
Still greatly helps against every illness,
So too even one whose conduct is corrupt
Can bring others' suffering to rest.

In formal acts of the Saṅgha and in the poṣadha,
I do not permit that śramaṇa to take his seat;
Yet he is entitled to teach the true Dharma to others,
And both of them will gain great merit.

One who has faith in and turns toward the Three Jewels,
And holds me to be the great Teacher,
Will give up much non-virtue and evil;
He is superior to a host of non-Buddhists (tīrthikas).

Just as the merchants wandering on the island of the rākṣasīs,
Fearful and terrified,
Each grasped a single hair of the horse,
And crossed the ocean, free from harm,

So one whose moral discipline is corrupt,
Giving up non-virtue and wrong views,
Through the power of faith as the single cause,
Will be freed from the rākṣasas of the afflictions.

If one guards the banner of liberation
Blessed by many buddhas,
And does no harm to those of corrupt discipline,
One will avoid the heavy evil of wrongdoing.

One who aspires to great merit,
Who delights in genuine liberation,
And protects vessels and non-vessels alike,
Will find liberation not hard to attain.

The fool who in his pride claims
The Great Vehicle has no power of understanding.
If he is ignorant of the Dharma of the two vehicles,
How could he come to know the Great Vehicle?

For example, if the eyeball is damaged,
One will not see the many kinds of color;
Just so, if faith has been lost and is absent,
One cannot know the Great Vehicle.

If one lacks the strength to drink even a trickle of water,
How could one drink up the great ocean?
If one cannot grow accustomed even to the two vehicles,
How could one train in the Great Vehicle?

Through having faith in the Dharma of the two vehicles,
One comes to have faith in the Great Vehicle.
Without faith in the Dharma of the Great Vehicle,
Merely speaking words does not make it real.

Inwardly holding firmly to annihilation,
They claim to belong to the Great Vehicle.
They do not guard against the three kinds of non-virtuous deeds,
And they corrupt and destroy my true Dharma.

When such persons pass from this life,
They will certainly fall into the Avīci hell.
Therefore teach those whose faculties are firm,
And not those who have not become vessels.

Those without a mind of loving-kindness, proud and arrogant,
Fierce and harsh, of feeble faculties,
Are holders of the view of annihilation:
So should the wise know them to be.

They are not vessels for the śrāvakas,
For the pratyekabuddhas,
Or for the Great Vehicle.

Because they revile and denigrate all the buddhas,
They will certainly fall into the Avīci hell.

Delighting in company, guarding moral discipline,
Relying on a master, and fearing suffering:
Let the wise understand
That this is the Śrāvaka Vehicle.

Delighting in giving, contemplating the cessation of birth,
Delighting in remote dwelling places:
Let the wise understand
That this is the vehicle of the pratyekabuddhas.

Endowed with every root of virtue,
With a mind of loving-kindness and compassion,
Always delighting in working for others' benefit:
That is the Great Vehicle.

Giving up body and life to guard moral discipline,
Neither hurting nor harming sentient beings,
Striving in the Dharma of emptiness:
Know this to be the Great Vehicle.

With a mind patient toward all phenomena,
Speaking gentle words, free of meanness,
Ever delighting and rejoicing in the Dharma:
That too is the Great Vehicle.

Toward all, vessels for the Dharma and non-vessels alike,
To work for their benefit with an even intention,
Unstained by worldly concerns:
Know that too to be the Great Vehicle.

Therefore the wise and intelligent
Should develop great faith in the three vehicles.
If they do no harm to the Saṅgha,
They will swiftly attain unsurpassed awakening.

Furthermore, son of noble family, if virtuous kṣatriyas, virtuous brahmins, virtuous ministers, virtuous householders, virtuous merchants, virtuous śramaṇas, and men or women of the vaiśya and śūdra castes and so on possess the ten wheels of abiding and practice, then in this very body they will not turn back or fall away from the lineage of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and in this very life they will become vessels for the noble Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle; but they will not become vessels for the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle or the Great Vehicle. What are the ten? They are these. First, with faith of pure intention, being convinced that the fruits of virtuous and non-virtuous deeds exist. Second, having conscience and shame, and so completely giving up non-virtuous companions and all wrong views. Third, abiding thoroughly in the vows, and completely giving up everything from taking life up to drinking liquor that causes heedlessness. Fourth, abiding in a mind of loving-kindness, and completely giving up all hatred and anger. Fifth, abiding in a mind of compassion, and rescuing all sentient beings in distress. Sixth, dwelling happily in a mind of joy, and completely giving up the four non-virtuous deeds of speech. Seventh, abiding in a mind of equanimity, and completely giving up all attachment, avarice and envy. Eighth, turning toward what is genuine, completely giving up all deceptive omens, good and bad, and not relying on perverse non-Buddhists. Ninth, having diligence with intense exertion, and applying oneself earnestly to all virtuous qualities. Tenth, always delighting in the wilderness, and seeking the meaning of the Dharma with enthusiasm and without weariness.

Son of noble family, if those men and women, from virtuous kṣatriyas to vaiśyas, śūdras and the rest, possess these ten wheels of abiding and practice, then in this very life they will not turn back or fall away from the lineage of the followers of the Śrāvaka Vehicle; in this very life the noble Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle will become manifest to them, and they will become vessels for the noble Dharma of the śrāvakas. But the noble Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and of the Great Vehicle will not become manifest to them, nor will they become vessels for the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and the Great Vehicle. By the same pattern, the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and the Great Vehicle should also be understood. Son of noble family, these ten wheels of abiding and practice are held in common by all śrāvakas, pratyekabuddhas, bodhisattvas, and buddhas, the blessed ones.

Furthermore, son of noble family, there are ten wheels of abiding and practice that are not shared with the śrāvakas but are shared with pratyekabuddhas, bodhisattvas, and buddhas, the blessed ones. If men and women, from virtuous kṣatriyas to vaiśyas, śūdras and the rest, possess these ten wheels of abiding and practice, then in this very life they will not fall away or turn back from the lineage of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and in this very body they will come to know the noble Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and become vessels for the noble Dharma of the pratyekabuddhas. What are the ten? They are these: purifying the deeds of body, speech and mind; having conscience and shame, growing weary of one's own body and developing great fear of the five appropriated aggregates; seeing that the desires of saṃsāra are exceedingly hard to cross; always delighting in solitary places and completely giving up busyness; delighting in remote wilderness and not speaking of the faults of others; restraining the senses and, with a mind always at peace, engaging in meditative absorption; being skilled in dependent origination and so examining cause and effect thoroughly; always delighting in resting evenly in meditative concentration; and being skilled in completely stopping the active afflictions.

Son of noble family, if those men and women, from virtuous kṣatriyas to vaiśyas, śūdras and the rest, possess these ten wheels of abiding and practice, then in this very life they will not turn back or fall away from the lineage of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle; in this very life the noble Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle will become manifest to them, and they will become vessels for the noble Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

Son of noble family, these are called the wheels of abiding and practice of all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas. If all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas abide in this wheel, they will swiftly cross the ocean of the three realms of existence and arrive at the great city of nirvāṇa.

Son of noble family, as to the meaning of the words "wheel of abiding and practice": "abiding" means that which involves grasping, that which involves a basis taken as "mine," that which involves gathering together, and that which involves connecting. "Practice" means the practice of the aggregates, the practice of the elements, the practice of the sense sources, and practice that involves connection.

"Wheel" means the wheel of training and of commitment. For example, a wheel-turning monarch makes chief use of a chariot wheel, or of that wheel.
