In the same way, all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas rely on this wheel as they seek the path to nirvāṇa. Therefore these two kinds are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Why is that? Because they rely on a low and feeble practice, they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle.

Weary and afraid of the practice of the aggregates, which involves grasping, they seek liberation from all suffering for themselves alone, and do not strive to liberate all sentient beings; therefore they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Weary and afraid of the practice of the elements, which involves a basis, they seek liberation from all suffering for themselves, and do not strive to liberate all sentient beings; therefore they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Weary and afraid of the practice of the sense sources, which come together as formations and involve gathering, they seek liberation from all suffering for themselves, and do not strive to liberate all sentient beings; therefore they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle.

In the practice that involves connection, they abide and rely through the force of connection, and having abided and relied, they build up abiding and reliance. They take no delight in gathering all sentient beings to themselves and lack loving-kindness and compassion; because they are bound up with connection, they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Even when they see others undergoing much suffering, they leave them be and are not stirred to weariness, seeking liberation for themselves alone; therefore they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Having cut off the head of their own afflictions, they have no zeal for cutting off the head of the afflictions of all sentient beings; therefore they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Since they do not come down to awakening by the wheel of the Great Vehicle, they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Since they do not come down to awakening by the wheel of great light, they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle. Since they delight in and strive for going alone, without companions, to the city of nirvāṇa, they are not vessels for the Great Vehicle.

Son of noble family, there are beings who have not applied themselves to the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and have not taken up diligence; their faculties are not fully ripened, their faculties are weak, and their diligence is slight. If the very profound sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle is taught to them, both the one who explains and the one who listens incur grave wrongdoing and evil, and that is an offense against all the buddhas.

Why is that? When beings who have not applied themselves to the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, who have not taken up diligence, whose faculties are weak and whose diligence is slight, hear the very profound sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle, such beings are truly foolish and deluded. Though they claim to understand, they fall into the extreme of annihilation and into wrong perception. They teach that there is no cause, and hold that the results of actions do not exist, and so hold that all the good that is done and all the evil that is done amount to nothing. They speak falsely about the Great Vehicle and corrupt my sacred Dharma. They declare what is not the Dharma to be the Dharma, and the Dharma to be not the Dharma. They declare those who are not ascetics to be ascetics, and true ascetics to be no ascetics. They declare what is not the Vinaya to be the Vinaya, and the true Vinaya to be not the Vinaya. Foolish, deluded by nature, in the grip of pride, wholly given over to jealousy and avarice, they praise, protect and spread the Dharma of the Great Vehicle, yet they scorn and disparage the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and do not spread it.

Such a person, relying on the Śrāvaka Vehicle, the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and the unsurpassed true vehicle, goes forth from home into homelessness and receives full ordination, yet does not truly understand, just as it is, what it is to become a monk, nor truly understand, just as it is, the cause that gathers all wholesome qualities. And so, toward my disciples, whether they have become vessels of the Dharma or have not, whether they are striving as those still in training or as those beyond training, or have attained the fruit of the final stage, and toward good people who keep moral discipline, whose discipline has lapsed, or who have no discipline, such people revile and abuse them in all manner of ways, do them harm, steal their robes and alms bowls, refuse them the use of the requisites of life, and even seize them and put them in bonds.

Those who thus regard all causes and results as nonexistent and proclaim annihilation, though they dwell among humans, are truly called rākṣasas. In the future, for countless great eons, it will be hard for them to obtain a human body. It is easier for a being to undergo immeasurable suffering in hell than to hold the view of annihilation among humans.

Such a foolish person, before long, will have the limbs of his body cut to pieces; for many days his tongue will be mute and he will be unable to speak; and he will undergo the great suffering of utterly unbearable sickness and fever. When he dies he will without doubt fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment, and he will revolve through all the lower realms like a wheel, taking on much suffering, with no time of release. Even for a hundred thousand eons it will be hard for him to obtain a human body.

Even if, after immeasurable and countless eons have passed, he does obtain a human body, he will be born in a world realm of the five degenerations, where no buddha dwells. He will be blind from birth, deaf, mute, or without a tongue; tormented by diseases of every kind; short of body and ugly, so that no one finds him pleasing; unskilled in speech, his words unpleasant to the ear; his mind constantly befuddled, understanding nothing; born into a poor and destitute family, obtaining nothing at all; never meeting a spiritual friend who is virtuous; delighting in following evil companions and doing evil deeds; attached to wrong views and delighting in committing the evils of immediate retribution. Because of this he will fall once again into the hell of Unceasing Torment, and it will be very hard for him to escape from the lower realms.

Such a foolish and deluded person, who proclaims annihilation and cessation, corrupts my sacred Dharma, and then zealously punishes my disciples, whether they keep moral discipline, have let their discipline lapse, or have no discipline, and does not let them practice in peace on the side of virtue. By that cause and that condition he will fall into the lower realms for a hundred thousand eons, going from darkness into darkness, with no time of release.

The evil of that being ripens in this way because, without having applied himself to listening to the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, he strives to listen to and apply himself to the very profound sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle. Since such a foolish and deluded person, who proclaims the view of annihilation, will find it hard to obtain even the body of the lowest and most base of humans, what need is there to speak of his becoming a vessel for the noble Dharma? Since he will not attain even the nirvāṇa of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, what need is there to speak of his attaining the very profound, unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening? The fault of that being is that he entered into the Dharma of the Great Vehicle without having trained in the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

Son of noble family, it is like this. If ghee and milk are poured into an unfired clay pot, it soaks them all up, and both the vessel and its contents are spoiled. Why is that? Because the vessel is flawed. In the same way, there are beings who have not thoroughly applied themselves to the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, who have not taken up true diligence, whose faculties are not fully ripened, whose faculties are weak and whose diligence is slight. If anyone explains the very profound sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle to them, both the one who explains and the one who listens incur grave wrongdoing and evil, and it becomes the fault of offending all the buddhas, just as was explained at length above.

Again, it is like this. If precious wealth is placed inside a worldly treasury that is leaking, crumbling and falling apart, it will be wasted and lost. In the same way, if the Great Vehicle is explained to beings who scorn and disparage the Dharma of the two vehicles, who have no faith in it and do not train in it diligently, they will not understand it correctly, just as it is. Because of that they will commit evil, and will not know how to bring saṃsāra to its end.

It is like boarding a leaking boat, full of holes, that can be neither rowed nor steered, and setting out on the ocean. In the same way, a being who is under the sway of avarice and jealousy, and who claims, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," without having applied himself to the Dharma of the two vehicles, holds the view of annihilation; proud, with a body that leaks with deceit and guile, he cannot enter the ocean of omniscience.

It is like this: a person who is blind is no one to whom jewels of various kinds can be shown. In the same way, one who is under the sway of pride, heedless, attached to the view of emptiness, and blind because he lacks the eye of wisdom, not having trained in the two vehicles, is no one to whom the jewel-like qualities of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle can be shown.

It is like this: a body that is foul-smelling and rotting will not become fragrant, even if it is anointed with fragrant ointments of every kind. In the same way, beings who are foolish and deluded, in the grip of pride, without faith to exert themselves in the Dharma of the two vehicles, who have not given up killing, and so on up to not having given up wrong view, cannot understand the very profound Dharma, even if they apply themselves to the unsurpassed Great Vehicle.

It is like this: even if good seed is sown in a salt marsh and great labor is spent on it, no fruit or harvest will be gained. In the same way, beings who are proud and lazy toward the Dharma of the two vehicles and do not apply themselves to it, who are attached to the five sense pleasures and feel no weariness with them, will achieve nothing at all in that very body, even if they make efforts in the family of the Great Vehicle that are very hard to carry out.

It is like this: when a little sugar is later put into a pot that was earlier used as a vessel for poison, it is unfit for use. In the same way, if the Great Vehicle is taught to a being who does not apply himself to the Dharma of the two vehicles and who teaches that there is no cause, it will not bring benefit to himself or others.

It is like this: if a little poison is later put into a pot that was earlier used as a vessel for sugar, that too is unfit for use. In the same way, if the Great Vehicle is taught to a being who has not applied himself to the Dharma of the two vehicles, both will decline and go astray.

It is like this: just as a madman whose mind is disturbed cannot tell one sound from another, even if all kinds of music are played before him, so too, there are beings who have not thoroughly applied themselves to the Dharma of the two vehicles, whose minds are corrupted by fierce afflictions such as attachment, aversion and delusion, who hold that there is no cause, who proclaim annihilation and nonexistence, and whose faculties are unripened. Even if the Great Vehicle is explained to them for a very long time, they cannot understand or realize it.

It is like this: whoever goes to the front line of battle without armor and carrying no weapons will without doubt be wounded and suffer. In the same way, if the Great Vehicle is explained to beings who have not applied themselves to the Dharma of the two vehicles, whose wisdom is weak, and who have not become fit in faculties or as vessels, then because they will without doubt grasp it wrongly, there will be no end to the evil deeds of every kind that they commit.

Such a foolish and deluded person, before long, will have the limbs of his body cut to pieces; for many days his tongue will be mute and he will be unable to speak; and he will undergo the great suffering of utterly unbearable sickness and fever. When he dies and passes on, he will without doubt be born in the hell of Unceasing Torment and will circle and wander through the lower realms, just as was explained at length before.

Therefore, son of noble family, the wise should first examine the mind of the other person, and only then teach the Dharma. They should first give rise to a mind of loving-kindness, a mind of compassion, a mind of joy, a mind of benefit, a mind free of laziness, a mind of great patience, a mind free of pride, a mind free of jealousy, a mind free of stinginess, and a mind of samādhi in meditative equipoise, and only then teach the sacred Dharma to the other person. They should not cause the other being, having heard the Dharma, to fall into the great abyss of circling in saṃsāra.

Therefore the Tathāgata, having fully understood the characteristics of the minds of all sentient beings, teaches the sacred Dharma by the wheel of stainless practice without appropriation. The bodhisattva great beings who have donned the great armor also teach the Dharma to others in the same way. Out of loving-kindness and affection they teach the sacred Dharma in order to cut off all afflictions completely, to bring beings across the ocean of the three realms of existence, and to lead beings into the one vehicle, whichever of the three vehicles they have faith in, and swiftly bring them to completion. Never do they cause them to fall into the great abyss of circling in saṃsāra.

Now, why is it called the wheel of stainless practice? Here, "stainless" means this: when they teach the Dharma, it is not because aggregates exist, not because sense sources exist, not because elements exist; not because the desire realm exists, not because the form realm exists, not because the formless realm exists; not because this world exists, not because the world beyond exists; not because formations exist, not because feeling exists, not because perception exists, not because conceptual thought exists, not because awareness exists, not because attention exists; not because ignorance exists, and so on up to not because aging and death exist. They do not teach the sacred Dharma to beings because there is going or because there is no going. Rather, it is with all of these, the aggregates, elements and sense sources, and so on up to going and not going, as explained at length, completely stilled, that they teach the sacred Dharma to beings. That is why it is called "stainless."

"Practice" means this: they teach the Dharma to beings in order to cut off completely dying and passing from here and being born in the beyond. Teaching the Dharma to beings in order to cut off completely, in full, the aggregates, elements and sense sources, and likewise up to going and not going, is called "practice."

"Wheel" means this: just as the light of the full moon, very cool and clear, appears unobstructed in the sky and so becomes an unobstructed field of activity everywhere, so too the deeds of the tathāgatas and bodhisattvas, done through the three wheels of complete victory, namely all their miraculous powers, their higher knowledges that reveal what is known, and their instruction in what is to be trained in, reach without obstruction throughout every world realm, for the benefit and happiness of all beings who have become vessels. Not mingled with beings going for refuge elsewhere, not mingled with any of the worldly Lokāyatas, and not mingled with any of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, they cut off all the suffering of saṃsāra for beings and bring them to attain the supremely blissful nirvāṇa of awakening. That is called "wheel." In this way, these are called the wheel of stainless practice of the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Now, why is it called the wheel of practice without appropriation? Unobstructed with regard to all phenomena, just as the disc of the sun shines everywhere, they teach the sacred Dharma without attachment, in accordance with the faculties and the capacity as vessels of those of the three vehicles. When the Tathāgata teaches the Dharma to beings, just as space has no characteristics of distinction, so he teaches the sacred Dharma to beings without attachment, sustained by immeasurable samādhis and by the mastery and array of the liberations. The bodhisattva great beings who have donned the great armor also teach the Dharma to others in the same way. Their teaching of the Dharma is neither of existence nor of nonexistence.

Form itself is not empty; what is other than form is not empty; and so on up to consciousness itself is not empty; what is other than consciousness is not empty. The eye itself is not empty; what is other than the eye is not empty; and so on up to the mind itself is not empty; what is other than the mind is not empty. Visible form itself is not empty; what is other than visible form is not empty; and so on up to mental phenomena themselves are not empty; what is other than mental phenomena is not empty. Eye consciousness itself is not empty; what is other than eye consciousness is not empty; and so on up to mind consciousness itself is not empty; what is other than mind consciousness is not empty.

The desire realm itself is not empty; what is other than the desire realm is not empty; and so on up to the sphere of infinite space itself is not empty; what is other than the sphere of infinite space is not empty. The sphere of infinite consciousness itself is not empty; what is other than the sphere of infinite consciousness is not empty. The sphere of nothingness itself is not empty; what is other than the sphere of nothingness is not empty. The sphere of neither perception nor non-perception itself is not empty; what is other than the sphere of neither perception nor non-perception is not empty.

The four applications of mindfulness themselves are not empty; what is other than the four applications of mindfulness is not empty; and so on up to the eightfold noble path itself is not empty; what is other than the eightfold noble path is not empty.

Dependent origination itself is not empty; what is other than the phenomena of dependent origination is not empty. The three things to be guarded themselves are not empty; what is other than the three things to be guarded is not empty. The four fearlessnesses themselves are not empty; what is other than the four fearlessnesses is not empty. The ten powers themselves are not empty; what is other than the ten powers is not empty. The eighteen unshared qualities themselves are not empty; what is other than the eighteen unshared qualities is not empty. Great loving-kindness, great compassion, great joy and great equanimity themselves are not empty; what is other than great loving-kindness, great compassion, great joy and great equanimity is not empty. Nirvāṇa itself is not empty; what is other than nirvāṇa is not empty. This is called the Middle Way application of the tathāgatas and bodhisattvas in teaching the sacred Dharma to sentient beings.

Son of noble family, in this way the Tathāgata teaches the Dharma to beings by the wheel of stainless practice, and he teaches it at length in the manner set out before, beginning with "just as the light of the full moon, very clear and cool, pervades all of space without obstruction and appears unobstructed in every field of activity." By the wheel of practice without appropriation, too, he teaches the sacred Dharma, unobstructed with regard to all phenomena: just as the light of the sun shines everywhere, he teaches the sacred Dharma without attachment, in accordance with the capacity as vessels and the faculties of those of the three vehicles. When the Tathāgata teaches the Dharma to beings, just as space has no characteristics of distinction, so, sustained by the mastery of display through immeasurable samādhis and by their array, he teaches the sacred Dharma to beings without attachment, and leads them into whichever of the three vehicles suits them.

The bodhisattva great beings who have donned the great armor also teach the Dharma to others in the same way. They cause beings to hear this supreme, sacred, very profound Dharma, and lead them into the one vehicle, whichever of the three vehicles they are inclined toward. They bring their roots of virtue to full ripeness and establish them in ease in the one vehicle. They do not let any kind of evil, nonvirtuous action increase in the abode of saṃsāra, and they make all of them utterly steadfast in nirvāṇa and irreversible.

Son of noble family, the bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma in order to cut off completely the saṃsāra of countless, immeasurable beings. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to cut off their own saṃsāra alone. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others in order to bring immeasurable, countless beings across the four floods. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to bring themselves across the four floods. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others in order to cure the disease of the afflictions of immeasurable, countless beings. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to cure the disease of their own afflictions. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others in order to cut off, without remainder, the continuum of the habitual tendencies of the afflictions in the aggregates of all beings. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to cut off, without remainder, the continuum of the habitual tendencies of the afflictions in their own aggregates alone.

The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others with great compassion as the cause, in order to accomplish the fruit of great compassion. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas do not teach the Dharma to others with great compassion as the cause for the sake of the fruit of great compassion. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others with all beings as their focus. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others without beings as their focus. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others in order to pacify the suffering of all beings. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to pacify their own suffering alone. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others in order to suffuse all beings with the taste of the Dharma. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others in order to suffuse themselves alone with the taste of the Dharma. The bodhisattva great beings teach the Dharma to others so that all beings may obtain the light of the Victor's Dharma. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas teach the Dharma to others so that they themselves alone may obtain the light of the Victor's Dharma.

Son of noble family, in brief, the bodhisattva great beings, through immeasurable vows, teach the Dharma to others in order to dispel completely the great darkness of ignorance of all beings, to dispel completely every great object of fear and everything that brings harm, and so that they may obtain great radiance of light, obtain great renown, and realize correctly, just as it is, the wisdom of omniscience. The śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, through slight vows, teach the Dharma to others in order to dispel their own darkness of ignorance, obtain a little radiance of light, obtain a little renown, and realize correctly, just as it is, a little of the wisdom that comprehends the Dharma.

Son of noble family, the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas have no true focus on others. They have no true compassion for others. They have no truly uncontemptuous attitude toward others. They have no true intention to bring benefit and happiness to others. They have no true intention to untie and free others. They have no true intention to lift others up. They have no true intention to extol and praise others. They do not truly praise others without deceit and guile. They do not truly disregard their own bodies for others and accomplish the benefit and happiness of others. Nor are their actions of body, speech and mind toward others truly free from lapse.

Son of noble family, those who abide in the Great Vehicle truly have nothing whatsoever that they take as their own, and so on, likewise at length, up to: they truly have no actions of body, speech or mind that cause harm to others.

Fascicle seven.

Son of noble family, furthermore, there are beings who are vicious by nature, harsh and rough in their speech, truly foolish, deluded by nature, conceited about their knowledge, who have not given up killing, and so on up to not having given up wrong view. When they see others obtaining gain, honor, offerings and renown, they grow jealous. They constantly seek gain, honor, offerings and renown for themselves and are never satisfied. They praise themselves and disparage others. They do not restrain their own actions of body, speech and mind, and always delight in committing every kind of evil. Their minds are vicious; they have no compassion, no scruple and no shame, and they delight in harming others. They sort fields of merit into good and bad. They delight in finding fault with those who have entered my teaching and gone forth, watching for a chance to harm them. When the slightest fault appears, without examining whether it is true or false, they scorn and disparage them, revile and abuse them. Their minds are untamed, agitated and distracted; they delight in harming those who have gone forth. They give no thought to their own faults and criticize others. When they hear the qualities and praises of the Great Vehicle, the wish arises in them to seek it out and enter it, yet they delight in committing grave misdeeds and evil, and are not at peace at all.

In order to deceive others, they make a show of listening to the Dharma of the Great Vehicle themselves and of having others listen too, a show of reading and reciting it themselves and having others read and recite it too. In order to praise themselves they disparage others. Though such a person praises the Dharma of the Great Vehicle with a show of respect, he has never trained diligently in the domain of the Great Vehicle and has not grasped its meaning. Yet he claims, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," and, in order to seek gain, renown and his own livelihood, he gets others to take him as their refuge and to study under him. He is like a Vinaya holder whose discipline has lapsed and who commits evil: though his own discipline has lapsed and he commits evil, for the sake of gain and renown he urges others to study the Vinaya basket.

Such beings, possessed of deceit and crookedness and cheating by nature, will find it hard to obtain even the lowest of human bodies. They will fall away from and fail to attain even the gods of the higher realms and the nirvāṇa of the two vehicles, so what need is there to speak of attaining the Great Vehicle? That is impossible. When he dies and passes on, he will fall into hell, with no time of release. Therefore the wise should not keep company with him.

Without embarrassment or shame, for the sake of gain and honor, he roars a lion's roar in the midst of a large assembly, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," and so he associates with and befriends those who are foolish and deluded. That is like a donkey that puts on a lion's skin and declares, "I am a lion." When a man sees it from afar, he takes it to be a real lion. But then, when it gives voice, everyone knows it is a donkey, and they all spit at it, each one saying, "Look, this is just a wretched donkey that eats filth!" and they abuse it with all kinds of foul words and go away.

In the same way, the person I have described, who delights in practicing the ten paths of nonvirtuous action, has burned the seed of all gods and humans. Since he has turned back even from the Dharma of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles, what need is there to speak of his becoming a vessel for the Great Vehicle? Such a foolish and deluded person, in the grip of pride, who deceitfully tells others, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," and gathers gain and honor, is like a foolish man without hands or feet who enters battle to overcome the enemy: he achieves nothing but his own distress. In the same way, the one who deceitfully claims to be a follower of the Great Vehicle, lacking the hands of faith and the feet of moral discipline, does not weigh his own actions and conduct, and is bent on attacking the great battle of the afflictions; it comes to nothing for him but distress.

That person, who does not restrain the three doors and commits nothing but evil, though he claims, "I am a follower of the Great Vehicle," is not a vessel for the Dharma of the three vehicles. So there is no possibility that he will conquer the deeply entrenched enemy of the afflictions of all beings, or teach the eight branches of the noble path to all beings, or establish them in the fearless city of nirvāṇa.

Why is that? Son of noble family, what is called the Great Vehicle is this: to keep excellent moral discipline in perfect purity; to be endowed with the practice of excellent and supreme virtue; to rely on excellent embarrassment and shame; to look closely at the fruit of suffering in the next world and fear it; to give up completely all nonvirtuous phenomena and take joy in applying oneself to all virtuous phenomena; to pervade all beings with loving-kindness and compassion; to strive to bring about the benefit and happiness of all beings; to rescue and deliver all beings from the suffering of saṃsāra; and, without regard for one's own bodily comfort, to bestow benefit and happiness on all beings. These are called abiding in the Great Vehicle.

Son of noble family, if you ask what characteristics one must possess to be called of the Śrāvaka Vehicle: there are those who abide happily in right mindfulness and, because they aspire to settle evenly in meditative absorption, are always diligent; who have given up deceit and guile and have faith in the fruits of karma; who are not attached to the five sense pleasures and are not tainted by the eight worldly concerns; who are intensely diligent in virtue, as though the crown of their head were on fire; who thoroughly examine the aggregates, the elements, and the sense sources; and who always aspire to abide happily in the lineage of the noble ones. Possessing these characteristics is called the Śrāvaka Vehicle. If such a person does not become even a vessel for the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, what need is there to speak of becoming a vessel for the Great Vehicle?

Son of noble family, if you ask what characteristics one must possess to be called of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle: there are beings who possess all those qualities of the śrāvakas and, in addition, abide in repeatedly contemplating the five appropriated aggregates as impermanent, abide in repeatedly contemplating their arising and cessation, and thoroughly understand that phenomena arising in dependence are subject to cessation. Possessing these characteristics is called the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Such a being is not a vessel for the Dharma of the Great Vehicle.

Then, in order to restate these points, the Blessed One spoke in verse:

If virtuous kings of the kṣatriya caste,
Down to vaiśyas and śūdras who are exceedingly virtuous,
Cultivate the ten wheels, beginning with faith,
They become vessels for the Śrāvaka Vehicle.

Those of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle are pure in their three doors,
Possess embarrassment and shame, and fear the aggregates.
Knowing the danger of wrongdoing, they delight in remote dwellings;
With mindfulness they guard their senses, and their minds are calm.
Understanding dependent origination, they cultivate meditative concentration,
And they are skilled in understanding the aggregates, elements, and sense sources.
Through these ten wheels of abiding and conduct,
One becomes a vessel of the supreme vehicle and crosses the ocean of existence.

Those who, by the wheels of two vehicles and not of three,
Strive to free only themselves from the afflictions,
Who do not give up their habitual tendencies and do not liberate beings,
Such persons are not vessels of the Great Vehicle.

The childish, of weak faculties and lazy,
Who do not apply themselves earnestly to the two vehicles,
Will never come to possess the wheels of the Great Vehicle;
They are not vessels of the vast Great Vehicle.

The childish who seek liberation for themselves alone,
Weak in understanding and in conduct, lacking a loving mind,
Attached to the view of annihilation, are headed for the lower realms.
Those who reject the true Dharma and teach what is not Dharma,
Who disparage the two vehicles and abandon their vows,
Who are fully ordained and yet lay claim to the Great Vehicle,
Destroy my Dharma and deceive all beings;
Because of this, it will be hard for them to gain a human body.

If they harm or injure the noble ones who have entered my teaching
And those who wear the saffron robe,
Or revile them, or abuse them, or rob them of their bowls and robes,
They will long be deprived of the happiness of gods and humans.

Therefore, whoever wishes to gain a human body,
To be free of the defect of muteness at the time of death,
And always wishes to meet all the buddhas,
Must at all times protect the teachings of the three vehicles.

One who, among the three vehicles, longs for the supreme vehicle
Is skilled in examining the teachings of the three vehicles;
If with joy and enthusiasm one teaches them extensively to others,
One will surely become a buddha; give up all doubt of this.

Those of corrupt discipline, stingy and proud,
Who praise themselves, disparage others, and lay claim to the Great Vehicle:
Shun them, and rely on the wise.
Then you will surely awaken and pass beyond the three realms.

In accord with whichever of the three vehicles suits each one,
Teach the Dharma of the three vehicles with love and compassion.
If you carry this through to completion, expecting nothing in return,
You will surely awaken; give up all doubt of this.

Knowing the aggregates, elements, and sense sources to be at peace,
Abiding in nothing at all, like space,
Teaching the Dharma and gathering the many beings,
One attains immeasurable wisdom and sublime intelligence.

The wicked who delight in broken discipline,
Who, having heard of the supreme qualities of the Great Vehicle,
Lay claim to the Great Vehicle for the sake of gain and fame,
Are like a donkey wearing a lion's skin.

Today, here, I declare to the entire assembly:
Whoever wishes swiftly for supreme awakening,
Apply yourselves earnestly to the ten virtuous courses of action;
Do not disparage my Dharma, but guard it well.

Formerly, in other sūtras, I said:
"Those who long for great awakening should practice the Great Vehicle;
Abandon the vehicles of the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas!"
That was Dharma taught to a pure assembly.

To those people of intense diligence
Who, having made offerings to countless koṭis of buddhas,
Have abandoned wrongdoing and trained their minds in purity,
I taught a single vehicle, without a second.

This present assembly holds vessels of the three vehicles.
Some possess the qualities of the Śrāvaka Vehicle;
Those daunted by the many undertakings of action
Are not vessels for supreme awakening.
Some delight in solitude and seek awakening for themselves alone;
They too are not vessels for supreme awakening.
Some are fit to be established in supreme wisdom.
Therefore teach the three vehicles according to their inclinations.

Those who wish for liberation through the ascetic practices alone,
If they hear the Great Vehicle explained, will fall into the lower realms.
It is like making one sick with a phlegm disorder drink milk:
For that one it turns to poison and does not cure the disease.
Likewise, those of the Śrāvaka Vehicle who are not fit vessels,
If they hear the Great Vehicle explained, become bewildered,
Proclaim annihilation, and fall into the lower realms.
Therefore I teach the Dharma after examining their faculties.

This concludes the fourth chapter, on abiding and conduct, from the Great Vaipulya Sūtra The Ten Wheels of the Bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha.

When the Blessed One had spoken these verses, within that circle of the assembly there were countless hundreds of thousands of beings who, on hearing the Dharma in the past, had misunderstood it, taking it to mean mere emptiness; they had come to view cause and effect as nonexistent, had completely severed their roots of virtue, and were about to fall into the lower realms. Having heard this sūtra explained, they once again came to possess right view. Then they rose from their seats and, feeling deep shame before the Blessed One, in order to confess individually, they joined their palms, bowed, and prostrated, and said to the Blessed One, "Venerable Blessed One, in the past we generated roots of virtue in the family of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, but before even that Śrāvaka Vehicle had fully matured in us, we heard the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Being confused about that as well, and not having realized it directly, we took the view of emptiness and held that there is no cause and no effect.

For that reason and on that account, we committed countless evil, nonvirtuous actions through body, speech, and mind, and so we were bound to fall into the lower realms. But today, having heard this sūtra explained in the presence of the Blessed One, we have regained right view, and with deep embarrassment and a sense of shame we confess these individually, without concealment. May they thereby be completely purified! From this day on, we wish to guard ourselves carefully so as never to commit any actions that would become obscurations. Blessed One, out of your loving care, please accept us, so that our evil deeds may be completely purified and we may not commit them again. Out of your loving care, please protect us from experiencing the sufferings of the lower realms in the future. We will once again practice and apply ourselves earnestly to the Śrāvaka Vehicle that we practiced before, so, Blessed One, out of your loving care, please teach us."

The Blessed One said, "Excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you confess individually in this way, with embarrassment and a sense of shame. In my teaching there are two kinds of holy beings who do not fall away. The first is one who is pure by nature and from the very beginning commits no wrongdoing. The second is one who, even though wrongdoing has been done, confesses it individually with embarrassment and a sense of shame. These two kinds of holy beings are called those whose aspiration toward my Dharma is utterly pure."

Then the Blessed One taught them the Dharma of the four noble truths in accordance with their inclinations. Among that assembly, some attained the lesser forbearance, some attained the middling forbearance, some attained the highest forbearance, some attained the supreme worldly quality, some attained the fruit of stream-entry, some attained the fruit of once-returner, and some attained the fruit of non-returner. In addition, eighty-four thousand monks exhausted all defilements, their minds were liberated, and they became arhats whose understanding was perfectly pure. Rejoicing, they bowed to the Buddha and sat down again, each on his own seat.

Again, within that circle of the assembly, there were fifty-seven hundred thousand koṭis of niyutas of beings who had mistaken the Dharma they had heard before to mean mere emptiness, had come to view cause and effect as nonexistent, had completely severed their roots of virtue, and were about to fall into the lower realms. Having heard this sūtra explained, they once again found right view. They rose from their seats, touched their heads to the feet of the Blessed One, and, with deep embarrassment and a sense of shame, confessed individually in the presence of the Blessed One. Joining their palms and bowing, they said to the Blessed One:

"Venerable Blessed One, in the past we generated roots of virtue in the family of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, but before we had fully matured even as vessels for the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, we heard the Dharma of the Great Vehicle. Though faith and devotion arose in us, we were blinded by ignorance and did not understand it, so we took the view of emptiness and held that there is no cause and no effect. For that reason and on that account, we committed countless evil, nonvirtuous actions with body, speech, and mind, and so we were bound to experience many kinds of utterly unbearable suffering for countless eons and to fall into the lower realms. But today, having heard the sūtra explained in the presence of the Blessed One, we have regained right view, and, possessed of deep embarrassment and a sense of shame, with minds that conceal nothing, we confess individually. From this day on, we wish to guard ourselves carefully so as never to commit any actions that would become obscurations. Loving Blessed One, please accept us, so that our evil deeds may be completely purified and we may not commit them again. Blessed One, through your compassion, please protect us from experiencing the sufferings of the lower realms in the future. We will once again practice and apply ourselves earnestly to the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle that we practiced before, so, loving Blessed One, please teach us."

The Blessed One said, "Excellent! Excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you confess individually in this way, with embarrassment and a sense of shame. In my teaching there are two kinds of beings who do not fall away. The first is one who is pure by nature and from the very beginning commits no wrongdoing. The second is one who, even though wrongdoing has been done, confesses it individually with embarrassment and a sense of shame. These two kinds of holy beings are called those whose aspiration toward my Dharma is utterly pure."

Then the Blessed One taught them the Dharma of dependent origination in accordance with their inclinations. All of them applied themselves earnestly to the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and all of them gradually perfected the meditative absorption called Focused on the Victory Banner. Becoming irreversible from the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, they rejoiced, bowed to the Blessed One, and sat down again, each on his own seat.

Again, within that circle of the assembly, there were some eighty hundred thousand koṭis of beings who had disparaged the Dharma of the buddhas of the past, or, at the very least, had reviled a single verse of the Buddha's teaching. For that reason and on that account they had fallen into the lower realms and experienced much suffering, and even when they regained a human body, they were mute from birth, stammering and tongue-tied, unable to speak. Having heard this sūtra explained, they found right view. They rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, and, with deep embarrassment and a sense of shame, confessed individually their former evil deeds in the presence of the Blessed One. Joining their palms and bowing, they gazed at the Blessed One without blinking.

The Blessed One blessed all of them so that they were able to speak. Then the Blessed One, though he knew, said to them, "What nonvirtuous actions did you do in the past, that you have become unable to speak within this circle of the assembly?"

They said to the Blessed One, "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Tathāgata Vipaśyin, some of us disparaged the Dharma of the Great Vehicle. Some disparaged the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Some disparaged the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle.

Through disparaging it, even if only a single verse of it, by the force of those nonvirtuous actions we have wandered in saṃsāra for ninety-one eons. We were born again and again among the hell beings, in the animal realm, and in the world of the pretas, where we were mute and stammering, without tongues and unable to speak, and experienced great and utterly unbearable suffering. Only in this life did we obtain a human body, and even so we were mute, stammering, and always afflicted with diseases of the tongue. Now, through the blessing of the Blessed One, we have become able to speak, and we have come to remember every obscuration of nonvirtuous action that we committed in times past."

Among them, some said this: "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Blessed One Śikhin, some of us disparaged the Great Vehicle, and some disparaged the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle," and so on, in the same way as the earlier account, explained in full just as before.

Among them, some said this: "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Tathāgata Viśvabhū, some of us disparaged the true Dharma of the Great Vehicle," and so on, in the same way as the account explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Tathāgata Krakucchanda, some of us disparaged the true Dharma of the Great Vehicle," and so on, in the same way as the account explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Tathāgata Kanakamuni, some of us disparaged the sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle," and so on, in the same way as the account explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "Venerable Blessed One, in the past, during the teaching of the Tathāgata Kāśyapa, some of us disparaged the sacred Dharma of the Great Vehicle. Some disparaged the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Some disparaged the Dharma of the followers of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, even if only a single verse of it. Through that disparagement, by the force of nonvirtuous actions, we have wandered in saṃsāra ever since. We were born again and again among the hell beings, in the animal realm, and in the world of Yama, where we were mute and stammering, without tongues and unable to communicate, and experienced great and utterly unbearable suffering. Only in this life did we obtain a human body, and even so we were mute, stammering, afflicted with diseases of the tongue, and unable to make ourselves understood. Now, through the blessing of the Blessed One, we have at last become able to speak, and we have come to remember every obscuration of nonvirtuous action that we committed in times past. Having heard this sūtra explained in the presence of the Blessed One, we have regained right view, and with deep shame, embarrassment, and minds that conceal nothing, we confess individually. From this day on, we wish to guard ourselves carefully so as never to commit any actions that would become obscurations. Loving Blessed One, please accept us, so that our evil deeds may be completely purified and we may not commit them again. Blessed One, through your compassion, please protect us from experiencing the sufferings of the lower realms in the future as well, and please teach us the sacred Dharma."

The Blessed One said, "Excellent! Excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you confess individually in this way, with embarrassment and a sense of shame. In my teaching there are two who are called holy beings who do not fall away. The first is one who is pure by nature and from the very beginning commits no wrongdoing. The second is one who, even though wrongdoing has been done, confesses it individually with embarrassment and a sense of shame. These two kinds of holy beings are called those whose aspiration toward the Dharma of my teaching is utterly pure."

Then the Blessed One taught and explained to them many kinds of sacred Dharma, in accordance with their inclinations, and led each of them to attain the happiness that delighted them. Rejoicing, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down, each on his own seat.

At that time, within that circle of the assembly, countless hundreds of thousands of śrāvakas and countless hundreds of thousands of koṭis of bodhisattvas, having heard this sūtra explained, remembered whatever obscurations of nonvirtuous action they had committed in the past. They bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, and in the presence of the Blessed One they confessed individually with deep embarrassment and a sense of shame. Joining their palms, they said to the Blessed One with reverence:

"Venerable Blessed One, in the past, within the sacred Dharma of countless buddhas, some of us spoke like this: toward attendants of those buddhas, whether they had become vessels of the Dharma or had not, we were angry and hostile; we reviled and abused them, disparaged and despised them, and mocked and denigrated them in all kinds of ways; we looked for chances to harm them, and we concealed their good qualities and proclaimed their faults. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions, we fell into the lower realms for countless eons and experienced great and utterly unbearable suffering. Later we served and made offerings to countless buddhas. Moreover, we served, made offerings to, and attended upon countless bodhisattva great beings, and before each of those buddhas and each of those bodhisattva great beings we confessed individually, with deep embarrassment and a sense of shame, our obscurations of evil action. From each of those buddhas and each of those bodhisattva great beings we also heard countless Dharma discourses, and having heard them we applied ourselves with great earnestness and practiced countless austerities that are extremely hard to practice. Yet because a remainder of karmic obscuration was left, we did not attain the perfectly blissful nirvāṇa. Nor did we attain the special gateways of meditative absorption or their qualities. Today, having heard this sūtra explained in the presence of the Blessed One, we confess individually, with ever deeper embarrassment, with minds of shame, and with minds that conceal nothing. May these be completely purified! From this day on, we wish to guard ourselves carefully so as never to commit any actions that would become obscurations.

Loving Blessed One, please accept us, so that our evil deeds may be completely purified and we may not commit them again. Blessed One, through your compassion, please protect us from experiencing the sufferings of the lower realms in the future. Through the power of the Blessed One's blessing, please let us swiftly attain the bliss of nirvāṇa in accordance with our inclinations, and attain the special qualities of the gateways of meditative absorption."

Among them, some said this: "In the past, toward attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, we caused injury and harm with all kinds of harsh and cutting words. By the force of those evil, nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Some said this: "In the past, toward attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, we caused injury and harm by beating and striking them. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "In the past we robbed attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, of their Dharma robes and alms bowls. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "In the past we robbed attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, of all kinds of requisites for their livelihood, seized their food and drink, and obstructed them. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "In the past we stripped attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, of their Dharma robes, reduced them to laypeople, and made them do all kinds of work. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "In the past, toward ordained attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, whether they had done wrong or had done no wrong, we tormented them by putting them in iron chains, fetters, and wooden stocks. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we fell into the lower realms for countless eons," and so on, just as was explained in full above.

Among them, some said this: "In the past, toward ordained attendants of a certain Blessed One, whether they were vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, we made them miserable with a domineering mind, through injury and all kinds of harm. By the force of those nonvirtuous actions we experienced great and utterly unbearable suffering for countless eons. Later we served, made offerings to, and pleased countless buddhas. Moreover, we also served, made offerings to, and pleased countless bodhisattva great beings, and before each of those buddhas and each of those bodhisattva great beings we confessed those nonvirtuous actions individually, with deep embarrassment. From each of those buddhas and each of those bodhisattva great beings we also heard countless Dharma discourses, and having heard them we applied ourselves earnestly with strenuous diligence. Yet even though we practiced what is extremely hard to practice, because a remainder of karmic obscuration was left, we have still not attained the bliss of perfectly blissful nirvāṇa. We have not attained the special gateways of the qualities of meditative absorption. Today, having heard this sūtra in the presence of the Blessed One, we have confessed individually, with ever deeper embarrassment and without concealment, and have become completely purified. From this day on, we wish to guard ourselves carefully so as never to commit any actions that would become obscurations. Loving Blessed One, please accept us, so that our evil deeds may be completely purified and we may not commit them again. Blessed One, through your compassion, please protect us from experiencing the sufferings of the lower realms in the future. Through the power of the Blessed One's blessing, please let us swiftly attain the bliss of nirvāṇa, and also attain the special qualities of the gateways of meditative absorption."

Then the Blessed One said to that assembly of śrāvakas and bodhisattvas, "Excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you confess individually in this way, with deep embarrassment. There are two kinds of holy beings who never fall away. The first is one who is pure by nature and from the very beginning commits no wrongdoing. The second is one who, even though wrongdoing has been done, confesses it individually with embarrassment. These two kinds of beings are called those whose aspiration toward my teaching is utterly pure.

"Son of noble family, compared with the evil of harming the ordained attendants of the Buddha's teaching in this way, the deeds of immediate retribution and the near-equivalent evils described above are mere names; they should not even be called evils. If one harms an ordained attendant of the Buddha, that evil is immeasurably, incalculably greater than the evil of the five deeds of immediate retribution described above. Why is this? Even a monk whose moral discipline is broken and who commits evil can still show countless hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of beings the unerring path to nirvāṇa. He becomes a treasury of jewels of great qualities for many beings, and so on, just as was explained in full. What need is there to speak of one who guards moral discipline and practices virtuous dharmas? Therefore, it should be understood that whoever injures or harms a disciple ordained in the Buddha's teaching cuts off the lineage of the Three Jewels. That is to gouge out the Dharma eye of all beings.

It is to ruin the sacred Dharma that I found long ago through practicing extreme austerities. It is to do immense harm to all beings. Therefore, if one harms someone ordained in the Buddha's teaching, that evil is immeasurably, incalculably greater than the evil of the five deeds of immediate retribution described earlier.

"Therefore, because today, before me, you have confessed individually all your former obscurations of nonvirtuous action with utterly pure intention, with heightened embarrassment, and with effort, you are embraced by great compassion, and all your obscurations of evil action will gradually be purified. In this buddhafield, before every one of the thousand buddhas who will appear in the Fortunate Eon, you must likewise confess thoroughly and individually, in the presence of those tathāgatas, all your obscurations of evil action. Guard yourselves carefully against future wrongdoing as well. The last of the thousand buddhas of the Fortunate Eon is the Tathāgata called Rocana, the arhat, the perfectly complete buddha, perfect in knowledge and conduct, the Sugata, knower of the world, unsurpassed guide of beings to be tamed, teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One. In his presence too you must confess thoroughly and individually all your obscurations of evil action, and then they will be completely purified, without remainder."

At that time those śrāvakas and those bodhisattvas said, "So be it, Blessed One! If, at the time of Rocana, the last of the thousand buddhas, we will thus attain right view, completely abandon wrong views, purify all obscurations of evil action without remainder, and be liberated from all suffering, then even if we are born in the hell of Unceasing Torment during this Fortunate Eon and experience all kinds of suffering, we will endure it."

The Blessed One said, "Excellent, excellent! Through your courage, through the steadfastness of your strenuous diligence, and through the power of your aspiration, at the time of the Buddha Rocana you will without doubt purify all your former karmic obscurations, and with superior intention and with reverence you will without doubt serve him and make offerings to him. The Tathāgata Rocana will without doubt cause you to become arhats who have completely cut off all afflictions. He will without doubt cause you to attain the special qualities of the gateways of meditative absorption."

Then those śrāvakas and those bodhisattvas, rejoicing, bowed at the feet of the Blessed One and sat down again, each on his own seat.

At that time the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva great being Vajragarbha, "Son of noble family, when I examine the whole world with the eye of a buddha, I see that in the future, in this buddhafield, there will be countless, incalculable hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of beings: caṇḍāla kṣatriyas, caṇḍāla brahmins, caṇḍāla ministers, caṇḍāla householders, caṇḍāla merchant chiefs, caṇḍāla śramaṇas, as well as vaiśyas, śūdras, and caṇḍālas, men or women and so on. Though they will have obtained a human body by generating a small root of virtue, they will associate with nonvirtuous friends and generate wrong views, and through evil, nonvirtuous actions they will destroy my unsurpassed, most profound sacred Dharma. They will not spread my teaching; they will do away with what has spread, and they will not generate devotion. They will mock and disparage the sacred Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle that I have taught, creating obstacles and interruptions and not spreading it. At times they will mock and disparage the sacred Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle that I have taught, creating obstacles and interruptions and not spreading it. At times they will mock and disparage the sacred Dharma of the unsurpassed vehicle that I have taught, creating obstacles and interruptions and not spreading it. At times, toward those ordained in my teaching, whether vessels of the Dharma or not vessels of the Dharma, they will give rise to hatred, revile and abuse them, disparage them, look for chances to harm them with all kinds of words, mock and denigrate them, conceal their good qualities and proclaim their faults, and likewise, with a disparaging mind, do them injury and all kinds of harm.

"Such persons, who have not become vessels for the noble Dharma, are thoroughly foolish and deluded by nature. Proud and puffed up with conceit over the little they know, as soon as they pass from this life they will fall into the three lower realms and experience great, utterly unbearable suffering for immeasurable ages. For countless hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of eons it will be hard for them to obtain a human body, and so on, just as was explained in full above.

"Son of noble family, it would be easier for such a being to dwell in the great hell of Unceasing Torment and experience great suffering than to have such an evil body, gripped by pride, following evil friends and doing nonvirtuous deeds, and so wandering in saṃsāra, extremely hard to rescue, forever tormented by suffering in saṃsāra."

At that time, within that circle of the assembly, countless, incalculable beings of great intelligence rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, and, turning toward the Blessed One with palms joined and tears streaming, said to the Blessed One:

"Venerable Blessed One, even though these beings of the world have found a human body, so very hard to obtain, they completely abandon faith. They completely abandon right aspiration. They completely abandon right devotion and right view. They completely abandon spiritual friends. They completely abandon the right time, the right place, and pure moral discipline. They completely abandon right meditative absorption and right wisdom. Although these beings have obtained a human body, so very hard to obtain, through foolishness, ignorance, and pride they commit all the grave evils described above: they mock and disparage the sacred Dharma of the Blessed One, and they injure and harm the ordained attendants of the Blessed One's teaching.

Today, in the presence of the Blessed One, with one-pointed mind we make a true vow. From this day forward, from now on as we wander in saṃsāra, until we attain liberation, we vow never to meet such evil friends, never to commit such evil deeds, never to disparage the sacred Dharma of the Buddha, never to harm persons who have gone forth, never to put out the Dharma eye of sentient beings, and never to break the lineage of the Three Jewels. Blessed One, out of compassion for us, please accept these vows that we have made.

At that time, within that assembly, countless hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of intelligent beings rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, joined their palms, bowed, and said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable Blessed One, today in the presence of the Blessed One we wish to make a vow with resolute intention. From now on, as we wander in saṃsāra, until we attain forbearance regarding the Dharma, may we not become kings. May we not become ministers. May we not become governors of a land. May we not become lords of towns, cities, or villages. May we not become lords commanding armies. May we not become guild masters. May we not become lords with authority over offerings and monasteries. May we not become lords over guild masters, householders, and renunciants. May we not become masters or teachers. May we not take the body of a householder. May we not become those empowered to pass judgment. May we not become lords of wealth and power. If, without having attained forbearance regarding the Dharma, we were born in such a station, that very thing would be called becoming a cause of unwholesomeness for the Buddha's teaching. Because we would commit great and blameworthy evils, it would be called mocking and disparaging the sacred Dharma spoken by the Blessed One. It would be injuring and harming those who have gone forth in the Buddha's teaching. It would be putting out the Dharma eye of many sentient beings. It would be breaking the lineage of the Three Jewels. It would be doing harm and injury to countless sentient beings. Because of that we would without doubt fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment, and there would be no time of release from wandering in the lower realms. Blessed One, out of compassion for us, please bless these aspirations."

At that time all who had gathered in that assembly, the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, humans, nonhumans, and others, rose from their seats, bowed their heads at the feet of the Blessed One, shed tears, joined their palms, bowed, and said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable Blessed One, wandering in saṃsāra since beginningless time, through foolishness, ignorance, and pride we have generated every kind of unwholesome karma. Unwholesome actions of body, unwholesome actions of speech, and unwholesome actions of mind, whether we did them ourselves, had others do them, or rejoiced on seeing or hearing them done: all these evils, today in the presence of the Blessed One, with a mind of deep shame and without concealment, we confess one by one. May we guard ourselves fully so as never to do them again, and may the evils that would become obscurations in times to come be purified." In the same way they said it once, twice, and up to three times.

"From this day forward, until we reach the end of saṃsāra, may we never meet evil friends, and may we not fall under the power of unwholesomeness. Even if we do meet them, we wish to vow with resolute intention not to follow them. May we not commit any of those evil deeds. May we not experience suffering for a long time. Blessed One, out of compassion for us, please accept these aspirations."

Then the Blessed One declared to all those assembled: "Excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you, fearful of future suffering and filled with shame, have made confession one by one. It is excellent that you have made such aspirations in order to cross over the great river of saṃsāra and to enter the fearless city of nirvāṇa.

"Son of good family, when bodhisattva great beings possess these ten qualities, they attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path. What are the ten? Son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell on the inner body, do not dwell on the outer body, do not dwell on the outer and inner body, do not dwell on the past body, do not dwell on the future body, and do not dwell on the present body. Through this first quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell on inner feeling, do not dwell on outer feeling, do not dwell on feeling that is both outer and inner, do not dwell on past feeling, do not dwell on future feeling, and do not dwell on present feeling. Through this second quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell on inner perception, do not dwell on outer perception, do not dwell on perception that is both outer and inner, do not dwell on past perception, do not dwell on future perception, and do not dwell on present perception. Through this third quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell on inner formations, do not dwell on outer formations, do not dwell on formations that are both outer and inner, do not dwell on past formations, do not dwell on future formations, and do not dwell on present formations. Through this fourth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell on inner consciousness, do not dwell on outer consciousness, do not dwell on consciousness that is both outer and inner, do not dwell on past consciousness, do not dwell on future consciousness, and do not dwell on present consciousness. Through this fifth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell in this world. Through this sixth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell in this world. Through this seventh quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell in the desire realm. Through this eighth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell in the form realm. Through this ninth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path.

"Furthermore, son of good family, bodhisattva great beings do not dwell in the formless realm. Through this tenth quality, bodhisattva great beings attain forbearance regarding the Dharma of the true and faultless path."

When the Blessed One taught this Dharma discourse, within that assembly some seventy-two hundred thousand koṭis of bodhisattva great beings attained forbearance regarding the unborn nature of phenomena. Some eighty-four hundred thousand koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas attained the forbearance that accords with the Dharma. Some hundreds of thousands of countless śrāvakas attained everything from the complete severing of all afflictions up to arhatship. Some hundreds of thousands of niyutas of sentient beings generated the mind set on unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, which they had not generated before, and came to abide on the stage of irreversibility from the wisdom of the Tathāgata. Countless, immeasurable sentient beings generated the mind of a pratyekabuddha, which they had not generated before. And countless, immeasurable sentient beings generated the mind of a śrāvaka, which they had not generated before.

Then again the Blessed One declared to the great assembly: "A sentient being who has attained forbearance regarding the Dharma I permit to take up the position of a kṣatriya king consecrated on the crown of the head, to enjoy precious wealth of every kind, and to enjoy a position of great eminence; but no other sentient being."

The bodhisattva Vajragarbha again said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, do you then definitely not permit a sentient being who has not attained forbearance regarding the Dharma to occupy the position of a kṣatriya king consecrated on the crown of the head, to enjoy precious wealth of every kind, and to occupy a position of great eminence? Or is there also an occasion when you permit it?"

The Blessed One declared: "If a sentient being, even though he has not attained forbearance regarding the Dharma, practices the ten courses of wholesome action and also causes other sentient beings to train in them, then I permit him too to dwell in the position of a kṣatriya king consecrated on the crown of the head and to enjoy precious wealth of every kind and other positions of great eminence. But some sentient beings have not attained forbearance regarding the Dharma, do not practice the ten courses of wholesome action, and do not establish other sentient beings in training either, yet by great force and coercion they become kings consecrated on the crown of the head and enjoy precious wealth of every kind and positions of great eminence. These are called caṇḍāla kṣatriyas, caṇḍāla lords who enjoy other positions of great eminence as well. Foolish, deluded, and seized by pride, they destroy my most profound sacred Dharma. They cut off the lineage of the Three Jewels and leave the lamp of the sacred Dharma extinguished. They inflict many kinds of harm and injury on those who have gone forth in my teaching: they beat them, revile them, and punish them; they rob them of their robes, their alms bowls, and their means of livelihood; they strip them of their robes, reduce them to householders, and force them to do all sorts of labor; they bind them and imprison them, and even deprive them of life. They mock and disparage the sacred Dharma of my teaching, obstruct it, hinder it, and do not let it flourish, and they seize the property of stūpas and of the Saṅgha. Such a person will without doubt fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment, experience great suffering, and wander in the lower realms with no time of release."

Then the bodhisattva Vajragarbha asked further: "Blessed One, there are sentient beings who have not attained forbearance regarding the Dharma, do not practice the ten courses of wholesome action, and do not encourage other sentient beings to practice them either, yet by great force and coercion become kings in the position of one consecrated on the crown of the head, and dwell amid precious wealth of every kind and other positions of great eminence. Is there any other cause or any other means by which they may be brought out of the hell of Unceasing Torment and also freed from the suffering of the other lower realms?"

The Blessed One declared: "There is another cause and another means by which they can be brought out."

"Suppose a sentient being who has assumed kingship, consecrated on the crown of the head, and enjoys many kinds of wealth, has not attained forbearance regarding the Dharma and does not possess the ten courses of wholesome action either, yet possesses the power of faith. He serves and honors the Three Jewels. He does not mock or disparage, interrupt, or obstruct the sacred Dharma of the Buddha's teaching, which comprises the three vehicles, not even so much as a single verse. He does not harm, injure, revile, beat, or punish any of the ordained attendants of the Buddha's teaching, whether they keep moral discipline, have broken their moral discipline, or have no moral discipline at all, provided they have shaved their hair and beards and wear saffron robes. He does not rob them of their robes, their alms bowls, or their means of livelihood; he does not strip them of their robes, reduce them to householders, and put them to labor; he does not throw them into prison, nor go so far as to deprive them of life. He does not seize the property of stūpas or of the Saṅgha. He does not incite base and wicked people to harm and injure those who have gone forth, nor incite them to seize and make use of the property of the Three Jewels. With respect he listens to the sacred Dharma of the Buddha's teaching, which comprises the three vehicles, and retains it; and having listened and retained it, he strives with diligence and earnestness to follow the Dharma just as it has been taught. With respect he makes offerings to, honors, and serves the noble attendants of the three vehicles that I have taught. He makes a firm commitment to the Great Vehicle and gives rise to no doubt and no thought of turning back. And he leads other sentient beings as well to have faith in and trust the Great Vehicle, to retain it, and to apply themselves earnestly to training in it. If so, then this caṇḍāla kṣatriya, and other caṇḍālas of great eminence, were permitted even by the buddhas of the past to be consecrated to kingship and to enjoy many kinds of wealth. Even though they enjoy villages, cities, and all riches, they will not fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment, nor will they fall into any of the other lower realms. Such a one I too permit to be consecrated to kingship and to enjoy many kinds of wealth. Thus, even though such beings enjoy villages and cities, they will not fall into the hell of Unceasing Torment, nor will they fall into the other lower realms.

"Any sentient being who wishes to purify all the obscurations of evil karma, without exception, should earnestly train in this Dharma discourse that I have taught and retain it. Whoever, in this way, in the presence of the Saṅgha, confesses his evil deeds one by one with a mind of deep shame and remorse, will have all the evil deeds committed in former times purified completely, without exception."

The eighth fascicle.

At that time the bodhisattva great being Vajragarbha again said this to the Blessed One: "Venerable Blessed One, how is a bodhisattva great being free of error regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle? How is he free of error regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle? How is he free of error regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle? How is he free of error regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle? How is he free of error regarding the Great Vehicle? How is he free of error regarding persons of the Great Vehicle? How does he make the lineage of the Three Jewels flourish continually? How is he free of error regarding all who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not, even those who merely wear a scrap of robe and have shaved their hair and beards? How does he advance ever higher in the Dharma of the Great Vehicle and become irreversible? How do keen wisdom and special merit continually arise and increase in him? How does he gain mastery of the meditative absorptions, the dhāraṇīs, the forbearances, and the grounds, and become irreversible in them? How does he always meet spiritual friends and live in harmony with them? How is he never parted from seeing all the buddhas, never parted from bodhisattvas and from the śrāvaka attendants, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings to and honoring the Saṅgha? How, striving earnestly in all roots of virtue, is his mind never satiated? How is his mind never satiated with the many kinds of conduct of awakening?"

Then the Blessed One spoke these words to the bodhisattva great being Vajragarbha: "Son of good family, there are ten kinds of wheels of the bodhisattva great being. A bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten wheels will be free of error regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will be free of error regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will be free of error regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will be free of error regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will be free of error regarding the Great Vehicle. He will be free of error regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make the lineage of the Three Jewels flourish continually. He will be free of error regarding all who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not, even those who merely wear a scrap of robe and have shaved their hair and beards.
