He will advance ever higher in the Dharma of the Great Vehicle and be irreversible. Keen wisdom and special merit will continually arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery of all the meditative absorptions, the dhāraṇīs, the forbearances, and the grounds, and be irreversible in them. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and pratyekabuddha attendants, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings to and honoring the Saṅgha. Striving in all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satiated, and his mind will never be satiated with the conduct of awakening and with aspirations of many kinds. Whatever evil, unwholesome deeds he committed in the past will all be destroyed without remainder by the noble wisdom of the Dharma, hard and sharp like a vajra, so that he will not experience their fruit in ripening. He will commit no new evil, unwholesome deeds at all. With a mind free of weariness and discouragement he will turn the unsurpassed wheel of Dharma. His mind will be free of weariness and discouragement in applying himself to the seven precious limbs of awakening. And with a mind free of weariness and discouragement in subduing the diseases of affliction of all sentient beings, he will become the support and refuge of all sentient beings and sustain them.

"Son of good family, it is like this. Wherever a universal monarch possessed of the seven precious things goes, the precious wheel goes first, and the other precious things follow behind it. It travels throughout the four continents, clearing away all turbidity of body and mind of all sentient beings, and causing the happiness of body and mind of all sentient beings to arise and increase. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten wheels will attain everything from being free of error regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle up to becoming the support and refuge of all sentient beings and sustaining them.

"Son of good family, it is like this. A great chariot has four wheels; many people sit within it and travel along a great road. Whatever is on that road, whether clods of earth, potsherds, gravel, grass, wood, leaves, branches, flowers, or fruits, all of it passes under the wheels of that chariot and is crushed and broken, so that none of it is of any use. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses the ten kinds of wheels completely destroys all obscurations of affliction, obscurations of sentient beings, and obscurations of phenomena, so that he does not take on their ripening.

"Son of good family, it is like this. A sharp discus, hurled for an instant, cuts off the head and limbs of an opposing enemy and leaves him powerless to act. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels overcomes all the great suffering of wandering through the five destinies and completely cuts off all afflictions and evil deeds, so that he does not take on their ripening.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When the aeon of fire comes, five suns rise at once, and there is no moisture belonging to the water element, whether in the rivers, lakes, or streams of the four continents, that is not dried up. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels completely dries up the four currents, the obscurations of affliction, the obscurations of sentient beings, the obscurations of phenomena, and everything that ripens into the roots of suffering.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When the aeon of wind comes, great winds arise from the four directions at once, and every world system, with its mountains great and small and all its great lands, is destroyed and scattered. In the same way, when a bodhisattva great being possesses these ten kinds of wheels, there is nothing among the four worldly distortions and all the mountains of pride that is not shattered, and all the obscurations of affliction, obscurations of sentient beings, and obscurations of phenomena of all sentient beings, and all the roots of the painful ripening of suffering, are destroyed and scattered.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When the maned king of lions lets out his roar for a single instant, all wild beasts take flight, and all creatures are terrified and unable to move. In the same way, when a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels proclaims the sound of the Dharma for a single instant, all non-Buddhists (tīrthikas) and all evil friends who teach wrongly are seized with fear and terror, their words become confused, and they are unable to question or dispute.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When Śakra, lord of the gods, battles the asuras, and he enters the fray surrounded by the army of the gods and holding a vajra in his hand, all the asuras turn back in fear. In the same way, before a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels, all non-Buddhists of wrong view and all evil friends who teach wrongly flee in fear.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When a precious wish-fulfilling jewel is fastened to the top of a victory banner, precious things of every kind fall like rain, providing for all poor and destitute beings. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels and stands on the victory banner of pure moral discipline pours down the great rain of the Dharma, and so provides for all sentient beings without measure.

"Son of good family, it is like this. At nighttime, when the whole world has become dark and people are bewildered and have lost their way, the full moon rises, dispels all the darkness, and shows the way to those who have lost it. In the same way, when a bodhisattva great being possesses these ten kinds of wheels, he teaches the sacred Dharma to sentient beings who are blinded by the darkness of ignorance and have lost the noble eightfold path. He dispels all the darkness of ignorance, and because the light of the Dharma has dawned for them, the noble eightfold path becomes manifest, so that they cut off all defilements and bring suffering to an end.

"Son of good family, it is like this. When the great disk of the sun rises in the world, all crops grow and increase; all flowers, crops, and leaves flourish; all stench and filth are cleared away; the grain of the crops and the fruits of medicinal plants all ripen; and all the snow mountains melt, so that all the streams roar and gradually flow down into the great ocean. In the same way, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten kinds of wheels abides in superior generosity, and the qualities of disciplined moral conduct, gentle patience, diligence, meditative concentration, wisdom, skillful means, loving-kindness, compassion, and eloquence flourish fully in him, and he teaches the sacred Dharma to all sentient beings. By the splendor of the light of that Dharma, the roots of virtue of all sentient beings, the many kinds of superior crops, grow and increase; the sacred flowers and the many kinds of leaves of superior conduct flourish fully; all the bad deeds of affliction are completely cleared away; the fruits of the higher realms and of nirvāṇa fully ripen; all the mountains of wrong view and pride melt and flow down, so that the waters of true faith, moral discipline, learning, generosity, wisdom, and meditative absorption gradually flow down and fill the ocean of nirvāṇa; and all sentient beings, just as they wish, enter the fearless city of nirvāṇa.

"Son of good family, what then are the ten wheels of the bodhisattva great being? Son of good family, it should be understood that what are called the ten wheels are not to be ascribed to any other dharma: they are the ten courses of wholesome action themselves. It is because he possesses these ten kinds of wheels that one is called a bodhisattva great being. He is fully liberated from all evil, he perfects all wholesome qualities just as he wishes, he swiftly fills the ocean of great nirvāṇa, and by the light of the wisdom of great skill in means he brings all beings to full maturity, benefits them all, and leads them to attain happiness. Why is that? Son of good family, all the buddhas, the blessed ones of the past, also attained the fruit by completely abandoning the ten courses of unwholesome action and wholeheartedly extolling these ten courses of wholesome action. Therefore, whoever guards even some of these ten courses of wholesome action that have been taught, without lapse for as long as he lives, will attain every supreme and exceptional fruit, just as has been taught.

"Furthermore, son of good family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons killing for as long as he lives, that itself is the gift of fearlessness to all sentient beings. Because he does not cause sentient beings fear and anxiety, he does not produce for them the suffering of fear, and by virtue of this all his roots of virtue are swiftly brought to completion. From beginningless time, sunk in the ocean of wandering through the five destinies, by virtue of killing he has committed evil, unwholesome deeds with body, speech, and mind, has had others commit them, and has rejoiced in them on seeing and hearing of them, and he has thus been obscured by the obscurations of affliction, the obscurations of sentient beings, the obscurations of phenomena, and the obscurations of life. Now the wheel of completely abandoning killing destroys and crushes all of these without remainder, so that he does not experience their fruit in ripening.

"Even in this very body, gods, humans, and all others will be delighted with him and find him pleasing, no suspicion will arise toward him, he will be thoroughly happy in body and mind, and he will live long. And when the time of death and transmigration comes, his body and mind will not be tormented by suffering; surrounded by beloved children, wife, and all his household as he passes away, he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama. Moreover, he will meet beloved spiritual friends, tamed by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, true fields of merit; he will be filled with joy in body and mind, and with reverence for them, deep faith will arise in him. And after he passes from that life he will be reborn again among humans, with all his faculties complete and all his limbs intact. Wherever he is born he will be free of illness, long-lived, handsome, upright of mind, and thoroughly happy and content. Moreover, he will meet beloved spiritual friends, tamed by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, true fields of merit. Following their training, he will completely abandon killing, completely cut off all evil, unwholesome qualities, perfect all exceptional wholesome qualities, and seek out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle. Gradually devoting himself to all the aspirations and practices of the bodhisattvas, he will enter the vast ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"In his buddha field there will be no weapons, and the very words 'enemy' and 'harm' will not be heard. Sentient beings will be born in a field free of every fear, thoroughly happy and content, free of illness, and long-lived. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma in whatever way will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the first wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of taking life. If a bodhisattva great being possesses that wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard.

"In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and be irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The other results he attains are as has been explained in detail.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons taking what is not given for as long as he lives, that very abstention is the gift of fearlessness to all sentient beings. Because he dwells without tormenting anyone and without harming anyone, content with wealth he has himself obtained properly, and without hope for wealth or profit gained contrary to the Dharma, by the power of this all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of taking what is not given, they have themselves committed the unwholesome paths of body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They have been veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding jewels and wealth. Since the wheel of completely abandoning taking what is not given destroys all of these without remainder, he does not experience their ripened result.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him and find him pleasing, so no suspicion arises toward him. His body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and he comes to possess all wealth and jewels. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering. Surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and all his household, he will not see, at the time of death and passing, the most terrifying messengers of Yama. Moreover, he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, he will develop faith toward him, together with reverence.

"After passing from that life he is born once again among humans, with faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, he possesses great wealth, is fair of form, is upright of mind, and is thoroughly happy and at ease, and his wealth does not become something shared with the five dangers. Again he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, possessed of merit and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him, he will completely abandon taking what is not given, cut off completely all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfect all the special wholesome phenomena, seek out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devote himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he will enter the vast ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"Moreover, sentient beings will be born in his buddha field, a field adorned with jewels of many kinds, filled with jewel trees, jewel pools, jewel palaces, and the like, free of grasping at I and mine, and arrayed with every ornament. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma in whatever way will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the second wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of taking what is not given. If a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle.

He will make no mistake regarding the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and be irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The results he attains are also as has been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons sexual misconduct for as long as he lives, that very abstention is a gift to all sentient beings: freedom from the fear of being distracted by the winds of desire, freedom from jealousy, freedom from harm, freedom from torment, and freedom from strife. Because he is content with his own wife and has no longing for the wives of others, by the power of this all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of sexual misconduct, they have themselves committed unwholesome actions of body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They have been veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding wives. Since the wheel of completely abandoning sexual misconduct destroys all of these without remainder, he does not take on their ripened result.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him and find him pleasing, so no suspicion arises toward him. His body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and his wife will not be unfaithful. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering. He passes away surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and all his household, and he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama. Moreover, he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, and with reverence toward him, he will develop deep faith in him.

"After passing from that life he is born once again among humans, with faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, he has an excellent household, is fair of form, is upright of mind, and is thoroughly happy and at ease. Again he will meet a most pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him, he will completely abandon sexual misconduct, completely abandon all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfect all the special wholesome phenomena, seek out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devote himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he will enter the vast ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will have no women. Sentient beings free of attachment and lust, endowed with supreme celibate conduct, will be born in that field, and all of them will be born miraculously, not from an impure womb. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma to sentient beings in whatever way will tame them. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the third wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of sexual misconduct. If a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and be irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and be in harmony with them. He will never be parted from seeing the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The other results he attains are as has been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons lying for as long as he lives, he will always be honored by all sentient beings. The words he sets down will be taken as authoritative, everyone will listen to his words with respect, and no doubt about them will arise. By the power of this his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of lying, they have themselves committed evil and unwholesome actions with body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They become veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding authoritative speech. Since the wheel of completely abandoning lying destroys all of these without remainder, he will not take on their ripened result.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him and find him pleasing, no suspicion arises toward him, his body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and others trust and listen to everything he says. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering. He passes away surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and all his household, and he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama. Moreover, he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, and with reverence toward him, he will develop deep faith.

"After passing from that life he is born once again in a human body, with faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, his words carry authority, he is fair of form, upright of mind, and thoroughly happy and at ease. He meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him, he completely abandons lying, cuts off completely all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfects all the special wholesome phenomena, seeks out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devotes himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he will enter the vast ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"In his buddha field, everyone speaks the truth. Sentient beings free of deceit, adorned with divine speech, never misleading, upright in the workings of their minds, engaged solely in the pure white dharmas of right conduct, wearing fragrant divine garments, and arrayed with jewel ornaments, will be born in that field. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the Dharma in whatever way will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the fourth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of lying. If a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding bringing about the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and become irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always increase and arise in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking out all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The other results he attains are as has been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons divisive speech for as long as he lives, he will always be honored and held in high esteem by all sentient beings. Others will listen to everything he says, they will be reconciled, and no suspicion will arise. By the power of this his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of having engaged in divisive speech, they have themselves committed unwholesome actions with body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They become veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding reconciliation. Since the wheel of completely abandoning divisive words destroys all of these without remainder, he does not take on their ripened result.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him and find him pleasing, no suspicion arises toward him, his body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and people listen to everything he says and are reconciled. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering. He passes away surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and all his household, and he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama.

"He meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, and with reverence toward him, he will develop deep faith. After passing from that life he is born once again among humans, with faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, the words he speaks are gentle and reconciling, he is fair of form, upright of mind, and thoroughly happy and at ease. Again he meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him and entirely free of divisive words, he cuts off completely all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfects all the special wholesome phenomena, seeks out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devotes himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he will enter the ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be utterly stable, solid, and indestructible, and fully adorned with fine divine substances. Sentient beings who live in harmony, without dispute, thoroughly gentle and reconciled, and who seek wholesome dharmas will be born in that field. They will always listen to the sacred Dharma in concord and with respect. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma by whatever means will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the fifth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of divisive speech. If a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and be irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The other results he attains are as has been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, when a bodhisattva great being completely abandons all harsh words for as long as he lives, he will be honored and held in high esteem by all sentient beings. Whatever he says, others will all delight in it and listen, and they will have no suspicion. By the power of this all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of harsh words, they have themselves committed unwholesome actions of body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They have been veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding gentleness. Since the wheel of completely abandoning harsh words destroys all of these without remainder, by its power he does not take on their ripened result at all.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him, so no suspicion arises toward him. His body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and everything he says is pleasing and sweet to others. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering, and he is not afflicted. He passes away surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and all his household, and he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama. He meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, and with reverence toward him, he will develop deep faith.

"After passing from that life, when he is born once again among humans, he has faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, he speaks gentle words, is fair of form, upright of mind, and thoroughly happy and at ease. Again he meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him, he completely abandons harsh words, cuts off completely all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfects all the special wholesome phenomena, seeks out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devotes himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he enters the vast ocean of wisdom and goes on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be free of every unpleasant sound, and that field will be filled with pleasing, melodious sounds of many kinds and with the sound of the Dharma. Sentient beings endowed with mindfulness and intelligence, having the voice of Brahmā, and thoroughly gentle, will be born in that field, and they will always encourage one another with gentle words. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the Dharma in whatever way will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the sixth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of harsh words. Because a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the full flourishing of the lineage of the Three Jewels, nor regarding the disciples who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, whether they are vessels of the Dharma or not, down to the least of them, who merely wear a patch of monastic robe and have shaved off hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise higher and higher and be irreversible. Sharp wisdom and a special merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience, and grounds, and will be irreversible. He will always meet spiritual friends and come to follow them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never parted from seeing bodhisattvas and śrāvaka disciples, never parted from hearing the Dharma, and never parted from making offerings and paying homage to the Saṅgha. Diligent in seeking all roots of virtue, his mind will never be satisfied, and his mind will never have its fill of the conduct of awakening and of aspirations of every kind. The other results he attains are as has been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, because a bodhisattva great being completely abandons idle talk for as long as he lives, he will be honored and held in high esteem by all sentient beings. Everything he says will be meaningful, and those who hear his words will listen with respect and have no suspicion. By the power of this all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion.

"Beings have circled through the five destinies from beginningless time and sunk into the ocean of saṃsāra, and this too is because, through the power of idle talk, they have themselves committed unwholesome actions of body, speech, and mind, have urged others to commit them, and have rejoiced at what they saw and heard. They have been veiled by the obscurations that arise from this: the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration regarding sentient beings, the obscuration regarding the Dharma, and the obscuration regarding meaningful speech. Since the wheel of completely abandoning idle talk destroys all of these without remainder, he does not take on their ripened result.

"In this very life, gods, humans, and others all delight in him and find him pleasing, so there is no suspicion toward him. His body and mind are thoroughly at ease, and everything he says is meaningful. When the time of his death and passing comes, his body and mind are not tormented by suffering. He passes away surrounded by beloved sons, wife, and household, and he will not see the most terrifying messengers of Yama. He meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit, and with body and mind filled with joy, and with reverence toward him, he will develop deep faith.

"After passing from that life he is born once again among humans, with faculties complete and limbs whole. Wherever he is born, he speaks words that are beneficial and meaningful, is fair of form, upright of mind, and thoroughly happy and at ease. Again he meets a pleasing spiritual friend, one tamed by the Dharma, possessed of moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. Training under him, he completely abandons idle talk, cuts off completely all evil and unwholesome phenomena, perfects all the special wholesome phenomena, seeks out all the Dharma discourses of the Great Vehicle, and gradually devotes himself to all the aspirations and conduct of bodhisattvas, and he will enter the vast ocean of wisdom and go on until he attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be free of all meaningless words, and that field will be pervaded by many kinds of supreme and sublime sounds of the great Dharma belonging to the Bodhisattva Piṭaka. Sentient beings who, through immeasurable aspirations and perfect sublime wisdom, are skilled at explaining the meaning of the Dharma in its many aspects will be born in that field. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma in whatever way will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the seventh wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of idle talk. Because a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. The lineage of the Three Jewels will flourish fully, and he will make no mistake regarding those who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, the disciples, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not vessels for the Dharma, down to those who merely wear a scrap of Dharma robe and have shaved their hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise ever higher and higher and will not turn back. Sharp wisdom and an exceptional merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience and grounds, and will not turn back. He will always meet spiritual friends and follow them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never be parted from seeing the bodhisattvas and the disciples who are śrāvakas, never be parted from hearing the Dharma, and never be parted from making offerings to and serving the Saṅgha. His mind will never be sated in striving to seek out all roots of virtue, and his mind will never be sated with the conduct of awakening and with aspirations of every kind. The other results he will obtain are as has already been explained.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, if a bodhisattva great being completely abandons covetousness for as long as he lives, all sentient beings will honor him and regard him as venerable. His mind will be pure and free of turbidity, and by its power all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion. From beginningless time he has wandered in saṃsāra through the five destinies and sunk in the ocean of saṃsāra; under the power of covetousness he has himself committed evil, nonvirtuous actions of body, speech and mind, has urged others to commit them, and has rejoiced on seeing and hearing them done. From this arise the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration concerning sentient beings and the obscuration concerning phenomena, and he is obscured by an obscuration that prevents freedom from covetousness. Yet because the wheel of completely abandoning covetousness destroys all of this utterly, without remainder, he will not undergo its result in karmic maturation. Even in this very body, gods, humans and others will all delight in him and find him pleasing. He will be free of anxiety, his body and mind will be thoroughly at ease, and his mind will be pure and free of turbidity. When the time comes for him to die and pass on, he will not be tormented by suffering in body or mind. He will die and pass on surrounded by beloved children, wife and retinue, and he will not see the terrifying messengers of Yama. He will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. His body and mind will be filled with joy, and with reverence for that friend he will give rise to deep faith.

"When he has passed on from that life, he will be reborn again among humans, with his faculties complete and his limbs whole. Wherever he is born, his mind will be pure and without turbidity; he will be handsome in appearance, upright in mind, and supremely happy and content. Once again he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. He will train by following him, completely abandon covetousness, entirely cut off nonvirtuous qualities, bring all the exceptional virtuous qualities to completion, and seek out all the Dharma teachings of the Great Vehicle. He will gradually devote himself to all the aspirations and conduct of a bodhisattva and enter the ocean of vast wisdom, until he finally attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be level like the palm of a hand and filled with many kinds of jewels. It will be thick with jewel trees of every variety, and filled with jewel garments, jewel ornaments and adornments, jewel victory banners, flags and parasols, nets of gold, silver and pearls, trees and the like. Sentient beings will be born in that field who are most pleasing and beautiful to behold, who have entirely abandoned pride, who are exceedingly handsome in appearance, whose faculties are unimpaired, and whose minds are impartial, and it will be adorned with the perfect quality of freedom from covetousness. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma according to whatever will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the eighth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of covetousness. Because a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. The lineage of the Three Jewels will flourish fully, and he will make no mistake regarding those who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, the disciples, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not vessels for the Dharma, down to those who merely wear a scrap of Dharma robe and have shaved their hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise ever higher and higher and will not turn back. Sharp wisdom and an exceptional merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience and grounds, and will not turn back. He will always meet spiritual friends and be in harmony with them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never be parted from seeing the bodhisattvas and the disciples who are śrāvakas, never be parted from hearing the Dharma, and never be parted from making offerings to and serving the Saṅgha. He will never be sated in striving to seek out all roots of virtue, and his mind will never be sated with the conduct of awakening and with aspirations of every kind. The other results he will obtain are as has already been explained."

The ninth section.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, if a bodhisattva great being completely abandons malice for as long as he lives, all sentient beings will honor him and hold him in high esteem. His mind will be pure and free of stain, and by its power all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion. From beginningless time he has wandered in saṃsāra through the five destinies and sunk in the ocean of saṃsāra; under the power of malice he has himself committed evil, nonvirtuous actions of body, speech and mind, has urged others to commit them, and has rejoiced on seeing and hearing them done. From this arise the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration concerning sentient beings and the obscuration concerning phenomena, and he has been obscured by an obscuration consisting in acts of malice. Yet because the wheel of completely abandoning malice destroys all of this utterly, without remainder, he will not undergo its result in karmic maturation. Even in this very body, gods, humans and others will all delight in him and find him pleasing. He will not be anxious, and his mind will be pure and free of stain. When the time comes for him to die and pass on, he will not be tormented by suffering in body or mind. He will die and pass on surrounded by beloved children, wife and all his retinue, and he will not see the terrifying messengers of Yama. He will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. His body and mind will be filled with joy, and with reverence for that friend he will give rise to deep faith.

"When he has passed on from that life, he will be reborn again among humans, with his faculties complete and his limbs whole. Wherever he is born, his mind will be pure and free of stain; he will be handsome in appearance, upright in mind, and supremely happy and content. Once again he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. He will train in harmony with him, completely abandon malice, entirely cut off all evil and nonvirtuous qualities, bring all the exceptional virtuous qualities to completion, and seek out all the Dharma teachings of the Great Vehicle. He will gradually devote himself to all the aspirations and conduct of a bodhisattva and enter the ocean of vast wisdom, until he finally attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be free of all turbidity, blemishes, clouds, mist, dust, dirt, unpleasantness and unsightliness. It will be beautifully adorned with many jewels and delightful. Sentient beings will be born in that field who are free of pride, lovely and beautiful in form, whose faculties are unimpaired, and whose minds are always at peace, and it will be adorned with loving-kindness, compassion and perfect qualities. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the sacred Dharma according to whatever will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time and bring benefit and happiness to countless sentient beings.

"Son of noble family, this is the ninth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of malice. Because a bodhisattva great being possesses this wheel, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. The lineage of the Three Jewels will flourish fully, and he will make no mistake regarding those who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, the disciples, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not vessels for the Dharma, down to those who merely wear a scrap of Dharma robe and have shaved their hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise ever higher and higher and will not turn back. Sharp wisdom and an exceptional merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience and grounds, and will not turn back. He will always meet spiritual friends and train under them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never be parted from seeing the bodhisattvas and the disciples who are śrāvakas, never be parted from hearing the Dharma, and never be parted from making offerings to and serving the Saṅgha. His mind will never be sated in striving to seek out all roots of virtue, and his mind will never be sated with the conduct of awakening and with aspirations of every kind. The other results he will obtain are as has already been taught.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, if a bodhisattva great being completely abandons wrong view for as long as he lives, all sentient beings will honor him and hold him in high esteem. His mind will be pure and free of wrong conceptions, and by its power all his roots of virtue will be brought to completion. From beginningless time he has wandered in saṃsāra through the five destinies and sunk in the ocean of saṃsāra; under the power of wrong view he has himself committed evil, nonvirtuous actions of body, speech and mind, has urged others to commit them, and has rejoiced on seeing and hearing them done. From this arise the obscuration of the afflictions, the obscuration concerning sentient beings and the obscuration concerning phenomena, and he has been obscured by an obscuration to right view. Yet because the wheel of completely abandoning wrong view destroys all of this without remainder, he will not undergo its result in karmic maturation. Even in this very body, gods, humans and others will all delight in him and find him pleasing. He will be free of anxiety, his body and mind will be thoroughly at ease, and his mind will be pure and free of wrong view. When the time comes for him to die and pass on, he will not be tormented by suffering in body or mind. He will die and pass on surrounded by beloved children, wife and all his retinue, and he will not see the terrifying messengers of Yama. He will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. His body and mind will be filled with joy, and with reverence he will give rise to deep faith.

"When he has passed on from that life, he will be reborn again among humans, with his faculties complete and his limbs whole. Wherever he is born, his mind will be pure and free of wrong view; he will be handsome in appearance, upright in mind, and supremely happy and content. Once again he will meet a pleasing spiritual friend, one disciplined by the Dharma, endowed with moral discipline and good qualities, a true field of merit. He will train in harmony with him, completely abandon the doctrines of wrong view, entirely cut off all evil and nonvirtuous qualities, bring all the exceptional virtuous qualities to completion, and seek out all the Dharma teachings of the Great Vehicle. He will gradually devote himself to all the aspirations and conduct of a bodhisattva and enter the ocean of vast wisdom, until he finally attains unsurpassed awakening.

"His buddha field will be free of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles, free of all the retinues of Māra of the gods, and free of all the retinues of the non-Buddhists. It will be adorned with jewels of every kind and exceedingly beautiful. Sentient beings will be born in that field who possess right view and have completely abandoned all belief in marvels and auspicious omens, all the various corrupt views, the eternalist view, the nihilist view, and every view of a self and of what belongs to a self. They will be very long-lived and will enjoy the single taste of the Great Vehicle. The lifespan of that tathāgata will be immeasurable, and he will teach the Dharma according to whatever will tame sentient beings. Even after his complete nirvāṇa, the sacred Dharma will remain for a long time, and for the benefit and happiness of countless sentient beings, the teaching of the Dharma of a single taste will spread fully without dispute, and there will be no obstructing spirits and no hindrances.

"Son of noble family, this is the tenth wheel of the bodhisattva great being: the complete abandonment of wrong view. Because a bodhisattva great being possesses these ten wheels, he will make no mistake regarding the Śrāvaka Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. He will make no mistake regarding the Great Vehicle, and he will make no mistake regarding persons of the Great Vehicle. The lineage of the Three Jewels will flourish fully, and he will make no mistake regarding those who have gone forth in the teaching of the Tathāgata, the disciples, whether they are vessels for the Dharma or not vessels for the Dharma, down to those who merely wear a scrap of Dharma robe and have shaved their hair and beard. In the Dharma of the Great Vehicle he will rise ever higher and higher and will not turn back. Sharp wisdom and an exceptional merit will always arise and increase in him. He will gain mastery over all meditative absorptions, dhāraṇīs, forms of patience and grounds, and will not turn back. He will always meet spiritual friends and train under them. He will never be parted from seeing all the buddhas, never be parted from seeing the bodhisattvas and the disciples who are śrāvakas, never be parted from hearing the Dharma, and never be parted from making offerings to and serving the Saṅgha. His mind will never be sated in striving to seek out all roots of virtue, and his mind will never be sated with the conduct of awakening, with aspirations, and with the six perfections. The other results he will obtain are as has already been explained.

"Son of noble family, a bodhisattva great being who possesses these ten wheels will swiftly attain manifest, complete awakening in unsurpassed, perfect awakening. Why is that? Because all the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas of the past likewise completely abandoned the ten nonvirtuous actions, and all of them praised and extolled the result of the path of the ten virtuous actions. For the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings, to make the path of awakening arise and grow, to remove without remainder the suffering of karma and afflictions of all sentient beings, to dry up the three lower realms, to sustain the lineage of the Three Jewels, to cut off existence in the three realms entirely, to cut off the aggregates, the elements and the sense sources, and to lead all beings swiftly into the city of fearless nirvāṇa, they completely abandoned the results of the ten nonvirtuous actions, just as has been explained.

"Therefore, son of noble family, suppose someone has not accomplished this path of the ten virtuous actions as it truly is, or at the very least does not guard even a few of the paths of the ten virtuous actions as they truly are, and yet says, 'I will attain manifest awakening,' or 'I truly practice the Great Vehicle,' or 'I am accomplishing unsurpassed, perfect awakening.' That person is a great liar. In the presence of the buddhas, the blessed ones of the ten directions, he deceives the world. Without scruple and without shame, he talks of emptiness while holding a nihilist view. He is a fool, ignorant, and a deceiver. Know that when he passes on from that life he will fall into the lower realms.

"Son of noble family, if merely saying the words, or merely hearing them, were enough, and awakening and nirvāṇa could be attained without needing to cultivate the path of the ten virtuous actions or to apply oneself diligently, then within a single eon, in a single instant, all sentient beings, as numerous as the atoms of the earth in all the buddha fields of the ten directions, would attain manifest awakening and pass into nirvāṇa. But no such thing is possible. Why is that? Because the path of the ten virtuous actions is the root of the Great Vehicle. It is the cause of awakening, and it is like a staircase to nirvāṇa.

"Son of noble family, if merely generating the resolve or merely making aspirations were enough, and awakening and complete nirvāṇa could be attained without needing to apply oneself diligently to becoming habituated to the path of the ten virtuous actions, then within a single eon, in a single instant, sentient beings as numerous as the atoms of the earth in all the buddha fields of the ten directions would attain manifest awakening in perfect awakening and pass into nirvāṇa. But no such thing is possible. Why is that? Because the path of the ten virtuous actions is the cause of the distinctive results and qualities, both worldly and beyond the world.

"Son of noble family, if one does not apply oneself diligently to the path of the ten virtuous actions, then even if for eons as numerous as the atoms in all the buddha fields of the ten directions one says, 'We are followers of the Great Vehicle,' and teaches, or listens, or generates the resolve, or makes aspirations, one will not attain awakening and nirvāṇa, nor will one be able to bring others out of the suffering of saṃsāra.

"Son of noble family, it is through diligence in the path of the ten virtuous actions that there come to be in the world the great families of kṣatriyas, brahmins and the like, and all states from the gods of the Four Great Kings up to the summit, the sphere of neither perception nor non-perception; and likewise the Śrāvaka Vehicle, the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and everything up to unsurpassed, perfect awakening. All of these come about through the distinctions and differences in how intensely one applies oneself to the path of the ten virtuous actions.

"Therefore, son of noble family, whoever wishes to attain unsurpassed, perfect awakening swiftly should be adorned with the ornament of diligence in the path of the ten virtuous actions; if he does not remain committed to the ten nonvirtues, he will attain manifest awakening in unsurpassed, perfect awakening. Whoever wishes swiftly to enter the domain of the Great Vehicle's practice, or wishes swiftly to attain unsurpassed, perfect awakening, or wishes swiftly to bring all virtuous qualities to completion, should first guard the path of the ten virtuous actions with great care. Why is that? Because the path of the ten virtuous actions is the cause that brings about all virtuous qualities, the ground on which good qualities are established, and the attainment of the supreme results, both worldly and beyond the world. Therefore, apply yourself with great diligence to the path of the ten virtuous actions."

Then, to set out these points clearly once again, the Blessed One spoke in verse:

"Those who wish to cut off all the suffering of existence
And to attain great awakening
Should not be lazy in their diligence in the wheels of the ten virtues,
But should exert themselves in it.

Toward the sacred Dharma of the three vehicles,
Toward persons of the three vehicles,
And toward all who have gone forth,
They will come to be free of mistakes.

With faith and devotion for the practice of the Great Vehicle,
And realizing the supreme Dharma
That brings relief to the whole assembly,
They swiftly attain great awakening in that pure field.

If one completely abandons killing,
All will honor one and hold one in high esteem;
One will always be free of illness and long-lived,
Happy and content, and free of harm.

In every place where one is born,
One will be devoted to the path that is the buddhas' domain,
One will see the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha,
And swiftly attain unsurpassed awakening.

If one completely abandons taking what is not given,
The wise will honor one and hold one in high esteem;
Every path by which desire arises will be stilled,
And freedom from desire will arise.

In all one's births one will be endowed with wealth
And will become a great patron,
And one will also attain a lovely buddha field
Adorned with many jewels.

If one completely abandons sexual misconduct,
The stains of the afflictions will be stilled,
The ocean of existence that is desire will dry up,
And one will swiftly attain a pure buddha field.

Many kinds of sentient beings, too,
One will draw out of the mire of desire;
Having firmly established them in the Great Vehicle,
One will set them to practice pure conduct.

If one completely abandons speaking falsehood,
One attains the faculties and wisdom of the noble ones;
One always delights in speaking the truth
And crushes the suffering that comes from falsehood.

Because one's single word is held as reliable,
One sees the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha;
By cultivating the refusal to speak falsehood,
One swiftly attains great awakening.

If one completely abandons divisive speech,
One becomes a vessel for virtuous qualities;
One sees the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha,
And never falls into the nihilist view.

One attains the noble ones' joy free of attachment
And the dhāraṇī of the treasury of jewels;
One understands the ocean of the profound Dharma
And swiftly attains unsurpassed awakening.

If one abandons cutting words
And speaks gentle and tender words,
All sentient beings will honor one,
And even one's former nonvirtuous karma will be stilled.

One's assembly will always rejoice,
And one will become a teacher of bodhisattvas;
One will know the domain of many buddhas
And will even pass beyond the ten grounds.

If one completely abandons idle talk,
The wise will honor one and hold one in high esteem;
All the words one speaks to others
Will come to have five qualities.

Always listening to the words of the noble ones,
One will delight in seeking the noble path;
Fulfilling the ocean of the buddhas' qualities,
One will swiftly attain omniscience.

If one completely abandons covetousness,
Does not disparage the noble ones,
And honors those who wear the saffron robe,
One accomplishes the awakening of the three vehicles.

One is always born in pure fields
And comes to abide in the station of a teacher;
Mounted on the unsurpassed vehicle,
One swiftly attains the wisdom of the Victor.

If one completely abandons malice
And single-mindedly cultivates a mind of loving-kindness,
One will swiftly attain meditative equipoise
And will be devoted to the domain of the noble ones.

One will be born in a pure buddha field,
Free from every fault and defect;
Abiding there, one attains awakening.
So all malice should be abandoned.

If one completely abandons wrong view
And cultivates a mind of pure faith,
One will be devoted to the teaching of the three vehicles
And will make offerings to all the buddhas.

Completely freed from the three lower realms,
One will meet the noble Saṅgha,
Will possess the qualities of a bodhisattva,
And will attain supreme wisdom as well.

It is said that through the paths of the ten virtuous actions
Supreme awakening is attained,
And the meditative attainments, the dhāraṇīs,
The grounds and the forms of patience arise.

Through this wheel of great nature
All the lower realms are utterly conquered,
All the obscurations of evil are destroyed,
And great awakening is swiftly attained."

This concludes the sixth chapter, called "The Path of the Ten Virtuous Actions," from the noble great extensive sūtra called The Ten Wheels of Kṣitigarbha.

"Furthermore, son of noble family, the bodhisattva great being has ten wheels, the great armor of the giving of material things. A bodhisattva great being who possesses those wheels has, from his very first generation of the resolve, cleared away the five sense pleasures. He far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, protected and served by all followers of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles.

"What are the ten? They are as follows. He gives food of every kind, drink, clothing, jewel ornaments, oxen, horses, chariots, the limbs of his body, his feet, hands, ears, nose, head, eyes, marrow, brain, skin, bones, flesh and blood, his land, his kingdom, his children, his wife, his male and female servants, his house, his fields and pastures, and so on. When he makes each of these gifts, he has no regard for his body or his life. He does not generate the resolve and give out of a wish for the worldly or supramundane happiness of himself alone. Rather, he generates the resolve to give gifts in order to make the sprout of great loving-kindness and great compassion grow toward all sentient beings. He generates the resolve to give gifts in order to develop skill in means and supreme wisdom. He generates the resolve to give gifts out of great loving-kindness toward all sentient beings, for the sake of their benefit and happiness. He generates the resolve to give gifts in order to remove the suffering of all sentient beings. He gives gifts with a mind free of the thought of being above others, a mind free of harshness, a mind free of jealousy, and a mind free of miserliness and reluctance. Whatever the thing given may be, large or small, even if he gives no more than a single handful of food, he generates the resolve to give gifts without hoping for a result in karmic maturation. He generates the resolve to give gifts without hoping for the result of the Śrāvaka Vehicle. He generates the resolve to give gifts without hoping for the result of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle. Whatever the thing given may be, large or small, even if he gives no more than a single handful of food, he generates the resolve to give gifts while dedicating it to the wisdom of omniscience.

"Son of noble family, if a bodhisattva great being possesses these ten wheels, the great armor of the giving of material things, then because he has cleared away the five sense pleasures at his first generation of the resolve, he far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, served and fully protected by all followers of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles. Why is that? When śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas generate the resolve to give gifts, they have no mind of loving-kindness and great compassion; they give gifts in order to be rid of their own poverty, to free themselves from suffering, to gain happiness for themselves, and to bring themselves to nirvāṇa. They do not give for the sake of all sentient beings. When a bodhisattva great being gives gifts, he gives with a mind of loving-kindness and great compassion, in order to remove the poverty and want of all sentient beings, to bring all sentient beings out of suffering, to bring all sentient beings to happiness, and to lead all sentient beings to nirvāṇa. He does not practice giving for his own sake alone. For this reason he far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, served and fully protected by all followers of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles.

"When a bodhisattva great being practices the perfection of the giving of material things, his mind is not attached to the finest of the five sense pleasures. He gives to all sentient beings every comfort that is his own to give. He gives while abiding in a mind that gathers in all sentient beings, a mind of patience that pays no heed to any suffering of his own, a mind intent on stilling the suffering of all sentient beings, a mind that gives happiness to all sentient beings, and a mind intent on leading all sentient beings to nirvāṇa. Therefore he far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, served and protected by all followers of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles.

"Son of noble family, if one's mind is not attached to the five sense pleasures and one gives with loving-kindness and great compassion, one is called a bodhisattva great being, and one is called a true field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas. But if one gives without having abandoned the five worldly sense pleasures and without loving-kindness and great compassion, then even if one gives away immeasurable, countless, incalculable things, one is not called a bodhisattva great being, nor is one a true field of merit for śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, for such giving does not bear the seal of the noble ones. Therefore one should give having abandoned the five worldly sense pleasures and possessing loving-kindness and great compassion. If one gives without having abandoned the five worldly sense pleasures and without loving-kindness and great compassion, one is not even called a bodhisattva, nor is one a true field of merit.

Son of good family, if one gives while attached to the five kinds of sense pleasure, not even a small part of the suffering of one's own body will be allayed, so what need is there to speak of removing the immeasurable suffering of all sentient beings?

Then, in order to restate these points clearly, the Blessed One spoke in verse:

The wise who possess the wheel of material giving,
With the conviction of a pure intention,
Abandon the five kinds of sense pleasure
And bring benefit and happiness to all sentient beings.

It is for the happiness of all sentient beings,
Not in hope of a ripened fruit.
Though the gift they give be small,
They become a true field of merit.

Though one makes many gifts,
If one dwells in the five kinds of sense pleasure,
One is not sealed with the seal of the noble ones,
And one remains within the indeterminate group.

Though the gift one gives be small,
One who does not dwell in the five sense pleasures
Becomes a true field of merit
For śrāvakas and solitary victors.

Therefore, abandoning the five kinds of sense pleasure,
Always giving with complete purity,
And bringing happiness to all sentient beings,
One becomes a true field of merit.

Furthermore, son of good family, the bodhisattva great being has a tenfold wheel of the great armor of the gift of the Dharma. When a bodhisattva great being possesses that wheel, then from the first generation of the mind of awakening, because the five kinds of sense pleasure have been entirely cleared away, he swiftly attains the meditative absorption called Light of the Sun Lamp. He far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, protected and served by all who follow the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

What are the ten? They are these: the true Dharma taught by the tathāgatas, namely the true Dharma connected with the Śrāvaka Vehicle, the true Dharma connected with the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, the true Dharma connected with the Great Vehicle, worldly dharmas, world-transcending dharmas, dharmas with defilements, dharmas in which the defilements are exhausted, conditioned dharmas, unconditioned dharmas, and nondual dharmas. The bodhisattva great being has faith in and respect for these ten kinds of Dharma, and retains as much as he is able of what he has heard and keeps it in mind. He examines it thoroughly. In the end he comes to understand it, and he teaches it to others as is fitting.

Whenever he teaches the Dharma, he should do so with a mind free of jealousy, a mind free of stinginess, a mind free of pride, a mind that does not hope for gain, a mind that does not disparage others, a mind that does not praise itself, a mind full of reverence, a mind of benefit, a mind of great loving-kindness, and a mind of great compassion.

To a person of the Śrāvaka Vehicle he teaches and explains the Dharma of the śrāvakas, and not the Dharma of the pratyekabuddhas or the Dharma of the Great Vehicle. To a person of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle he teaches the Dharma of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, and not the Dharma of the Śrāvaka Vehicle or the Dharma of the Great Vehicle. To a person of the Great Vehicle he teaches the Dharma of the Great Vehicle, and not the Dharma of those of the Śrāvaka and Pratyekabuddha Vehicles. He teaches the true Dharma in accordance with the faculties and the capacity of sentient beings, and does not teach the Dharma to those whose faculties are not suited and who are not vessels for it.

He does not urge beings of the Great Vehicle to practice the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle or to practice the Śrāvaka Vehicle. There are occasions when he urges beings of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle, saying, "Devote yourselves to the practice of the Great Vehicle," and there are also occasions when he urges beings of the Śrāvaka Vehicle, saying, "Devote yourselves to the practice of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle and to the practice of the Great Vehicle."

Toward even the smallest portion of the true Dharma of the Tathāgata's teaching, even a single verse or just half a verse, he has deep faith and shows reverence, and he does not deride it, denigrate it, obstruct it or hinder it. He regards the teacher of the Dharma as like the Buddha, regards the assembly listening to the Dharma as like the sick, and regards the Dharma being explained as like excellent medicine; and having entirely cut off the five kinds of sense pleasure, he teaches the true Dharma without expectation.

Son of good family, these are called the tenfold wheel of the great armor of the gift of the Dharma of the bodhisattva great being. The bodhisattva great being who possesses that wheel clears away the five sense pleasures entirely, swiftly attains the meditative absorption called Light of the Sun Lamp, far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, protected and served by all who follow the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

Then, in order to restate these points clearly, the Blessed One spoke in verse:

The wise who practice the giving of the Dharma
Teach the Dharma of the three vehicles according to the vessel.
They do not teach them the other vehicles,
So that, having heard, they do not come to deride and denigrate them.

They teach the true Dharma according to faculties and vessel,
And not to those whose faculties are not vessels.
Just as each one inclines toward whatever it may be,
They teach that very Dharma and so bring delight.

To a person who practices the Great Vehicle,
They do not teach the Dharma of the two other vehicles.
To persons of the two vehicles below,
They teach the Dharma of the middle and the highest vehicle.

With deep faith and respect one should listen to the Dharma,
And should not deride or denigrate it.

Those who make offerings to one who speaks the Dharma
Should regard him as like the Buddha.
To clear away the sickness of the afflictions,
They urge others to listen to the medicine of the true Dharma.
Keeping profit and fame far away,
One should teach and explain the true Dharma.

Furthermore, son of good family, the bodhisattva great being has a wheel of the great armor of pure moral discipline. The bodhisattva great being who possesses that wheel, from the first generation of the mind of awakening, entirely clears away the five sense pleasures, far surpasses all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, becomes a great field of merit for all śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, and is honored, protected and served by all who follow the Śrāvaka Vehicle and the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle.

What, then, is the wheel of the great armor of pure moral discipline? Son of good family, the pure moral discipline of a bodhisattva is of two kinds: the common and the uncommon.

What, then, is the common wheel of a bodhisattva's pure moral discipline? It is when someone living in the household takes the vows of moral discipline of a layman or a laywoman, or else, having gone forth, receives full ordination through the moral discipline of the prātimokṣa.
